She was girlish, and at the same time filled one with an impression of great reserve strength of mind and character. She was very brown, showing exposure to the sun, yet her skin was clear﻿—almost translucent. Her garb was similar to mine﻿—the common garmenture of people of our class, both men and women. She wore the tunic and breeches and boots just as Mother and Mollie and the rest of us did; but somehow there was a difference﻿—I had never before realized what a really beautiful costume it was.

===Chinese===

忽然间我一点也不觉得勇敢了，只觉得虚弱、傻气：我只会捻着刀，眼睛看地。是父亲开了口，这打岔解了我的窘。
“你是谁？”他问，“从哪里来？深夜里一个年轻女子独自乱走，已经稀奇；更稀奇的是听见有人敢称那被禁的神明。”
到那时我才发觉她用了祂的名号；一经想起，我不由得惊惶地四下张望，看还有没有旁人听见。父亲和吉姆我知道不要紧：两家之间有一条共同的纽带，就是我们每月一次的秘密宗教仪式。自我父亲出生之前那可怕的一天——那一天谁也不敢高声提起，各教派的教士奉二十四人团（The Twentyfour）之命被杀得一个不剩——从那天起，以任何形式敬拜上帝都是死罪。
我们把姑娘领进屋；母亲一见她这样年轻貌美，把她搂进怀里，孩子崩溃了，啜泣着偎住母亲，半天两人都说不出话。烛光里我看清了这个陌生姑娘美得惊人。我说过我母亲是我见过最美的妇人，这是实情；可这个突然来到我们中间的姑娘，是我见过最美的少女。她约莫十九岁，身段纤秀而不纤弱。她的一举一动、脸上的神情、手势和谈吐，处处透着气力与生气。她带着少女气，同时又给人一个心性坚毅、内力深厚的印象。她肤色很深，是晒的，皮肤却明净——几乎是半透明的。她的衣着同我相仿——我们这个阶级男女通用的平常衣装；她穿外衣、短裤和靴子，同母亲、莫莉和我们大家一样；可不知怎的就是不一样——我从前竟没发现这身衣裳原来这样美。

===Original===

The band about her forehead was wider than was generally worn and upon it were sewn numerous tiny shells, set close together and forming a pattern﻿—it was her only attempt at ornamentation; but even so it was quite noticeable in a world where women strove to make themselves plain rather than beautiful﻿—some going even so far as to permanently disfigure their faces and those of their female offspring, while others, many, many others, killed the latter in infancy. Mollie had done so with two. No wonder that grownups never laughed and seldom smiled!
When the girl had quieted her sobs on Mother’s breast, Father renewed his questioning; but Mother said to wait until morning, that the girl was tired and unstrung and needed sleep, and then came the question of where she was to sleep. Father said that he would sleep in the living room with me and that the stranger could sleep with Mother; but Jim suggested that she come home with him as he and Mollie had three rooms, as did we, and no one to occupy his living room; and so it was arranged, although I would rather have had her remain with us.
At first she rather shrank from going, until Mother told her that Jim and Mollie were good, kindhearted people and that she would be as safe with them as with her own father and mother. At mention of her parents the tears came to her eyes and she turned impulsively toward my mother and kissed her, after which she told Jim that she was ready to accompany him.
She started to say goodbye to me and to thank me again; but, having found my tongue at last, I told her that I would go with them as far as Jim’s house. This appeared to please her and so we set forth. Jim walked ahead and I followed with the girl, and on the way I discovered a very strange thing. Father had shown me a piece of iron once that pulled smaller bits of iron to it. He said that it was a magnet. This slender, stranger girl was certainly no piece of iron, nor was I a smaller bit of anything; but nevertheless, I could not keep away from her. I cannot explain it﻿—however wide the way was, I was always drawn over close to her, so that our arms touched and once our hands swung together and the strangest and most delicious thrill ran through me that I had ever experienced.

===Chinese===

她额上的束带比常人戴的宽些，上面密密缝着一排小小的贝壳，排出花样——这是她仅有的妆点；可即便这样，在这个女人们争相把自己弄得丑陋而非美丽的世界里，也已十分惹眼了——有的甚至把她们自己的脸、连同女儿的脸永久毁掉，还有的——很多很多——把女婴干脆弄死。莫莉就弄死过两个。难怪大人们从不笑出声，连微笑都少！
姑娘在母亲怀里哭止了，父亲重新问她的来历；可母亲说等明天再问，姑娘累了，神经松了，需要睡觉；接着就是她睡哪里的问题。父亲说他同我睡外间，让客人同母亲睡；可吉姆提议让她去他家——他家同我们家一样有三间屋，外间空着没住人；就这样定了，虽然我更愿意她留在我们家。
起初她有点怯于去；直到母亲告诉她，吉姆和莫莉是善良厚道的人家，在她那里同在自己父母身边一样安全。一提她的父母，她眼里就涌上泪，冲动地转身吻了母亲，随后对吉姆说，她愿意跟去。
她要同我道别、再谢一遍；可我总算找回了舌头，对她说我送他们到吉姆家。这话像是叫她欢喜，我们便上路了。吉姆走在前头，我同姑娘跟着；一路我发现一件顶奇怪的事。父亲从前给我看过一块铁，能把小铁屑吸过去；他说那叫磁石。这位苗条的陌生姑娘自然不是什么铁块，我也不是什么铁屑；可我偏偏离不开她。我说不清——路再宽，我也总被拖到她身边去，臂膀挨着臂膀；有一回两手荡到一处，一阵我从未经历过的、最奇妙的震颤流遍我全身。

===Original===

I used to think that Jim’s house was a long way from ours﻿—when I had to carry things over there as a boy; but that night it was far too close﻿—just a step or two and we were there.
Mollie heard us coming and was at the door, full of questionings, and when she saw the girl and heard a part of our story she reached out and took the girl to her bosom, just as Mother had. Before they took her in, the stranger turned and held out her hand to me.
“Good night,” she said, “and thank you again, and once more, may God, our Father, bless and preserve you.”
And I heard Mollie murmur: “The Saints be praised!” And then I turned homeward, treading on air.

===Chinese===

从前我总觉得吉姆家离我们家很远——小时候往那边扛东西，觉得远；可那天夜里，它近得可恶——只一两步，就到了。
莫莉听见我们来，早候在门口，满肚子疑问；一见姑娘、听了我们的部分来历，她也像母亲那样伸出双臂，把姑娘搂进怀里。进门之前，陌生姑娘转身向我伸出手来。
“晚安，”她说，“再谢您一遍；还有，愿我们的天父上帝，保佑您、护佑您。”
我听见莫莉喃了一声：“感谢诸圣！”随后我转身回家，一路踩着云。

## III Brother General Or-Tis / 三 奥提斯弟兄将军
===Original===

The next day I set out as usual to peddle goat’s milk. We were permitted to trade in perishable things on other than market days, though we had to make a strict accounting for all such bartering. I usually left Mollie until the last as Jim had a deep, cold well on his place where I liked to quench my thirst after my morning trip; but today Mollie got her milk fresh and first and early﻿—about half an hour earlier than I was wont to start out.
When I knocked and she had bade me enter and saw who it was, she looked surprised at first, for just an instant, and then a strange expression came into her eyes﻿—half amusement, half pity﻿—and she rose and went into the kitchen for the milk jar. I saw her wipe the corners of her eyes with the back of one finger; but I did not understand why﻿—not then.
The stranger girl had been in the kitchen helping Mollie, and the latter must have told her I was there, for she came right in and greeted me. It was the first good look I had had of her, for candle light is not brilliant at best. If I had been enthralled the evening before, there is no word in my limited vocabulary to express the effect she had on me by daylight. She﻿—but it is useless, I cannot describe her!
It took Mollie a long time to find the milk jar, bless her, though it seemed short enough to me, and while she was finding it the stranger girl and I were getting acquainted. First she asked after Father and Mother and then she asked our names. When I told her mine she repeated it several times to herself in a low voice. “Julian 9th,” she said, “Julian 9th!” and then she smiled up at me. “It is a nice name, I like it.”
“And what is your name?” I asked.
“Juana,” she said﻿—she pronounced it Whanna; “Juana St. John.”
“I am glad,” I said, “that you like my name; but I like yours better.” It was a very foolish speech, and it made me feel silly; but she did not seem to think it foolish, or if she did, she was too nice to let me know it. I have known many girls; but mostly they were homely and stupid. The pretty girls were seldom allowed in the market place﻿—that is, the pretty girls of our class. The Kalkars permitted their girls to go abroad for they did not care who got them, as long as someone got them; but American fathers and mothers would rather slay their girls than send them to the market place, and the former often was done.

===Chinese===

第二天我照常出门叫卖羊奶。非集市的日子，易坏的吃食是准许交易的，只是每一笔都得严报。我往常总把莫莉家留在末尾，因为吉姆家有一口深而凉的老井，早上跑完路我爱去那儿解渴；可今天莫莉家的奶是头一份、最新鲜的——我比平素早出门约莫半个钟头。
我一敲门，她叫我进去，看清是我，起先怔了一瞬，随即眼里浮起一种奇怪的神色——半是好玩，半是怜悯——起身进厨房取奶罐去了。我看见她用一根指背抹了抹眼角；可当时我不懂为什么——当时不懂。
陌生姑娘方才在厨房帮莫莉的忙，莫莉想必告诉了她我来了，因为她径直进来迎我。这是我头一回好好看她：烛光再好也好不到哪里去。倘若昨晚我已被她迷住，那么天光底下她给我的震动，我这点贫乏的词汇里就找不出字眼来描了。她——算了，没用，我描不出她！
莫莉找那只奶罐找了老半天，愿上帝保佑她，我却只嫌它短；就在她找的当儿，我同陌生姑娘彼此熟络起来。她先问了父亲母亲好，又问我们的名字。我说了我的；她低声自言自语念了好几遍。“朱利安九世，”她说，“朱利安九世！”随后仰起脸朝我一笑。“这个名字真好，我喜欢。”
“那你叫什么名字？”我问。
“胡安娜，”她说——她念作“华娜”；“胡安娜·圣约翰（Juana St. John）。”
“你既然喜欢我的名字，我很高兴，”我说，“可我更喜欢你的。”这话傻透了，说得我自己都觉得傻；可她似乎不觉得傻，或者就算觉得，她心地太好，不肯让我知道。姑娘我认识不少，多半又丑又蠢。漂亮的姑娘难得进集市——我说的是我们阶级的漂亮姑娘。卡尔卡人倒准他们的姑娘抛头露面，因为弄到手的是谁他们不在乎，只要有人弄到手就行；美国的父母们宁肯杀了自家姑娘也不送她们去集市——前一种事常常真就做了。

===Original===

The Kalkar girls, even those born of American mothers, were coarse and brutal in appearance﻿—low-browed, vulgar, bovine. No stock can be improved, or even kept to its normal plane, unless high grade males are used.
This girl was so entirely different from any other that I had ever seen that I marveled that such a glorious creature could exist. I wanted to know all about her. It seemed to me that in some way I had been robbed of my right for many years that she should have lived and breathed and talked and gone her way without my ever knowing it, or her. I wanted to make up for lost time and so I asked her many questions.
She told me that she had been born and raised in the teivos just west of Chicago, which extended along the Desplaines River and embraced a considerable area of unpopulated country and scattered farms.
“My father’s home is in a district called Oak Park,” she said, “and our house was one of the few that remained from ancient times. It was of solid concrete and stood upon the corner of two roads﻿—once it must have been a very beautiful place, and even time and war have been unable entirely to erase its charm. Three great poplar trees rose to the north of it beside the ruins of what my father said was once a place where motor cars were kept by the long dead owner. To the south of the house were many roses, growing wild and luxuriant, while the concrete walls, from which the plaster had fallen in great patches, were almost entirely concealed by the clinging ivy that reached to the very eaves.
“It was my home and so I loved it; but now it is lost to me forever. The Kash Guard and the tax collector came seldom﻿—we were too far from the station and the market place, which lay southwest of us, on Salt Creek. But recently the new Jemadar Jarth appointed another commandant and a new tax collector. They did not like the station at Salt Creek and so they sought for a better location, and after inspecting the district they chose Oak Park, and my father’s home being the most comfortable and substantial, they ordered him to sell it to The Twentyfour. You know what that means. They appraised it at a high figure﻿—$50,000.00 it was, and paid him in paper money. There was nothing to do, and so we prepared to move.

===Chinese===

卡尔卡姑娘——就连美国母亲生的——个个相貌粗野：额低、俗气、母牛似的。不采用上等的雄种，任何种群都改良不了，连维持平常水准都不能。
这个姑娘同我见过的任何姑娘都那样不同，以致我惊奇这样光彩照人的造物竟能存在。我想知道她的一切。我总觉得，这多少年来我的权利被人偷走了：她活着、呼吸着、说着话、走着她自己的路，而我竟一直不知道有她，也不认识她。我想把损失的岁月补回来，于是问了她许多问题。
她告诉我，她生在长在芝加哥正西的特沃斯里；那一带沿德斯普兰斯河延伸，包着一大片没人烟的乡野和零零星星的农场。
“我父亲的家在一个叫橡树园（Oak Park）的地方，”她说，“我们的房子是古时候留下的少数几座之一，混凝土浇筑的，立在两条路的角上——从前想必是个很美的地方，连岁月和战争都没能抹尽它的风致。屋北立着三棵大杨树，旁边是一片废墟，父亲说那里从前是早已死绝的房主存放汽车的地方。屋南有许多玫瑰，野生野长，繁茂得很；灰泥大片剥落的混凝土墙，几乎叫爬到檐口的常春藤整个遮住了。
“那是我的家，我爱它；可如今它永远失去了。卡什卫队和收税的难得来——我们离盐溪（Salt Creek）的哨站和集市太远，那两处在我们的西南。可近来新贾玛达尔雅思（Jarth）另派了一位指挥官、一个新收税官。他们不喜欢盐溪的哨站，要寻个更好的地方；把这一带查看一番之后，他们选中了橡树园；而我父亲的房子最舒服、最结实，他们便命令他卖给二十四人团。这什么意思你是知道的。他们给它估了个高价——五万美元——用纸币付给他。没有别的路，我们便准备搬家。

===Original===

Whenever they had come to look at the house my mother had hidden me in a little cubbyhole on the landing between the second and third floors, placing a pile of rubbish in front of me; but the day that we were leaving to take a place on the banks of the Desplaines, where Father thought that we might live without being disturbed, the new commandant came unexpectedly and saw me.
“How old is the girl?” he asked my mother.
“Fifteen,” she replied, sullenly.
“You lie, you sow!” he cried angrily; “she is eighteen if she is a day.”
“Father was standing there beside us and when the commandant spoke as he did to Mother I saw Father go very white and then, without a word, he hurled himself upon the swine and before the Kash Guard who accompanied him could prevent, Father had almost killed the commandant with his bare hands.
“You know what happened﻿—I do not need to tell you. They killed my father before my eyes. Then the commandant gave my mother to one of his Kash Guard; but she snatched his bayonet from his belt and ran it through her heart before they could prevent. I tried to follow her example; but they seized me.
“I was carried to my own bedroom on the second floor of my father’s house and locked there. The commandant said that he would come and see me in the evening and that everything would be all right with me. I knew what he meant and I made up my mind that he would find me dead.
“My heart was breaking for the loss of my father and mother and yet the desire to live was strong within me. I did not want to die﻿—something urged me to live, and in addition was the teaching of my father and mother. They were both from Quaker stock and very religious. They educated me to fear God and to do no wrong by thought or violence to another, and yet I had seen my father attempt to kill a man and I had seen my mother slay herself. My world was all upset. I was almost crazed by grief and fear and uncertainty as to what was the right thing for me to do.
“And then darkness came and I heard someone ascending the stairway. The windows of the second story are too far from the ground for one to risk a leap; but the ivy is old and strong.

===Chinese===

他们每回来看房子，母亲总把我藏在二三楼之间楼梯平台上一个小洞里，在我前面堆一堆破烂；可就在我们要动身去德斯普兰斯河边安身——父亲以为在那里可以不受打扰地过活——的那天，新指挥官不期而至，看见了我。
“姑娘多大了？”他问我母亲。
“十五。”她阴沉沉地答。
“你撒谎，母猪！”他怒喝，“她一天不满十八也满了十八。”
父亲正站在我们旁边；指挥官对母亲说出这种话时，我看见父亲一下子白了脸，随即一言不发，扑向那头猪猡；陪来的卡什卫队还没来得及拦，父亲几乎赤手把指挥官打了个半死。
“后来的事你们知道——不用我说了。他们当着我的面杀了我父亲。指挥官随后把我母亲赏给他手下一名卡什卫兵；可她从他腰带上夺下刺刀，没等他们拦住，捅进了自己的心口。我想学她的样，他们抓住了我。
“我被抬进我父亲房子二楼我自己的卧室，锁了起来。指挥官说他晚上会来看我，说一切都亏得我。我懂他的意思，我拿定主意叫他见到一具尸首。
“我的心为失去父母而碎，可求生的欲望在我身上很强。我不想死——有什么东西催我活下去；此外还有我父母的教诲。他们都是贵格会的后人，非常虔诚。他们教我敬畏上帝，教我无论存心还是暴力，都不可加害于人；可我亲眼见父亲试图杀人，亲眼见母亲自我了断。我的天地全乱了。悲伤、恐惧，加上不知怎样才算对，我几乎疯了。
“随后天黑了，我听见有人上楼。二楼的窗离地太高，不敢跳；可那常春藤又老又壮。

===Original===

The commandant was not sufficiently familiar with the place to have taken the ivy into consideration, and before the footsteps reached my door I had swung out of the window and clinging to the ivy made my way to the ground down the rough and strong old stem.
“That was three days ago. I hid and wandered﻿—I did not know in what direction I went. Once an old woman took me in overnight and fed me and gave me food to carry for the next day. I think that I must have been almost mad, for mostly the happenings of the past three days are only indistinct and jumbled fragments of memory in my mind. And then the hellhounds! Oh, how frightened I was! And then﻿—you!”
I don’t know what there was about the way she said it; but it seemed to me as though it meant a great deal more than she knew herself. Almost like a prayer of thanksgiving, it was, that she had at last found a safe haven of refuge﻿—safe and permanent. Anyway I liked the idea.
And then Mollie came in and as I was leaving she asked me if I would come that evening, and Juana cried: “Oh, yes, do!” and I said that I would.
When I had finished delivering the goats’ milk I started for home, and on the way I met old Samuels, the Jew. He made his living, and a scant one it was, by tanning hides. He was a most excellent tanner; but as nearly everyone else knew how to tan there were not many customers; but some of the Kalkars used to bring him hides to tan. They knew nothing of how to do any useful thing, for they were descended from a long line of the most ignorant and illiterate people in the Moon, and the moment they obtained a little power they would not even work at what small trades their fathers once had learned, so that after a generation or two they were able to live only off the labor of others. They created nothing, they produced nothing, they became the most burdensome class of parasites the world ever has endured.
The rich non-producers of olden times were a blessing to the world by comparison with these, for the former at least had intelligence and imagination﻿—they could direct others and they could transmit to their offspring the qualities of mind that are essential to any culture, progress or happiness that the world ever may hope to attain.

===Chinese===

指挥官对这地方不够熟，没想到那常春藤；脚步声还没到我的门前，我已荡出窗子，攀着藤蔓，顺着粗糙坚韧的老藤溜到了地上。
“那是三天前。我一路藏、一路走——不知道自己走的是哪个方向。有个老婆子留我过了一夜，给我饭吃，还给我带上下一天的。我想我必定几乎是疯了，因为这三天的事，在我心里大半只是些模糊杂乱的碎片。再后来就是地狱犬！哦，我吓成了什么样！再后来——你！”
她说“你”字的那种口气里有什么东西，我说不上来；可我觉得它包含的，远比她自己知道的还多。几乎像一首谢恩的祈祷：她终于找到了一个安全的避风港——安全而长久。反正我喜欢这个意思。
随后莫莉进来了；我临走时她问我晚上来不来，胡安娜喊道：“哦，来吧，一定要来！”我说我一定来。
送完羊奶，我动身回家，路上遇见老萨缪尔斯，那个犹太人。他靠硝皮子过活——日子紧巴巴的。他是位顶呱呱的硝皮匠；可差不多人人都会硝皮，主顾便不多；有些卡尔卡人倒常拿生皮给他硝。有用的手艺他们一样不会——他们的祖上是月球上最无知、最不识字的种族，一长串一长串传下来；而他们一旦到手一点权势，连他们父辈学会的那点小手艺也不屑做了，不过一两代，就只剩靠别人的劳动过活。他们不创造什么，不生产什么，成了世上养过的最重的寄生阶级。
旧时代那些不事生产的富人，同这些人一比，倒还是世界的福气：前者至少有智力、有想象力——他们能指挥人，也能把心智传给后代，而那种心智，是世界指望得到任何文化、进步或幸福所必需的。

===Original===

So the Kalkars patronized Samuels for their tanned hides, and if they had paid him for them the old Jew would have waxed rich; but they either did not pay him at all or else mostly in paper money that did not even burn well, as Samuels used to say.
“Good morning, Julian,” he called as we met. “I shall be needing some hides soon, for the new commander of the Kash Guard has heard of old Samuels and has sent for me and ordered five hides tanned the finest that can be. Have you seen this Or-tis, Julian?” He lowered his voice.
I shook my head negatively.
“Heaven help us!” whispered the old man. “Heaven help us!”
“Is he as bad as that, Moses?” I asked.
The old man wrung his hands. “Bad times are ahead, my son,” he said. “Old Samuels knows his kind. He is not lazy like the last one, and he is more cruel and more lustful; but about the hides. I have not paid you for the last﻿—they paid me in paper money; but that I would not offer to a friend in payment for a last year’s bird’s nest. Maybe that I shall not be able to pay you for these new hides for a long time﻿—it depends upon how Or-tis pays me. Sometimes they are liberal﻿—as they can afford to be with the property of others; but if he is a half-breed, as I hear he is, he will hate a Jew and I shall get nothing. However, if he is pure Kalkar it may be different﻿—the pure Kalkars do not hate a Jew more than they hate other Earth Men, though there is one Jew who hates a Kalkar.”
That night we had our first introduction to Or-tis. He came in person; but I will tell how it all happened. After supper I went over to Jim’s. Juana was standing in the little doorway as I came up the path. She looked rested now and almost happy. The hunted expression had left her eyes and she smiled as I approached. It was almost dusk, for the spring evenings were still short; but the air was balmy and so we stood outside, talking.

===Chinese===

于是卡尔卡人照顾萨缪尔斯硝皮；倘若他们真付钱，老犹太人早发起来了；可他们要么根本不付，要么大半付纸币——照萨缪尔斯的说法，那玩意儿连烧都不好烧。
“早安，朱利安，”碰面时他招呼我，“我不久就要几张皮子了：卡什卫队的新指挥官听说了老萨缪尔斯，把我叫了去，吩咐硝五张皮，要硝到顶好。你见过这位奥提斯没有，朱利安？”他压低了嗓子。
我摇头说没见过。
“天可怜见！”老头耳语道，“天可怜见！”
“他真有那么坏么，摩西？”我问。
老人绞着双手。“苦日子在后头呢，我的孩子，”他说，“老萨缪尔斯认得他这路人。他不似上一个那样懒，更狠，更好色；还是说皮子吧。上一回的账我还没付你——他们付我的是纸币；那种东西，我拿去付朋友，连去年的鸟窝都不如，鸟窝好歹还是去年的。这几张新皮子，兴许我好久也付不了你——得看奥提斯怎样付我。他们有时候倒大方——拿别人的财产，大方得起；可他若是杂种，像我听说的是杂种，他恨犹太人，我就一个子儿也拿不着。不过他若是纯种卡尔卡，也许另当别论——纯种卡尔卡恨犹太人，不比恨别的地球人更甚；虽说也有一个犹太人恨卡尔卡人。”
那天夜里，我们头一回见识了奥提斯。他是亲身来的；我且说说事情的原委。晚饭后我去了吉姆家。我顺着小径走近时，胡安娜正立在那扇小门口。她歇过来了，几乎算得上快活。受追捕的神色已从她眼里退去；我走近时她笑了。天已近暮——春天的黄昏还短着呢；可空气温煦，我们便站在屋外说话。

===Original===

I recited the little gossip of our district that I had picked up during my day’s work﻿—The Twentyfour had raised the local tax on farm products﻿—Andrew Wright’s woman had given birth to twins, a boy and a girl; but the girl had died (no need of comment here as most girl babies die)﻿—Soor had said that he would tax this district until we all died of starvation (pleasant fellow, Soor)﻿—one of the Kash Guard had taken Nellie Levy﻿—Hoffmeyer had said that next winter we would have to pay more for coal﻿—Dennis Corrigan had been sent to the mines for ten years because he had been caught trading at night. It was all alike, this gossip of ours﻿—all sordid, or sad, or tragic; but then life was a tragedy with us.
After awhile I took Juana over to our house to see my mother. She liked the house very much. My father’s father built it with his own hands. It is constructed of stone taken from the ruins of the old city﻿—stone and brick. Father says that he thinks the bricks are from an old pavement as we still see patches of these ancient bricks in various localities. Nearly all our houses are of this construction, for timber is scarce. The foundation and the walls above the ground for about three feet are of rough stones of various sizes and above this are the bricks. The stones are laid so that some project farther than others and the effect is odd and rather nice. The eaves are low and overhanging and the roof is thatched. It is a nice house and Mother keeps it scrupulously clean and meticulously neat within.
We had been talking for perhaps an hour, sitting in our living room﻿—Father, Mother, Juana, and I﻿—when the door was suddenly thrust open without warning and we looked up to see a man in the uniform of a Kash Guard confronting us. Behind him were others. We all rose and stood in silence. Two entered and took posts on either side of the doorway and then a third came in﻿—a tall, dark man in the uniform of a commander and we knew at once that it was Or-tis. At his heels were six more.
Or-tis looked at each of us and then singling out Father he said: “You are Brother Julian 8th.”
Father nodded. Or-tis eyed him for a moment and then his gaze wandered to Mother and Juana and I saw a new expression lessen the fierce scowl that had clouded his face from the moment of his entry. He was a large man, his nose was thin and rather fine, his eyes cold, gray and piercing.

===Chinese===

我把白天跑腿时听来的本区小新闻一一学给她听——二十四人团把本地农产品税加了——安德鲁·赖特的女人生了双胞胎，一男一女，女婴死了（此处无须多说，女婴多半活不成）——苏尔放话说要把本区刮到我们统统饿死为止（苏尔这家伙可真够交情）——卡什卫队的一个兵把内莉·利维取走了——霍夫迈耶说，明年冬天煤要涨价——丹尼斯·科里根因夜里交易被抓，判去矿山十年。我们的这些闲话全是一个腔调——不是鄙琐，就是悲苦，就是惨烈；我们过的本就是一出悲剧。
过了一会，我带胡安娜过我们家去看母亲。她很喜欢那房子。父亲的父亲亲手盖的，用老城废墟里起出的石头——石头加砖。父亲说那些砖想必来自一段古路面，因为好些地方至今还见得着一团团的古砖。我们的房子差不多都是这个盖法，木料稀罕。地基和地上约三尺的墙用大小不一的粗石，以上便是砖。石头砌得有些凸出有些缩进，样子古怪，倒也别致。屋檐低低的、探出着，屋顶苫草。这是座好房子，母亲把它收拾得里外洁净、一尘不染。
我们坐着聊了约莫一个钟头——父亲、母亲、胡安娜和我——门忽然毫无预兆地被推开，抬头一看，一个穿卡什卫队制服的人对着我们。他身后还有人。我们全体起立，默然。进来两个，分立门侧；随后进来第三个——一个高大的黑脸汉子，穿指挥官的制服，我们立刻明白：这就是奥提斯。他脚跟又跟进来六个。
奥提斯把我们挨个看了一遍，然后单挑出父亲：“你是朱利安八世弟兄。”
父亲点头。奥提斯打量他片刻，目光又转到母亲和胡安娜身上；我看见一种新的表情，冲淡了他一进门就罩在脸上的那副凶恶的怒容。他是个大个子，鼻子瘦而相当端正，眼睛冷冷的、灰色、刺人。

===Original===

He was very different from the fat swine that had preceded him﻿—very different and more dangerous; even I could see that. I could see a thin, cruel upper lip and a full and sensuous lower. If the other had been a pig, this one was a wolf and he had the nervous restlessness of the wolf﻿—and the vitality to carry out any wolfish designs his crafty brain might entertain.
“So you are Brother Julian 8th!” he repeated. “I do not have good reports of you. I have come for two reasons tonight. One is to warn you that the Kash Guard is commanded by a different sort of man from him whom I relieved. I will stand no trifling and no treason. There must be unquestioned loyalty to the Jemadar at Washington﻿—every national and local law will be enforced. Trouble makers and traitors will get short shift. A manifesto will be read in each market place Saturday﻿—a manifesto that I have just received from Washington. Our great Jemadar has conferred greater powers upon the commanders of the Kash Guard. You will come to me with all your grievances. Where justice miscarries I shall be the court of last resort. The judgment of any court may be appealed to me.
“On the other hand, let wrongdoers beware, as under the new law any cause may be tried before a summary military court over which the commander of the Kash Guard must preside.
“And,” continued Or-tis, “I have come for another reason﻿—a reason that looks bad for you, Brother Julian; but we shall see what we shall see,” and turning to the men behind him he issued a curt command: “Search the place!” That was all; but I saw, in memory, another man standing in this same living room﻿—a man from beneath whose coat fell an empty sack when he raised an arm.
For an hour they searched that little three room house. For an hour they tumbled our few belongings over and over; but mostly they searched the living room and especially about the fireplace did they hunt for a hidden nook. A dozen times my heart stood still as I saw them feeling of the stones above the mantel.
We all knew what they sought﻿—all but Juana﻿—and we knew what it would mean if they found it. Death for Father and for me, too, perhaps, and worse for Mother and the girl. And to think that Johansen had done this awful thing to curry favor for himself with the new commander! I knew it was he﻿—I knew it as surely as though Or-tis had told me. To curry favor with the commander!

===Chinese===

他同他接替的那头肥猪全然两样——全然两样，也更危险；连我都看得出。我看得出那薄薄的、残忍的上唇，和丰厚、肉感的下唇。倘若前一个是猪，这一个便是狼；他有狼那种不安的躁动——也有那份精力，去实行他狡诈的脑壳里转的任何狼子企图。
“原来你就是朱利安八世弟兄！”他重复道，“关于你，我听到的报告不妙。今晚我来，为两件事。一件是警告你：如今的卡什卫队，统领它的是另一种人了，同我接替的那位不一样。我不容搪塞，不容叛逆。对华盛顿的贾玛达尔必须绝对忠诚——一切国法地法都要严格执行。捣乱分子和叛徒，从重从速。礼拜六各集市要宣读一份宣言——我刚从华盛顿接到的宣言。我们伟大的贾玛达尔授予了卡什卫队指挥官更大的权力。你们有冤屈，都到我这里来。司法不公之处，我就是终审法庭。任何法庭的判决，都可向我上诉。
“另一方面，作恶的人也当心：依新法，任何案情都可交简易军事法庭审理，由卡什卫队指挥官亲自主持。
“还有，”奥提斯接着说，“我来为另一件事——一件对你不妙的事，朱利安弟兄；不过咱们走着瞧。”他转向身后的人，简短下令：“搜！”就这两个字；可我在记忆里看见另一个人站在这间外屋里——那人一抬手臂，外衣底下掉出过一只空口袋。
他们搜那三间小屋搜了一个钟头。一个钟头里把我们那点家当翻来覆去；主要搜的是外间，尤其是壁炉四周，专找藏东西的暗洞。看他们的手在炉台上方的石头上一遍遍摸，我的心一次次停跳。
他们找什么，我们全知道——只有胡安娜不知道——也知道一旦搜出来意味着什么。父亲死，多半我也死，母亲和姑娘更惨。想想吧，约翰森竟干出这种事，只为巴结新指挥官！我知道是他——我知道得就像奥提斯亲口告诉了我一样。为巴结指挥官！

===Original===

I thought that that was the reason then. God, had I but known his real reason!
Well, they searched for an hour and found nothing; but I knew that Or-tis was not satisfied that the thing he sought was not there, and toward the end of the search I could see that he was losing patience. He took direct charge at last and then when they had no better success under his direction he became very angry.
“Yankee swine!” he cried suddenly, turning upon Father; “you will find that you cannot fool a descendant of the great Jemadar Orthis as you have fooled the others﻿—not for long. I have a nose for traitors﻿—I can smell a Yank farther than most men can see one. Take a warning, take a warning to your kind﻿—it will be death or the mines for every traitor in the teivos.”
He stood then, in silence for a moment, glaring at Father and then his gaze moved to Juana, where she stood just behind my shoulder at the far side of the room.
“Who are you, girl?” he demanded. “Where do you live and what do you that adds to the prosperity of the community?”
“Adds to the prosperity of the community!” It was a phrase often on their lips, and it was always directed at us﻿—a meaningless phrase, as there was no prosperity. We supported the Kalkars and that was their idea of prosperity. I suppose ours was to get barely sufficient to sustain life and strength to enable us to continue slaving for them.
“I live with Mollie Sheehan,” replied Juana, “and help her care for the chickens and the little pigs, also I help with the house work.”
“Mm-m,” ejaculated Or-tis; “house work! That is good﻿—I shall be needing someone to keep my quarters tidy. How about it, my girl? It will be easy work and I will pay you well﻿—no pigs or chickens to slave for. Eh?”
“But I love the little pigs and chickens, I like to care for them,” she pleaded, “and I am happy with Mollie﻿—I do not wish to change.”
“Do not wish to change, eh?” he mimicked her. She had drawn farther behind me now, as though for protection, and closer﻿—I could feel her body touching mine. “Mollie can doubtless take care of her own pigs and chickens without help. If she has so many she cannot do it alone then she has too many, and we will see why it is that she is more prosperous than the rest of us﻿—probably she should pay a larger income tax﻿—we shall see.”
“Oh, no!”

===Chinese===

我当时以为，缘故就是巴结。上帝啊，倘若我知道他真正的缘故！
哎，他们搜了一个钟头，一无所获；可我知道，奥提斯并不信他要的东西不在屋里；搜到末尾，我看得出他耐不住了。末了他亲自督搜，可在他指挥下照样没有结果，他勃然大怒。
“美国猪猡！”他突然转向父亲吼道，“你们会发现的：伟大的贾玛达尔奥西斯的后人，可不是旁人骗得了的——骗不了多久。我这鼻子专嗅叛徒——美国佬的气味，别人隔着老远才看得见，我隔着更远就闻得着。记住这警告，带话给你们那一族——这个特沃斯里的每个叛徒，不是死，就是下矿山。”
他默立了片刻，狠盯着父亲；随后目光移到胡安娜身上——她站在屋子那头，正在我肩膀后面。
“你是谁，丫头？”他喝问，“住在哪里，你干的什么营生，于本地方的兴旺有什么增益？”
“于本地方的兴旺有增益！”这是他们嘴边的老话，总是冲着我们说的——一句空话，因为根本没有什么兴旺。我们养卡尔卡人，这便是他们所谓的兴旺；我们的那份，想必是挣得刚够活命、够有力气继续给他们当牛做马。
“我住在莫莉·希汉家，”胡安娜答，“帮她照看鸡和小猪，也帮着做家务。”
“呣，”奥提斯哼了一声，“家务！好嘛——我正要人收拾我的住处呢。怎么样，我的丫头？活儿轻省，工钱优厚——不必给猪哇鸡呀当牛马。呣？”
“可我爱那些小猪小鸡，我喜欢照看它们，”她恳求道，“我在莫莉那里很快活——我不想换。”
“不想换，呣？”他学她的话。这时她又往我背后缩了缩，像要寻求庇护，也挨得更近——我觉出她的身子贴着我的身子。“莫莉自己的猪鸡，没人帮想必也照看得过来。她若多到一个人忙不过来，那就是太多了，我们倒要查一查，她凭什么比别人兴旺——多半该加她的所得税——我们查查看。”
“哦，别！”

===Original===

cried Juana, frightened now on Mollie’s account; “please, she has only a few, scarcely enough that she and her man may live after the taxes are paid.”
“Then she does not need you to help her,” said Or-tis with finality, a nasty sneer upon his lip. “You will come and work for me, girl!”
And then Juana surprised me﻿—she surprised us all, and particularly Or-tis. Before, she had been rather pleading and seemingly a little frightened; but now she drew herself to her full height and with her chin in air looked Or-tis straight in the eye.
“I will not come,” she said, haughtily; “I do not wish to.” That was all.
Or-tis looked surprised. His soldiers, shocked. For a moment no one spoke. I glanced at Mother. She was not trembling as I had expected. Her head was up, too, and she was openly looking her scorn of the man. Father stood as he usually did before them, with his head bowed; but I saw that he was watching Or-tis out of the corners of his eyes and that his fingers were moving as might the fingers of hands fixed upon a hated throat.
“You will come,” said Or-tis, a little red in the face now at this defiance. “There are ways,” and he looked straight at me﻿—and then he turned upon his heel and followed by his Kash Guard left the house.

===Chinese===

胡安娜喊道，这回是为莫莉害怕了；“求求您，她只有那么几只，勉强够她同她的男人缴完税之后活命。”
“那她用不着你帮，”奥提斯一言而决，唇边一个恶毒的冷笑，“你要来给我干活，丫头！”
随即胡安娜叫我们吃了一惊——叫我们全体吃了一惊，尤其是奥提斯。方才她还是恳求的口气，看着也有点怕；此刻她把身子挺得笔直，下巴扬起，直直望着奥提斯的眼睛。
“我不来，”她高傲地说，“我不愿意。”就这一句。
奥提斯显得意外，他的兵则惊骇了。一时没有人说话。我看母亲：她并不像我担心的那样发抖。她也昂着头，公然把她的轻蔑落在那人脸上。父亲照他素常的样子垂着头站在他们面前；可我看见他在眼角里盯着奥提斯，指头动着，像一双掐在仇恨喉咙上的手。
“你要来，”奥提斯说，这番公然违抗叫他的脸红了几分，“有的是法子。”他直直地望了我一眼——随后掉转脚跟，带着他的卡什卫队出了屋。

## IV A Fight on Market Day / 四 集市日的一场打斗
===Original===

When the door had closed upon them, Juana buried her face in her hands.
“Oh, what misery I bring everywhere,” she sobbed. “To my father and mother I brought death and now to you all and to Jim and Mollie I am bringing ruin and perhaps death also. But it shall not be﻿—you shall not suffer for me! He looked straight at you, Julian, when he made his threat. What could he mean to do? You have done nothing. But you need not fear. I know how I may undo the harm I have so innocently done.”
We tried to assure her that we did not care﻿—that we would protect her as best we could and that she must not feel that she had brought any greater burden upon us than we already carried; but she only shook her head and at last asked me to take her home to Mollie’s.
She was very quiet all the way back, though I did my best to cheer her up.
“He cannot make you work for him,” I insisted. “Even The Twentyfour, rotten as it is, would never dare enforce such an order. We are not yet entirely slaves.”
“But I am afraid that he will find a way,” she replied, “through you, my friend. I saw him look at you and it was a very ugly look.”
“I do not fear,” I said.
“I fear for you. No, it shall not be!” She spoke with such vehement finality that she almost startled me, and then she bade me good night and went in to Mollie’s house and closed the door.
All the way back home I was much worried about her, for I did not like to see her unhappy.
As I approached the house I saw that the candle was still burning in the living room﻿—I had left so hurriedly that I had given it no thought﻿—and as I came closer I saw something else, too. I was walking very slowly, and in the soft dust of the pathway my soft boots made no sound, or I might not have seen what I did see﻿—two figures, close in the shadow of the wall, peering through one of our little windows into the living room.
I crept stealthily forward until I was close enough to see that one was in the uniform of a Kash Guard while the other was clothed as are those of my class, and in the latter I recognized the stoop-shouldered, lanky figure of Peter Johansen. I was not at all surprised at this confirmation of my suspicions.

===Chinese===

门在他们身后关上后，胡安娜把脸埋进双手。
“哦，我走到哪里就把苦难带到哪里，”她哽咽道，“我给父母带来的是死，如今给你们全家、给吉姆和莫莉带来的是毁灭，说不定还有死。可不能这样——你们不能为我受罪！他放狠话的时候，眼睛直直盯着你，朱利安。他能想干什么？你什么也没做。可你不必怕。我知道怎样补救我无辜闯下的祸。”
我们竭力叫她相信我们并不在意——我们会尽力护她，叫她千万别觉得她给我们添了什么我们本来没有背着的重担；可她只是摇头，末了叫我送她回莫莉家去。
一路上她非常安静，尽管我千方百计逗她宽心。
“他不能强迫你去给他干活，”我坚持道，“就连二十四人团，烂归烂，也决不敢强制执行这样一道命令。我们还不算是十足的奴隶。”
“可我怕他会找到法子，”她答，“从你身上找法子，我的朋友。我看见他看你的那一眼了，那一眼凶得很。”
“我不怕。”我说。
“我替你怕。不，不能这样！”她说得那样决绝，几乎吓我一跳；随后她道了晚安，进莫莉家去，关上了门。
回家一路我都为她忧心，因为我不愿见她不快活。
走近家门，我看见外间的蜡烛还亮着——我走得急，压根没想到它——走近了，我又看见一样东西。我走得极慢，软靴在松软的尘土小径上没有一点声响；不然我也许看不见我所看见的——两条人影，紧贴着墙根的暗处，正从我们的一扇小窗朝外间里张望。
我蹑手蹑脚挨近，看清了：一个穿卡什卫队的制服，另一个穿的是我这个阶级的衣裳——后一个，我认出那是彼得·约翰森佝肩缩背的瘦长身影。猜疑得了这个印证，我一点也不意外。

===Original===

I knew what they were there for﻿—hoping to learn the secret hiding place of The Flag﻿—but I also knew that unless they already knew it, there was no danger of their discovering it from the outside since The Flag had been removed from its hiding place. So I hid and watched them for awhile, and then circled the house and entered from the front as though I did not know they were there, for it would never do to let them know that they had been discovered.
Taking off my clothes I went to bed, after putting out the candle. I do not know how long they remained﻿—it was enough to know that we were being watched, and though it was not pleasant I was glad that we were forewarned. In the morning I told Father and Mother what I had seen. Mother sighed and shook her head.
“It is coming,” she said. “I always knew that sooner or later it would come. One by one they get us﻿—now it is our turn.”
It was market day, and I went in with a few wethers, some hides and cheese. Father did not come along﻿—in fact, I advised him not to as Soor would be there and also Hoffmeyer. One cheese I took as tribute to Soor. God, how I hated to do it! But both Mother and Father thought it best to propitiate the fellow, and I suppose they were right. A lifetime of suffering does not incline one to seek further trouble.
The market place was full, for I was a little late. There were many Kash Guards in evidence﻿—more than usual. It was a warm day﻿—the first really warm day we had had﻿—and a number of men were sitting beneath a canopy at one side of the market place in front of Hoffmeyer’s office. As I approached I saw that Or-tis was there, as well as Pthav the coal baron, and Hoffmeyer of course, with several others, including some Kalkar women and children. I recognized Pthav’s woman﻿—a renegade Yank who had gone to him willingly﻿—and their little child, a girl of about six. The latter was playing in the dust in front of the canopy some hundred feet from the group, and I had scarcely recognized her when I saw that which made my heart almost stop beating for an instant.
Two men were driving a small bunch of cattle into the market place upon the other side of the canopy when suddenly I saw one of the creatures, a great bull, break away from the herd and with lowered head charge toward the tiny figure playing, unconscious of danger, in the dust.

===Chinese===

我知道他们来干什么——指望探出旗帜的藏处——可我也知道，除非他们已经知晓，否则从外头是探不出来的，因为旗帜早已挪离了原处。于是我躲着望了他们一会，随后绕屋一周，从正门进屋，装作不知他们在那里——叫他们知道行迹败露，是万万不成的。
熄了蜡烛，我脱衣上床。他们待了多久我不知道——知道我们被人盯上就够了；这并不愉快，可我庆幸得了预警。早晨我把所见告诉了父亲母亲。母亲叹气摇头。
“该来的来了，”她说，“我早知道迟早要来。他们把我们一个一个收拾——如今轮到我们了。”
那天是集市日，我带了几只阉羊、几张皮子和奶酪进城。父亲没有同去——其实是我劝他别去，因为苏尔会在那里，霍夫迈耶也会。我带了一块奶酪，作献给苏尔的贡礼。上帝，我多不愿意干这个！可父母都觉得还是安抚那家伙为好，我想他们是对的。受了一辈子的罪，人就不想再自寻麻烦。
集市上人已满，因为我来得晚了些。卡什卫兵四处都是——比往常多。天气暖和——头一个真正暖和的日子——一伙人坐在集市一侧霍夫迈耶办公处前的一座凉棚底下。走近时我看见奥提斯在那里，还有煤炭大王普塔夫，当然还有霍夫迈耶，另有几个，包括几个卡尔卡女人孩子。我认得普塔夫的女人——一个自愿跟了他的美国叛徒——还有他们的小女儿，约莫六岁。小姑娘在离那伙人约百尺的棚前尘土里玩耍；我刚认出她，就看见了那叫我心脏几乎停跳的一幕。
两个人正把一小群牛往集市里赶，赶到凉棚的另一侧；忽然我看见牛群里一头大公牛脱群而出，低着头，直冲向那个在尘土里玩耍、浑然不知危险的小小人影。

===Original===

The men tried to head the beast off but their efforts were futile. Those under the canopy saw the child’s danger at the same time that I did, and they rose and cried aloud in warning. Pthav’s woman shrieked and Or-tis yelled lustily for the Kash Guard; but none hastened in the path of the infuriated beast to the rescue of the child.
I was the closest to her and the moment that I saw her danger I started forward; but even as I ran, there passed through my brain some terrible thoughts. She is Kalkar! She is the spawn of the beast Pthav and of the woman who turned traitor to her kind to win ease and comfort and safety! Many a little life has been snuffed out because of her father and his class! Would they save a sister or a daughter of mine!
I thought all these things as I ran; but I did not stop running﻿—something within impelled me to her aid. It must have been simply that she was a little child and I the descendant of American gentlemen. No, I kept right on in the face of the fact that my sense of justice cried out that I let the child die.
I reached her just a moment before the bull did and when he saw me there between him and the child he stopped, and with his head down he pawed the earth, throwing clouds of dust about, and bellowed﻿—and then he came for me; but I met him halfway, determined to hold him off until the child escaped, if it were humanly possible for me to do so. He was a huge beast and quite evidently a vicious one, which possibly explained the reason for bringing him to market, and altogether it seemed to me that he would make short work of me; but I meant to die fighting.
I called to the little girl to run and then the bull and I came together. I seized his horns as he attempted to toss me and I exerted all the strength in my young body. I had thought that I had let the hellhounds feel it all that other night; but now I knew that I had yet more in reserve, for to my astonishment I held that great beast and slowly, very slowly, I commenced to twist his head to the left.
He struggled and fought and bellowed﻿—I could feel the muscles of my back and arms and legs hardening to the strain that was put upon them; but almost from the first instant I knew that I was master.

===Chinese===

赶牛人想拦，拦不住。棚下那伙人同我同时看见了孩子的危险，纷纷起身高声示警。普塔夫的女人尖叫，奥提斯扯着嗓子喊卡什卫队；却没有一个人抢到那头狂怒的畜生前面去救孩子。
我离她最近，一看见她的危险便冲了出去；可就在奔跑之间，一些可怕的念头掠过我的脑子。她是卡尔卡人！她是那头畜生普塔夫、同那个为安逸舒适出卖族类的女人的种！多少条小命就是断在她父亲和他那个阶级手里！他们会救我的姊妹、我的女儿么！
我一面跑一面想着这些；可我没有停步——我体内有什么推着去救她。想来只因为她是个小孩子，而我是美国绅士的后人。不，我照旧直冲，哪怕我的正义感在呐喊着叫我由那孩子死去。
我赶在公牛前一刻到她跟前；它见我横在它同孩子之间，便收住脚，低头刨地，扬起团团尘土，哞哞地吼——随即朝我冲来；我迎上去半途接它，存心把它挡住，好叫孩子脱身，只要人力办得到。它是头巨兽，而且显然性子恶——这大约就是把它赶到集市上来的缘故——照我看，它收拾我用不了多久；可我打算战死。
我喊小姑娘快跑，随后公牛同我撞在了一起。它挑我时，我一把攥住它的双角，把这一身年轻的力气尽数使出。我原以为那晚在地狱犬身上已使尽了；此刻我才知道还有富余——因为我竟然攥住了那头巨兽，还慢慢地、慢慢地把它的头往左拧。
它挣扎、踢腾、吼叫——我觉出背上、臂上、腿上的肌肉在那份应力下硬起来；可几乎从第一瞬起，我就知道胜负已定。

===Original===

The Kash Guards were coming now, on the run and I could hear Or-tis shouting to them to shoot the bull; but before they reached me I gave the animal a final mighty wrench so that he went down first upon one knee and then over on his side and there I held him until a sergeant came and put a bullet through his head.
When he was quite dead Or-tis and Pthav and the others approached﻿—I saw them coming as I was returning to my wethers, my skins and my cheese. Or-tis called to me, and I turned and stood looking at him, as I had no mind to have any business with any of them that I could avoid.
“Come here, my man,” he called.
I moved sullenly toward him a few paces and stopped again.
“What do you want of me?” I asked.
“Who are you?” He was eyeing me closely now. “I never saw such strength in any man. You should be in the Kash Guard. How would you like that?”
“I would not like it,” I replied. It was about then, I guess, that he recognized me, for his eyes hardened. “No,” he said, “we do not want such as you among loyal men.” He turned upon his heel; but immediately wheeled toward me again. “See to it, young man,” he snapped, “that you use that strength of yours wisely and in good causes.”
“I shall use it wisely,” I replied, “and in the best of causes.”
I think Pthav’s woman had intended to thank me for saving her child, and perhaps Pthav had, too, for they had both come toward me; but when they saw Or-tis’ evident hostility toward me, they turned away, for which I was thankful. I saw Soor looking on with a sneer on his lips and Hoffmeyer eyeing me with that cunning expression of his.
I gathered up my produce and proceeded to that part of the market place where we habitually showed that which we had to sell, only to find that a man named Vonbulen was there ahead of me. Now there is an unwritten law that each family has its own place in the market. I was the third generation of Julians who had brought produce to this spot﻿—formerly horses mostly, for we were a family of horsemen; but more recently goats, since the government had taken over the horse industry. Though Father and I still broke horses occasionally for The Twentyfour, we did not own or raise them any more.

===Chinese===

卡什卫队跑步赶来了，我听得见奥提斯喊他们开枪打牛；可不等他们到，我猛地给了那牲口最后一拧，它先跪下一膝，随即翻倒在一侧；我就那样按住它，直到一名中士过来，一枪打穿了它的头。
它死透了，奥提斯、普塔夫和众人才走近——我正回到我的阉羊、皮子和奶酪那里，望见他们过来。奥提斯唤我，我转身立定看他：能不同他们打任何交道，我都不想打。
“过来，伙计。”他喊。
我闷闷地朝他挪了几步，又站住。
“你要我做什么？”我问。
“你是干什么的？”他此刻细细打量我，“这样大的力气，我从没见过。你该进卡什卫队。你意下如何？”
“我不愿意。”我答。大约就在这时，我想，他认出了我，因为他的眼睛硬了。“不错，”他说，“忠诚的人中间，我们不要你这种。”他掉转脚跟，随即又旋回来。“当心，年轻人，”他厉声道，“你这身力气，要用得明智，用在正路上。”
“我会用得明智，”我答，“用在最正的路上。”
我想普塔夫的女人本打算谢谢我救了她的孩子，普塔夫多半也是，因为两人都朝我走来；可一见奥提斯对我那副公然的敌意，他们便转开了，为此我倒感激。我看见苏尔挂着一脸冷笑旁观，霍夫迈耶用他那副狡猾的神气打量我。
我拾掇好我的货物，走到我们惯常摆摊的那一片，却发现一个叫冯布伦的先占了地方。集市里有一条不成文的规矩：每家每户有自己的摊位。朱利安家往这块地方送农产品已经是第三代了——从前主要是马，因为我们是养马的世家；近些年是羊，因为政府把马业收归了官办。父亲同我偶尔还给二十四人团驯几匹马，可我们自己早已不养马、不有马了。

===Original===

Vonbulen had had a little pen in a far corner, where trade was not so brisk as it usually was in our section, and I could not understand what he was doing in ours, where he had three or four scrub pigs and a few sacks of grain. Approaching, I asked him why he was there.
“This is my pen now,” he said. “Tax collector Soor told me to use it.”
“You will get out of it,” I replied. “You know that it is ours﻿—everyone in the teivos knows that it is and has been for many years. My grandfather built it and my family have kept it in repair. You will get out!”
“I will not get out,” he replied truculently. He was a very large man and when he was angry he looked quite fierce, as he had large mustaches which he brushed upward on either side of his nose﻿—like the tusks of one of his boars.
“You will get out or be thrown out,” I told him; but he put his hand on the gate and attempted to bar my entrance.
Knowing him to be heavy minded and stupid I thought to take him by surprise, nor did I fail, as, with a hand upon the topmost rail, I vaulted the gate full in his face and letting my knees strike his chest I sent him tumbling backward into the filth of his swine. So hard I struck him that he turned a complete back somersault, and as he scrambled to his feet, his lips foul with oaths, I saw murder in his eye. And how he charged me! It was for all the world like the charge of the great bull I had just vanquished, except that I think that Vonbulen was angrier than the bull and not so good looking.
His great fists were flailing about in a most terrifying manner, and his mouth was open just as though he intended eating me alive; but for some reason I felt no fear. In fact, I had to smile to see his face and his fierce mustache smeared with soft hog dung.
I parried his first wild blows and then stepping in close I struck him lightly in the face﻿—I am sure I did not strike him hard, for I did not mean to﻿—I wanted to play with him; but the result was as astonishing to me as it must have been to him, though not so painful. He rebounded from my fist fully three feet and then went over on his back again, spitting blood and teeth from his mouth.
And then I picked him up by the scruff of his neck and the seat of his breeches and lifting him high above my head I hurled him out of the pen into the market place, where, for the first time, I saw a large crowd of interested spectators.

===Chinese===

冯布伦原在远角上有一个小圈，那里的生意不如我们这一段兴旺，我不明白他跑到我们这里来干什么：他有三四只劣种猪、几口袋谷子。走近了，我问他为什么在这里。
“这圈如今是我的，”他说，“收税官苏尔叫我用的。”
“你给我出去，”我答，“你知道这是我们的——整个特沃斯都知道是我们的，多少年了。我祖父搭的圈，我家一直修葺保养。你出去！”
“我就不出去。”他蛮横地答。他是个大块头，发起怒来样子很凶，两撇大胡子往鼻子两边撅着——像他那些公猪的獠牙。
“你出去，不然叫你出去。”我告诉他；可他把手按在圈门上，想拦我进去。
我知道他脑子迟钝、蠢，便想攻其不备，也没有失手：一只手按住门顶横档，我当着他的面翻进圈去，双膝撞上他的胸口，把他仰面掼进他自己猪圈的污秽里。我撞得那样重，他整个翻了个后滚翻；等他手脚并用地爬起来，满嘴污言秽语，我看见他眼里有杀气。他冲得可真凶！活脱是我刚制服的那头大公牛的冲法，只是我想冯布伦比公牛还气，却没有公牛体面。
他那一对大拳头可怕地抡着，嘴张得老大，仿佛要把我活吞了；可不知怎么，我不觉得怕。说实话，看见他的脸、他那两撇凶胡子涂满了稀猪粪，我还得憋着笑。
我格开他头几下乱拳，随即抢进身，轻轻在他脸上打了一拳——我保证没用劲，因为我不想伤他——我要耍耍他；可那结果之于我之惊愕，想必不亚于他，只是不那么痛：他从我的拳头上弹回去足有三英尺，又仰面倒了，嘴里啐出血和牙。
随后我拎住他的后脖领和裤裆，高高举过头顶，把他攒出圈外、攒进集市——在那里，我头一回看见一大圈看热闹的。

===Original===

Vonbulen was not a popular character in the teivos, and many were the broad smiles I saw on the faces of those of my class; but there were others who did not smile. They were Kalkars and half-breeds.
I saw all this in a single glance and then I returned to my work, for I was not through. Vonbulen lay where he had alighted and after him and onto him, one by one, I threw his sacks of grain and his scrub pigs and then I opened the gate and started out to bring in my own produce and livestock. As I did so, I almost ran into Soor, standing there eyeing me with a most malignant expression upon his face.
“What does this mean?” he fairly screamed at me.
“It means,” I replied, “that no one can steal the place of a Julian as easily as Vonbulen thought.”
“He did not steal it,” yelled Soor. “I gave it to him. Get out! It is his.”
“It is not yours to give,” I replied. “I know my rights and no man shall take them from me without a fight. Do you understand me?” And then I brushed by him without another glance and drove my wethers into the pen. As I did so, I saw that no one was smiling any more﻿—my friends looked very glum and very frightened; but a man came up from my right and stood by my side, facing Soor, and when I turned my eyes in his direction I saw that it was Jim.
Then I realized how serious my act must have seemed and I was sorry that Jim had come and thus silently announced that he stood with me in what I had done. No others came, although there were many who hated the Kalkars fully as much as we.
Soor was furious; but he could not stop me. Only The Twentyfour could take the pen away from me. He called me names and threatened me; but I noticed that he waited until he had walked a short distance away before he did so. It was as food to a starving man to know that even one of our oppressors feared me. So far, this had been the happiest day of my life.
I hurriedly got the goats into the pen and then, with one of the cheeses in my hand, I called to Soor. He turned to see what I wanted, showing his teeth like a rat at bay.
“You told my father to bring you a present,” I yelled at the top of my lungs, so that all about in every direction heard and turned toward us. “Here it is!” I cried. “Here is your bribe!” and I hurled the cheese with all my strength full in his face. He went down like a felled ox and the people scattered like frightened rabbits.

===Chinese===

冯布伦在特沃斯里人缘坏得很，我这个阶级的许多张脸上都绽着开怀的笑；可也有不笑的。那是些卡尔卡人和杂种。
这一切我一瞥收尽，随即回去干我的活，因为还没完。冯布伦趴在他落下的地方，我把他的谷袋和劣种猪一件一件、一只一只地攒到他身上、砸在他身上；随后拉开圈门，出去赶我自己的货物和牲口。这一开步，几乎撞上苏尔——他正站在那里，满脸的恶毒望着我。
“这是什么意思？”他简直是在朝我尖叫。
“意思是，”我答，“谁想抢朱利安家的位子，都没有冯布伦想的那么容易。”
“他不是抢的，”苏尔嚷，“是我给的。滚出去！这是他的。”
“它不是你的，轮不到你给，”我答，“我认得我的权利，谁想拿走，得先打一架。你听懂了么？”说罢我眼皮也不多抬他一下，擦身而过，把我的阉羊赶进圈去。赶羊时我看见，再没有人笑了——我的朋友们个个阴沉、惊惶；可一个人从我右边走上来，立在我身边，对着苏尔；我转眼看他，是吉姆。
我这才意识到我这行为有多严重，也难过吉姆来了——他这一站，就是默默宣告：我做的事，他同我共担。再没有别人来，尽管恨卡尔卡人不亚于我们的人多得很。
苏尔气疯了；可他拦不住我。只有二十四人团才能夺走我的圈。他骂我、威吓我；可我注意到，他是退开一段路之后才骂的。连我们的压迫者里也有一个怕我——这于我如同饿汉得了食。到此为止，这是我一生最快活的一天。
我匆匆把羊赶进圈，随后拿起一块奶酪，唤苏尔。他转身看我何事，龇着牙，像一头被逼到绝路的鼠。
“你叫我父亲给你带件礼，”我放开嗓子喊，四下里四面八方的人都听见了，都转向我们。“这就来了！”我喊，“这是你的贿礼！”说着我用足力气，把那块奶酪正正掼在他脸上。他像一头放倒的牛般栽了下去，人群像受惊的兔子般四散。

===Original===

Then I went back into the pen and started to open and arrange my hides across the fence so that they might be inspected by prospective purchasers.
Jim, whose pen was next to ours, stood looking across the fence at me for several minutes. At last he spoke.
“You have done a very rash thing, Julian,” he said; and then: “I envy you.”

===Chinese===

随后我回到圈里，动手把皮子一张张开、搭上栅栏，好教买主们查验。
吉姆的圈挨着我们家的；他隔着栅栏望了我好几分钟，末了开口。
“你干了一件非常莽撞的事，朱利安，”他说；随后又补一句：“我羡慕你。”

## V The Court-Martial / 五 军事法庭
===Original===

That afternoon I saw a small detachment of the Kash Guard crossing the market place. They came directly toward my pen and stopped before it. The sergeant in charge addressed me. “You are Brother Julian 9th?” he asked.
“I am Julian 9th,” I replied.
“You had better be Brother Julian 9th when you are addressed by Brother General Or-tis,” he snapped back. “You are under arrest﻿—come with me!”
“What for?” I asked.
“Brother Or-tis will tell you if you do not know﻿—you are to be taken to him.”
So! It had come and it had come quickly. I felt sorry for Mother; but, in a way, I was glad. If only there had been no such person in the world as Juana St. John I should have been almost happy, for I knew Mother and Father would come soon, and as she had always taught me, we would be reunited in a happy world on the other side﻿—a world in which there were no Kalkars or taxes﻿—but then there was a Juana St. John and I was very sure of this world, while not quite so sure of the other which I had never seen.
There seemed no particular reason for refusing to accompany the Kash Guard. They would simply have killed me with their bullets, and if I went I might have an opportunity to wipe out some more important swine than they before I was killed﻿—if they intended killing me. One never knows what they will do﻿—other than that it will be the wrong thing.
Well, they took me to the headquarters of the teivos, way down on the shore of the lake; but as they took me in a large wagon drawn by horses it was not a tiresome trip, and as I was not worrying, I rather enjoyed it. We passed through many market places, for numerous districts lie between ours and headquarters, and always the people stared at me, just as I had stared at other prisoners being carted away to no one knew what fate. Sometimes they came back﻿—sometimes they did not. I wondered which I would do.
At last we arrived at headquarters after passing through miles of lofty ruins where I had played and explored as a child. I was taken immediately into Or-tis’ presence. He sat in a large room at the head of a long table and I saw that there were other men sitting along the sides of the table, the local representatives of that hated authority known as The Twentyfour, the form of government that the Kalkars had brought with them from the Moon a century before. The Twentyfour originally consisted of a committee of that number.

===Chinese===

那天下午，我看见一小队卡什卫兵穿过集市，径直朝我的围栏走来，停在栏前。领队的中士开口：“你是朱利安九世弟兄？”
“我是朱利安九世。”我答。
“奥提斯弟兄将军同你说话的时候，你最好是朱利安九世弟兄。”他厉声回敬，“你被捕了——跟我走！”
“凭什么？”我问。
“你不知道，奥提斯弟兄会告诉你——带你去见他。”
来了！来得这样快。我为母亲难过；可从某方面说，我倒高兴。倘若世上没有胡安娜·圣约翰这个人，我几乎就快活了：我知道母亲父亲随后就会来；照母亲一贯的教海，我们将在那一边一个快乐的世界里重聚——一个没有卡尔卡人、没有捐税的世界——可偏偏有胡安娜·圣约翰，而这个世界我是拿得稳的，那一边我却拿不稳，我从没见过它。
似乎没有什么特别的理由拒绝跟卡什卫队走。不去，他们不过用枪子打死我；去了，说不定在被杀之前还有机会先收拾掉几头比他们更重要的猪猡——倘若他们打算杀我的话。他们要干什么，谁也说不上——只有一样说得准：必定不是好事。
于是他们把我带到特沃斯的总部，远在湖岸边；由于坐的是马拉的大车，一路倒不累，我心又不焦，反而颇为受用。我们穿过许多集市，因为总部同我们区之间隔着许多区；一路人们盯着我看，正如从前别人被拉走、去向不明时我也盯过他们。有的回来——有的不回来。我不知道自己会是哪一种。
末了我们到了总部，一路穿过绵延数里的高大楼群废墟——儿时我在那里玩耍、探幽。我立刻被带到奥提斯面前。他坐在一个大间的长桌上首；桌两侧还坐着些人——那个可恨的、名为二十四人团的权力的本地代表们；一个世纪前卡尔卡人从月球带来的政体。二十四人团原初就是那样一个数目的委员会。

===Original===

Now, however, it was but a name that stood for power, for government and for tyranny. Jarth the Jemadar was, in reality, what his lunar title indicated﻿—emperor. Surrounding him was a committee of twenty-four Kalkars; but as they had been appointed by him and could be removed by him at will, they were nothing more than his tools. And this body before which I had been haled had in our teivos the same power as The Twentyfour which gave it birth, and so we spoke of it, too, as The Twentyfour, or as the teivos, as I at first thought it to be.
Many of these men I recognized as members of the teivos. Pthav and Hoffmeyer were there, representing our district, or misrepresenting it, as Father always put it, yet I was presently sure that this could not be a meeting of the teivos proper, as these were held in another building farther south﻿—a magnificent pillared pile of olden times that the Government had partially restored, as they had the headquarters, which also had been a beautiful building in a past age, its great lions still standing on either side of its broad entranceway, facing toward the west.
No, it was not the teivos; but what could it be, and then it dawned upon me that it must be an arm of the new law that Or-tis had announced, and such it proved to be﻿—a special military tribunal for special offenders. This was the first session, and it chanced to be my luck that I committed my indiscretion just in time to be haled before it when it needed someone to experiment on.
I was made to stand under guard at the foot of the table, and as I looked up and down the rows of faces on either side, I saw not a friendly eye﻿—no person of my class or race﻿—just swine, swine, swine. Low-browed, brute-faced men, slouching in their chairs, slovenly in their dress, uncouth, unwashed, unwholesome looking﻿—this was the personnel of the court that was to try me﻿—for what?
I was soon to find out. Or-tis asked who appeared against me and what was the charge and then I saw Soor for the first time. He should have been in his district collecting his taxes; but he wasn’t. No, he was here on more pleasant business. He eyed me malevolently and stated the charge: Resisting an officer of the law in the discharge of his duty, and assaulting same with a deadly weapon with intent to commit murder.

===Chinese===

如今它只是一个名字，代表着权力、政府和暴虐。贾玛达尔雅思实际上就是他那个月球头衔所标明的——皇帝。他周围有一个二十四人的卡尔卡委员会；可委员由他任命、也随他罢免，不过是他手里的工具罢了。我被押到的这个机构，在我们特沃斯里拥有同生它的二十四人团一样的权力，于是我们也叫它二十四人团，或者叫它特沃斯——起初我就以为它是特沃斯。
这些人里有许多我认得，是特沃斯的成员。普塔夫和霍夫迈耶都在，代表我们这个区——照父亲的说法，是歪曲我们这个区。可我随即断定这不会是特沃斯正式的会议，因为那种会在更南面的另一座建筑里开——一座古时候带柱廊的堂皇建筑，政府局部修缮过；总部也是这样修缮的——它从前也是一座美丽的建筑，宽大的入口两侧至今蹲着一对大石狮，面朝西。
不，这不是特沃斯；那能是什么？随即我明白了：这必是奥提斯宣布的那条新法的一只爪子，后来证明确实是——一个审理特殊案犯的特别军事法庭。这是它的头一次开庭，而我的运气偏偏这样好：正当它需要一个人来做试验品时，我恰好不失时机地莽撞了一场，被押到了它面前。
他们叫我持卫立在桌子下首；我扫视两侧那一排排面孔，看不见一只友善的眼睛——没有一个我这个阶级、我这个种族的人——只有猪猡，猪猡，猪猡。低额头、畜生脸的汉子们，歪在椅子里，衣冠不整，粗野、肮脏、一脸病相——这就是要审我的法庭的全体人等——为什么审我？
我很快就会知道。奥提斯问谁控告我、控的什么罪；随后我头一回看见了苏尔。他本该在他的区里收他的税；可他不。不，他到这里来干更愜意的差事了。他恶狠狠地盯着我，宣读罪名：抗拒执法官吏执行公务，并以致命凶器殴打该官吏，意图谋杀。

===Original===

They all looked ferociously at me, expecting, no doubt, that I would tremble with terror, as most of my class did before them; but I couldn’t tremble﻿—the charge struck me as so ridiculous. As a matter of fact, I am afraid that I grinned. I know I did.
“What is it,” asked Or-tis, “that amuses you so?”
“The charge,” I replied.
“What is there funny about that?” he asked again﻿—“men have been shot for less﻿—men who were not suspected of treasonable acts.”
“I did not resist an officer in the discharge of his duty,” I said. “It is not one of a tax collector’s duties to put a family out of its pen at the market place, is it?﻿—a pen they have occupied for three generations? I ask you, Or-tis, is it?”
Or-tis half rose from his chair. “How dare you address me thus?” he cried.
The others turned scowling faces upon me, and beating the table with their dirty fists they all shouted and bellowed at me at once; but I kept my chin up as I had sworn to do until I died and I laughed in their faces.
Finally they quieted down, and again I put my question to Or-tis and I’ll give him credit for answering it fairly. “No,” he said, “only the teivos may do that﻿—the teivos or the commandant.”
“Then I did not resist an officer in the discharge of his duty;” I shot back at them, “for I only refused to leave the pen that is mine. And now another question. Is a cheese a deadly weapon?”
They had to admit that it was not. “He demanded a present from my father,” I explained, “and I brought him a cheese. He had no right under the law to demand it, and so I threw it at him and it hit him in the face. I shall deliver thus every such illegal tithe that is demanded of us. I have my rights under the law and I intend to see that they are respected.”
They had never been talked to thus before, and suddenly I realized that by merest chance I had stumbled upon the only way in which to meet these creatures. They were moral as well as physical cowards. They could not face an honest, fearless man﻿—already they were showing signs of embarrassment. They knew that I was right, and while they could have condemned me had I bowed the knee to them they hadn’t the courage to do it in my presence.
The natural outcome was that they sought a scapegoat, and Or-tis was not long in finding one﻿—his baleful eye alighted upon Soor.
“Does this man speak the truth?” he cried at the tax collector.

===Chinese===

他们全体凶狠地望着我，多半等着看我像我这个阶级多数人那样吓得发抖；可我抖不起来——这罪名在我看来荒唐透顶。说实在的，我怕是咧了嘴。我知道我咧了。
“什么事，”奥提斯问，“叫你这样好笑？”
“这罪名。”我答。
“这有什么可笑？”他又问——“比这轻的都有人枪毙——还是没有被疑心叛乱的。”
“我没有抗拒执法官吏执行公务，”我说，“把一家人从集市上他们自己的围栏里撵出去，不在收税官的职分之内，对不对？——一个他们家占了三代的围栏。我问你，奥提斯，在不在？”
奥提斯从椅子里半直起来。“你敢这样同我说话？”他喊。
众人都把怒脸转向我，用肮脏的拳头擂着桌子，齐声朝我咆哮；可我把下巴照旧扬着——我发过誓，到死都要扬着——当面笑他们。
末了他们静下来，我把问题又向奥提斯提了一遍；我得给他记上一笔，他答得还算公道。“不在，”他说，“只有特沃斯才能这样做——特沃斯，或者指挥官。”
“那么我没有抗拒执法官吏执行公务，”我反手掷回去，“我不过是拒绝离开属于我的围栏。还有一问：奶酪算不算致命凶器？”
他们只好承认不算。“他向我父亲索讨礼物，”我解释道，“我给他带来一块奶酪。依法他无权索讨，我便把它朝他攒去，正中他的脸。今后凡这类非法勒索的贡礼，我一律这样送。法律底下我有我的权利，我打算叫它们得到尊重。”
从来没有谁这样同他们讲过话；我猛然醒悟，纯粹是碰巧，我撞上了对付这群畜生的唯一法门。他们不光身体上是懦夫，道义上也是。他们受不了一个诚实无畏的人当面而立——他们已经露出窘态了。他们知道我有理；我若跪下去，他们满可以定我的罪；可当着我的面，他们没有这份胆量。
顺理成章的结果便是找一只替罪羊，而奥提斯没费多少工夫就找到了——他那以毒的目光落在了苏尔身上。
“这人说的可是实情？”他朝收税官喝问。

===Original===

“Did you turn him out of his pen﻿—did he do no more than throw a cheese at you?”
Soor, a coward before those in authority over him, flushed and stammered.
“He tried to kill me,” he mumbled lamely, “and he did almost kill Brother Vonbulen.”
Then I told them of that﻿—and always I spoke in a tone of authority and I held my ground. I did not fear them and they knew it. Sometimes I think they attributed it to some knowledge I had of something that might be menacing them﻿—for they were always afraid of revolution. That is why they ground us down so.
The outcome of it was that I was let go with a warning﻿—a warning that if I did not address my fellows as Brother I would be punished, and even then I gave the parting shot, for I told them I would call no man Brother unless he was.
The whole affair was a farce; but all trials were farces, only as a rule the joke was on the accused. They were not conducted in a dignified or proper manner as I imagine trials in ancient times to have been. There was neither order nor system.
I had to walk all the way home﻿—another manifestation of justice﻿—and I arrived there an hour or two after supper time. I found Jim and Mollie and Juana at the house, and I could see that Mother had been crying. She started again when she saw me﻿—poor Mother. I wonder if it has always been such a terrible thing to be a mother; but no, it cannot have been, else the human race would long since have been extinct﻿—as the Kalkars will rapidly make it anyway.
Jim had told them of the happenings in the market place﻿—the episode of the bull, the encounter with Vonbulen and the matter of Soor. For the first time in my life, and the only time, I heard my Father laugh aloud. Juana laughed, too; but there was still an undercurrent of terror that I could feel, and which Mollie finally voiced.
“They will get us yet, Julian,” she said; “but what you have done is worth dying for.”
“Yes!” cried my father, “I can go to The Butcher with a smile on my lips after this. He has done what I always wanted to do; but dared not. If I am a coward I can at least thank God that there sprang from my loins a brave and fearless man.”
“You are not a coward!” I cried and Mother looked at me and smiled. I was glad that I said that, then.
You may not understand what Father meant by “going to The Butcher;” but it is simple.

===Chinese===

“你把他撵出围栏了？——他不过朝你攒了一块奶酪？”
苏尔在上司面前素来是懦夫，红着脸，讷讷地。
“他要杀我，”他有气无力地咕哝，“他还几乎杀了冯布伦弟兄。”
于是我把那些事也对他们讲了——自始至终，我用发号施令的口吻说话，寸步不让。我不怕他们，他们也知道。有时我想，他们把这归之于我握着什么可能威胁他们的底细——因为他们永远在怕革命。他们那样碾磨我们，为的正是这个。
结局是我被警告开释了——警告说，再不称同伙为弟兄，便要受罚；即便这样，临走我还补了一枪：我告诉他们，谁不是我的弟兄，我便不称谁弟兄。
整场官司是出闹剧；可所有的审判都是闹剧，只是照例笑柄落在被告身上。它们并不按我想象中古代法庭那样庄重得体地进行，既无秩序，也无章法。
回家的路得靠两条腿走完——司法的又一副面孔——到家已是晚饭后一两个钟头。吉姆、莫莉和胡安娜都在我们家，我看得出来母亲哭过。一见我她又哭了——可怜的母亲。我不知道做母亲是不是从来都是这样可怕的一件事；不，不会的，不然人类早就绝种了——反正卡尔卡人也会很快叫人类绝种。
吉姆已经把集市上的事讲了——公牛那一幕，同冯布伦的遭遇，同苏尔的纠葛。平生头一次，也是唯一的一次，我听见父亲放声大笑。胡安娜也笑了；可我仍能觉出底下淌着一股恐怖的暗流，末了由莫莉说了出来。
“他们早晚要收拾咱们，朱利安，”她说，“可你干的事，死了也值。”
“正是！”父亲喊，“打这以后，我可以含笑去见屠夫了。他干成了我一直想干而不敢干的事。我纵然是个懦夫，至少可以感谢上帝：我的腰间生得出一个勇敢无畏的汉子。”
“你不是懦夫！”我喊；母亲望着我笑了。这句话我说得正是时候，我很高兴。
父亲说的“去见屠夫”，你们也许不懂；其实很简单。

===Original===

The manufacture of ammunition is a lost art﻿—that is, the high powered ammunition that the Kash Guard likes to use﻿—and so they conserve all the vast stores of ammunition that were handed down from ancient times﻿—millions upon millions of rounds﻿—or they would not be able to use the rifles that were handed down with the ammunition. They use this ammunition only in cases of dire necessity, a fact which long ago placed the firing squad of old in the same class with flying machines and automobiles. Now they cut our throats when they kill us, and the man who does it is known as The Butcher.
I walked home with Jim and Mollie and Juana; but more especially Juana. Again I noticed that strange magnetic force which drew me to her, so that I kept bumping into her every step or two, and intentionally I swung my arm that was nearest to her in the hope that my hand might touch hers, nor was I doomed to disappointment, and at every touch I thrilled. I could not but notice that Juana made no mention of my clumsiness, nor did she appear to attempt to prevent our contact; but yet I was afraid of her﻿—afraid that she would notice and afraid that she would not. I am good with horses and goats and hellhounds; but I am not much good with girls.
We had talked upon many subjects and I knew her views and beliefs and she knew mine, so when we parted, and I asked her if she would go with me on the morrow, which was the first Sunday of the month, she knew what I meant. She said that she would, and I went home very happy, for I knew that she and I were going to defy the common enemy side by side﻿—that hand in hand we would face the grim reaper for the sake of the greatest cause on Earth.
On the way I overtook Peter Johansen going in the direction of our home. I could see that he had no mind to meet me and he immediately fell to explaining lengthily why he was out at night, for the first thing I did was to ask him what strange business took him abroad so often lately after the sun had set.
I could see him flush even in the dark.
“Why,” he exclaimed, “this is the first time in months that I have gone out after supper,” and then something about the man made me lose my temper and I blurted out what was in my heart.
“You lie!” I cried. “You lie, you damned spy!”

===Chinese===

弹药制造是一门失传的技艺——我指的是卡什卫队爱用的那种大威力弹药——于是他们把古时传下来的那大宗弹药省着用——千百万发——不然连同弹药传下来的那些步枪就没了用武之地。只有万不得已他们才肯用弹药，这一条早把老式的行刑队同飞行机器、汽车归进了同一类。如今他们杀我们用割喉，动手的那个人，人称屠夫。
我同吉姆、莫莉和胡安娜一路回家——更确切说，同胡安娜。我又觉出那股把我往她身边吸的奇怪的磁力，弄得我每走一两步就撞上她一回；我又故意抡起靠她那边的那条手臂，巴望我的手碰上她的手，也没有失望——每碰一回，我便震颤一回。我不能不注意到，胡安娜绝口不提我的笨拙，似乎也不想法子避开我们的相碰；可我还是怕她——怕她察觉，又怕她不察觉。马呀、羊呀、地狱犬呀，我都对付得好；姑娘，我不大行。
我们谈过许多题目，我知道了她的看法和信仰，她也知道了我的；所以分手时我问她明天——当月第一个礼拜天——肯不肯同我一起去，她懂我的意思。她说肯；我便欢欢喜喜回了家，因为我知道，她同我要并肩公然对抗共同的敌人——手拉着手，为着世上最伟大的事业，一同去直面那持镰的死神。
路上我追上了彼得·约翰森，他正朝我们家的方向走。我看得出他不想碰见我，赶忙长篇大论解释自己为什么夜里出门；因为我头一件事就是问他：近来太阳落山以后，是什么稀罕营生这样常把他引到外面去。
黑地里我也看得出他红了脸。
“咦，”他嚷，“几个月来这是头一回，我晚饭后出门。”随即，也不知这人身上有什么叫我动了火，我心里的活脱口而出。
“你撒谎！”我喊，“你撒谎，你这个该死的奸细！”

===Original===

And then Peter Johansen went white and suddenly whipping a knife from his clothes he leaped at me, striking wildly for any part of me that the blade might reach. At first he like to have got me, so unexpected and so venomous was the attack; but though I was struck twice on the arm and cut a little, I managed to ward the point from any vital part and in a moment I had seized his knife wrist. That was the end﻿—I just twisted it a little﻿—I did not mean to twist hard﻿—and something snapped inside his wrist.
Peter let out an awful scream, his knife dropped from his fingers and I pushed him from me and gave him a good kick as he was leaving﻿—a kick that I think he will remember for some time. Then I picked up his knife and hurled it as far as I could in the direction of the river, and went on my way toward home﻿—whistling.
When I entered the house Mother came out of her room and putting her arms about my neck she clung closely to me.
“Dear boy,” she murmured, “I am so happy, because you are happy. She is a dear girl and I love her as much as you do.”
“What is the matter?” I asked. “What are you talking about?”
“I heard you whistling,” she said, “and I knew what it meant﻿—grown men whistle but once in their lives.”
I picked her up in my arms and tossed her to the ceiling.
“Oh, Mother, dear!” I cried, “I wish it were true, and maybe it will be some day﻿—if I am not too much of a coward; but not yet.”
“Then why were you whistling?” she asked, surprised and a bit skeptical, too, I imagine.
“I whistled,” I explained, “because I just broke the wrist of a spy and kicked him across the road.”
“Peter?” she asked, trembling.
“Yes, Mother, Peter. I called him a spy and he tried to knife me.”
“Oh, my son!” she cried. “You did not know. It is my fault, I should have told you. Now he will fight no more in the dark; but will come out in the open, and when he does that I am lost.”
“What do you mean?” I asked.
“I do not mind dying,” she said; “but they will take your father first, because of me.”
“What do you mean?﻿—I can understand nothing of what you are driving at.”
“Then listen,” she said. “Peter wants me. That is the reason he is spying on your father. If he can prove something on him, and Father is taken to the mines or killed, Peter will claim me.”
“How do you know this?” I asked.
“Peter himself has told me that he wants me.

===Chinese===

彼得·约翰森脸白了，猛地从衣裳里抽出一把刀扑向我，乱攒乱刺，刀刃够着哪儿算哪儿。起初他险些得手，这一击来得那样突然、那样毒；可我虽在臂上挨了两下、划破了点皮，总算把刀尖挡开了要害，转眼便攥住了他持刀的手腕。这就完了——我只轻轻一拧——我并没想使劲拧——他手腕里什么东西喀的一声断了。
彼得一声惨叫，刀从指间掉了下去；我把他一推，趁他退出时又结结实实补了他一脚——我想这一脚他有一阵子忘不了。随后我拾起他的刀，朝河的方向用尽力气攒出去老远，继续走我的路——一路吹着口哨。
进屋时母亲从房里出来，双臂搂住我的脖子，紧紧偎着我。
“亲爱的孩子，”她低声说，“我真快活，因为你快活。她是个好姑娘，我爱你有多深，我爱她就有多深。”
“怎么了？”我问，“你说什么呀？”
“我听见你吹口哨，”她说，“我知道那是什么意思——成年男人一生只吹一回口哨。”
我把她抱起来，抛向天花板。
“哦，母亲，亲爱的！”我喊，“但愿是真的，说不定有一天会是真的——倘若我不至于太胆小的话；可还不是时候。”
“那你为什么吹口哨？”她问，又惊讶又有点将信将疑，我猜。
“我吹口哨，”我解释道，“因为我刚拧断了一个奸细的手腕，又把他一脚踢过了路。”
“彼得？”她发着抖问。
“是的，母亲，彼得。我骂他奸细，他想捅我。”
“哦，我的孩子！”她喊，“你不知道。这要怪我，我早该告诉你。这下他再不会在暗地里斗了，要明着来了；他一明来，我就完了。”
“你这是什么意思？”我问。
“我死不要紧，”她说，“可他们会先拿你父亲抵罪，因为我。”
什么意思？——你这话我一旬也听不懂。
“那你听着，”她说，“彼得要的是我。这就是他监视你父亲的缘故。他若能坐实你父亲一点什么，你父亲下了矿山或送了命，彼得就要来认领我。”
“你怎么知道的？”我问。
“彼得亲口对我说过，他要我。

===Original===

He tried to make me leave your dear father and go with him, and when I refused he bragged that he was in the favor of the Kalkars and that he would get me in the end. He has tried to buy my honor with your father’s life. That is why I have been so afraid and so unhappy; but I knew that you and Father would rather die than have me do that thing and so I have withstood him.”
“Did you tell Father?” I asked.
“I dared not. He would have killed Peter and that would have been the end of us, for Peter stands high in the graces of the authorities.”
“I will kill him,” I said.
She tried to dissuade me, and finally I had to promise her that I would wait until I had provocation that the authorities might recognize﻿—God knows I had provocation enough, though.
After breakfast the next day we set out singly and in different directions, as was always our custom on the first Sunday in each month. I went to Jim’s first to get Juana, as she did not know the way, having never been with us. I found her ready and waiting and alone, as Jim and Mollie had started a few minutes before, and she was seemingly very glad to see me.
I told her nothing of Peter, as there is enough trouble in the world without burdening people with any that does not directly threaten them﻿—each has plenty of his own. I led her up the river for a mile and all the while we watched to see if we were followed. Then we found a skiff, where I had hidden it, and crossed the river, and after hiding it again we continued on up for a half a mile. Here was a raft that I had made myself and on this we poled to the opposite shore; if any followed us they must have swum, for there were no other boats on this part of the river.
A mile west of the river is a thick forest of very old trees, and toward this I led Juana. At its verge we sat down, ostensibly to rest; but really to see if anyone was near who might have followed us or who could accidentally discover our next move. There was no one in sight and so with light hearts we arose and entered the forest.
For a quarter of a mile we made our way along a winding path and then I turned to the left at a right angle, and entered thick brush where there was no trail. Always we did this, never covering the last quarter of a mile over the same route, lest we make a path that might be marked and followed.

===Chinese===

他要我离开你亲爱的父亲，跟他去；我不肯，他便夸口说他得卡尔卡人的宠，早晚把我弄到手。他拿你父亲的性命作价，想买我的名节。这就是我为什么那样害怕、那样不快活；可我知道，你同你父亲宁可死，也不愿我做那件事，所以我一直顶着他。”
“告诉父亲了么？”我问。
“我不敢。他会杀了彼得，那咱们就全完了——彼得在当局跟前很得脸。”
“我来杀了他。”我说。
她竭力劝住我；末了我只好答应她等一等，等一个当局也认账的衅端——虽然上帝知道，我的衅端早够了。
第二天早饭之后，我们分头出发，各走各的方向——每逢当月第一个礼拜天，这是我们的老规矩。我先去吉姆家接胡安娜，她不认得路，从没同我们一起去过。见她已装束停当，只候着我一个人——吉姆同莫莉几分钟前已经动身——她见了我，看样子很高兴。
彼得的事我一个字没对她讲：世上烦恼尽够多了，不直接落在人头上的，就不必再叫人背——各人自己的已经够沉。我领她沿河上行一里，一路留神有无人跟。随后找到我藏着的一条小船，渡过河，再把船藏好，继续上行半里。那里有一只我自己扎的木筏，我们撑着它到了对岸；若有人跟踪，只好游水，这一段河面上再没有别的船。
河西一里是一片古树参天的密林，我领胡安娜朝那里去。林边我们坐下，名义是歇脚，实际是看四下有没有人——或跟踪我们的，或碰巧撞见我们下一步的。一个人影也没有；于是我们怀着轻快的心起身入林。
我们沿一条弯弯曲曲的小径走了四分之一里，随后我向左直角拐弯，钻进没有路的密灌木。我们回回都这样：末一段路从不走同一条线，惟恐踩出一条会被认出、会被跟踪的小道来。

===Original===

Presently we came to a pile of brush wood beneath one edge of which was an opening into which, by stooping low, one might enter. It was screened from view by a fallen tree over which had been heaped broken branches. Even in winter time and early spring the opening in the brush beyond was invisible to the passersby, if there had been any passersby, which except upon rare occasions there were not. A man trailing lost stock might come this way; but no others, for it was a lonely and unfrequented spot. During the summer, the season of the year when there was the greatest danger of discovery, the entire brush pile and its tangled screen were hidden completely beneath a mass of wild vines, so that it was with difficulty that we found it.
Into this opening I led Juana﻿—taking her by the hand as one might a blind person, although it was not so dark within that she could not see perfectly every step she took. However, I took her by the hand, a poor excuse being better than none. The winding tunnel beneath the brush was a hundred yards long, perhaps﻿—I wished then that it had been a hundred miles﻿—it ended abruptly before a rough stone wall in which was a heavy door. Its oaken panels were black with age and streaked with green from the massive hinges that ran across its entire width in three places, while from the great lag screws that fastened them to the door, brownish streaks of rust ran down to mingle with the green and the black. In patches, moss grew upon it, so that all-in-all it had the appearance of great antiquity, though even the oldest among those who knew of it at all could only guess at its age﻿—it had been there longer than they could recall. Above the door, carved in the stone, was a shepherd’s crook and the words, *Dieu et mon droit*.
Halting before this massive portal I struck the panels once with my knuckles, counted five and struck again, once; then I counted three and, in the same cadence, struck three times. It was the signal for the day﻿—never twice was it the same. Should one come with the wrong signal, and later force the door he would find only an empty room beyond.
Now the door opened a crack and an eye peered forth, then it swung outward and we entered a long, low room lighted by burning wicks floating in oil.

===Chinese===

不多时我们来到一堆枝柴前，柴堆一边的底下有个洞口，弯腰低头便可钻进。洞口叫一棵倒树遮着，上面又堆满了断枝。即便在冬季和早春，里层灌木的那个洞口也是过路人看不见的——倘若有过路人的话，除难得的例外，并没有。追踪走失牲口的人或许会走到这里；旁人不会，这是个荒僻无人的去处。到了夏天——一年中被发觉的危险最大的季节——整个柴堆连同它缠结的屏风都完全隐没在一堆野藤底下，我们找它都得费一番事。
我领胡安娜钻进这个洞口——拉着她的手，像搀引一个盲人；其实里面并非黑得看不见，她每一步都看得清清楚楚。不过我还是拉了她的手——蹩脚的借口总比没有强。灌木底下那条弯弯曲曲的地道约有百码长——我那时倒巴不得它有一百哩——它在一面粗糙的石壁前戛然而止，壁上有一扇厚重的门。橡木门板黑得上了年纪，三道粗大的铰链横贯门幅，淋下绿色的锈痕；铆住铰链的大方头螺丝上，褐色的锈迹流下来，同绿痕黑迹混作一处。门上一片片生着苔藓，总之是一副极古老的样子；尽管连知情的人里最老的也只能猜猜它的年头——比他们的记性还老。门楣上方的石头里刻着一柄牧杖，还有一行字：上帝与我的权利（Dieu et mon droit）。
在这座厚重的大门前站定，我用指节在门板上叩一下，数五下，再叩一下；数三下，按同样的节奏叩三下。这是当日的暗号——从来没有两天相同。若有人拿错了暗号来，事后破门而入，门后只剩一间空屋。
此刻门开了一条缝，一只眼睛探出来；随即门向外转开，我们走进一间长而低矮的屋子，点着漂浮在油里的灯芯照明。

===Original===

Across the width of the room were rough wooden benches and at the far end a raised platform upon which stood Orrin Colby, the blacksmith, behind an altar which was the sawn-off trunk of a tree, the roots of which, legend has it, still run down into the ground beneath the church, which is supposed to have been built around it.

===Chinese===

屋子横里摆着粗木长凳；顶头是一座高起的台，台上立着铁匠奥林·科尔比，身前一座祭坛——一截锯断的树干。传说那树的根，至今还扎在教堂底下的土里，教堂就是绕着它建起来的。

## VI Betrayed / 六 出卖
===Original===

There were twelve people sitting on the benches when we entered, so that with Orrin Colby, ourselves, and the man at the door we were sixteen in all. Colby is the head of our church, his great-grandfather having been a Methodist minister. Father and Mother were there, sitting next to Jim and Mollie, and there were Samuels the Jew, Betty Worth, who was Dennis Corrigan’s woman, and all the other familiar faces.
They had been waiting for us, and as soon as we were seated the services commenced with a prayer, everyone standing with bowed head. Orrin Colby always delivered this same short prayer at the opening of services each first Sunday of every month. It ran something like this:

> God of our fathers, through generations of persecution and cruelty in a world of hate that has turned against You, we stand at Your right hand, loyal to You and to our Flag. To us Your name stands for justice, humanity, love, happiness and right and The Flag is Your emblem. Once each month we risk our lives that Your name may not perish from the Earth. Amen!

===Chinese===

我们进去时，长凳上已坐着十二个人；加上奥林·科尔比、我们几个和守门的人，一共十六个。科尔比是我们教会的头，他的曾祖父做过监理会牧师。父亲母亲都在，挨着吉姆和莫莉坐；还有犹太人萨缪尔斯、贝蒂·沃思——丹尼斯·科里根的女人——以及所有那几张熟面孔。
他们候着我们；我们一落座，礼拜便以祈祷开始，人人垂首肃立。每逢当月第一个礼拜天，奥林·科尔比开场的总是这同一篇短祷，大致是这样：

> 我们列祖的上帝啊，世代以来，在一个背弃了您的仇恨世界里，在迫害与残暴中间，我们站在您的右手边，忠于您，忠于我们的旗帜。在我们看来，您的名就是正义、人道、仁爱、幸福与公道，而旗帜是您的徽记。每月一次，我们冒死奉行，为的是您的名不至于从地上湮灭。阿门！

===Original===

From behind the altar he took a shepherd’s crook to which was attached a flag like that in my father’s possession, and held it aloft, whereat we all knelt in silence for a few seconds, then he replaced it and we arose. Then we sang a song﻿—it was an old, old song that started like this: “Onward, Christian soldier.” It was my favorite song. Mollie Sheehan played a violin while we sang.
Following the song Orrin Colby talked to us﻿—he always talked about the practical things that affected our lives and our future. It was a homely talk; but it was full of hope for better times. I think that at these meetings, once each month, we heard the only suggestions of hope that ever came into our lives. There was something about Orrin Colby that inspired confidence and hope. These days were the bright spots in our drab existence that helped to make life bearable.
After that we sang again and then Samuels, the Jew, prayed, and the regular service was over, after which we had short talks by various members of our church. These talks were mostly on the subject which dominated the minds of all﻿—a revolution; but we never got any further than talking. How could we? We were probably the most thoroughly subjugated people the world ever had known﻿—we feared our masters and we feared our neighbors. We did not know whom we might trust, outside that little coterie of ours, and so we dared not seek recruits for our cause although we knew that there must be thousands who would sympathize with us. Spies and informers were everywhere﻿—they, The Kash Guard and The Butcher, were the agencies by which they controlled us; but of all, we feared most the spies and informers. For a woman, for a neighbor’s house, and in one instance of which I know, for a setting of eggs, men have been known to inform on their friends﻿—sending them to the mines or The Butcher.
Following the talks we just visited together and gossiped for an hour or two, enjoying the rare treat of being able to speak our minds freely and fearlessly. I had to retell several times my experiences before Or-tis’ new court-martial, and I know that it was with difficulty that they believed that I had said the things I had to our masters and come away free and alive. They simply could not understand it.

===Chinese===

他从祭坛后面取出一柄牧杖，杖上系着一面旗，同父亲收藏的那面一样，高举起来；我们全体默跪了几秒，他把旗放回，我们起身。随后我们唱了一首歌——一支很老很老的歌，开头是「基督精兵前进」。这是我最心爱的一首。唱歌时莫莉·希汉拉小提琴伴奏。
唱罢，奥林·科尔比对我们讲话——他讲的永远是那些关乎我们生活和将来的实在事。一番家常的谈话，却满含着对好日子的盼望。我想，每月一次的这些聚会，是我们一生里听得到的仅有的希望的声响。奥林·科尔比身上有一种叫人生出信心和希望的东西。这些日子是我们灰色生存里的亮点，靠它们，日子才捱得下去。
之后我们又唱了一支歌，随后由犹太人萨缪尔斯祈祷，正礼就算完了；接着是会众中几个人的短讲。讲的题目多半是压在所有人心上的那一桩——造反；可我们从来没有走出过空谈。又怎么走得出去？我们大概是世上被征服得最彻底的民族——我们怕主人，也怕邻居。除了我们这个小圈子，不知道还能信谁，于是不敢为我们的机密招收一个人，尽管明知同情我们的该有几千。奸细和告密者无处不在——他们，加上卡什卫队和屠夫，就是他们辖制我们的三大工具；而这中间我们最怕的是奸细和告密者。为一个女人，为邻家一座房，有一桩我知道的，为一窝鸡蛋——都有人告发过自己的朋友，送他们下矿山、送他们去见屠夫。
短讲之后，我们只是聚谈闲话一两个钟头，享受这难得的畅所欲言、无所畏惧。我在奥提斯那个新军事法庭前的经历，不得不重讲了好几遍；我知道，他们很难相信我竟对我们的主人说了那些话，还全须全尾活着回来了。他们简直无法理解。

===Original===

All were warned of Peter Johansen and the names of others under suspicion of being informers were passed around that we might all be on our guard against them. We did not sing again, for even on these days that our hearts were lightest they were too heavy for song. About two o’clock the pass signal for the next meeting was given out and then we started away singly or in pairs. I volunteered to go last, with Juana, and see that the door was locked, and an hour later, after the rest had gone, we started out about five minutes behind Samuels, the Jew.
Juana and I had emerged from the wood, when we noticed a man walking cautiously in the shade of the trees ahead of us. He seemed to be following someone and immediately there sprang to my thoughts the ever-near suspicion﻿—spy.
The moment that he turned a bend in the pathway and was out of our sight Juana and I ran forward as rapidly as we could, that we might get a closer view of him, nor were we disappointed. We saw and recognized him, and we also saw whom he shadowed. It was Peter Johansen, carrying one arm in a sling, sneaking along behind Samuels.
Casting about in my mind for some plan to throw Peter off the track I finally hit upon a scheme which I immediately put into execution. I knew the way that the old man followed to and from church, and that presently he would make a wide detour that would bring him back to the river about a quarter of a mile below. Juana and I could walk straight to the spot and arrive long before Samuels did. And this we proceeded to do.
About half an hour after we reached the point at which we knew he would strike the river we heard him coming and withdrew into some bushes. On he came, all oblivious of the creature on his trail, and a moment later we saw Peter come into view and halt at the edge of the trees. Then Juana and I stepped out and hailed Samuels.
“Did you see nothing of them?” I asked in a tone of voice loud enough to be distinctly heard by Peter, and then before Samuels could reply I added: “We have searched far up the river and never a sign of a goat about﻿—I do not believe that they came this way after all; but if they did the hellhounds will get them after dark. Come, now, we might as well start for home and give the search up as a bad job.”

===Chinese===
