众人都被提醒提防彼得·约翰森；还有几个疑似告密者的名字也传了一遍，叫大家一齐存着戒心。我们没有再唱：即便在这些心境最轻快的日子，我们的心对歌来说也还是太沉。两点钟左右，下次聚会的通行暗号发了下来，随后我们三三两两、陆陆续续散去。我自告奋勇同胡安娜殿后，管锁门；一个钟头后，其余的人都走了，我们在萨缪尔斯身后约五分钟起步。
胡安娜同我刚出树林，就注意到前面有个人在树荫里蹑足而行。他像在跟踪什么人；那股永远悬在心头的疑心立刻跳了出来——奸细。
他一拐过小径的弯、没了影，我们便拔腿飞跑，想凑近看看他；没有失望。我们看清了他，认出了他，也看清了他盯的是谁。是彼得·约翰森，一条胳膊吊在胸前，跟在萨缪尔斯背后溜。
我在心里盘算甩开彼得的办法，末了想出一条，立刻照办。我知道老人往返教堂的路线，知道他稍后要兜一个大圈，回到下游约四分之一里处的河边。胡安娜同我可以抄直路先到，比萨缪尔斯早到许久。我们就这样办了。
到河边的地点约莫半个钟头后，听见他来了，我们退进灌木。他走来了，全然不知身后那条影子；不多时彼得也进了视野，在林子边上停住。于是胡安娜同我走出去，招呼萨缪尔斯。
“你瞧见他们没有？”我问，嗓门拿捏得叫彼得听得清清楚楚；不等萨缪尔斯答话，我又接下去：“咱们往上游找了这半天，连一根羊毛都没见着——我看它们到底不是走的这边；就算走了，天黑地狱犬也会收拾它们。来吧，咱们回家去，这趟找白找认了就是。”

===Original===

I had talked so much and so rapidly that Samuels had guessed that I must have some reason for it and so he held his peace, other than to say that he had seen nothing of any goats. Not once had Juana or I let our glances betray that we knew of Peter’s presence, though I could not help but see him dodge behind a tree the moment that he saw us.
The three of us then continued on toward home in the shortest direction, and on the way I whispered to Samuels what we had seen. The old man chuckled, for he thought as I did that my ruse must have effectually baffled Johansen﻿—unless he had followed Moses farther than we guessed.
Very cautiously during the ensuing week the word was passed around by means with which we were familiar that Johansen had followed Samuels from church; but as the authorities paid no more attention to Moses than before, we finally concluded that we had thrown Peter off the trail.
The Sunday following church we were all seated in Jim’s yard under one of his trees that had already put forth its young leaves and afforded shade from the sun. We had been talking of homely things﻿—the coming crops, the newborn kids, Mollie’s little pigs. The world seemed unusually kindly. The authorities had not persecuted us of late﻿—rather they had left us alone﻿—a respite of two weeks seemed like heaven to us. We were quite sure by this time that Peter Johansen had discovered nothing, and our hearts were freer than for a long time past.
We were sitting thus in quiet and contentment enjoying a brief rest from our lives of drudgery, when we heard the pounding of horses’ hoofs upon the hard earth of the path that leads down the river in the direction of the market place. Suddenly the entire atmosphere changed﻿—relaxed nerves became suddenly taut; peaceful eyes resumed their hunted expression. Why? The Kash Guard rides.
And so they came﻿—fifty of them, and at their head rode Brother General Or-tis. At the gateway of Jim’s house they drew rein and Or-tis dismounted and entered the yard. He looked at us as a man might look at carrion; and he gave us no greeting, which suited us perfectly. He walked straight to Juana, who was seated on a little bench beside which I stood leaning against the bole of the tree. None of us moved. He halted before the girl.

===Chinese===

又一个礼拜天，礼拜之后，我们全体坐在吉姆家院里一棵树下；树已抽出嫩叶，正好遮阳。我们谈着家常——将熟的庄稼，新生的羊羔，莫莉的小猪。世界显得少见的和气。当局近来没有作践我们——干脆没来纠缠——两个礼拜的喘息，在我们就像天堂了。到这时我们已十分笃定：彼得·约翰森什么也没发现；我们的心比好长时间以来都自由。
我们正这样安安静静坐着，享受苦役生涯里片刻的安逸，忽然听见马蹄砸在沿河通集市那条小路的硬土上。霎时间整个空气都变了——松弛的神经蓦地绷紧；平和的眼睛又罩上受追捕的神色。为什么？卡什卫队来了。
他们来了——五十骑，为首的是奥提斯弟兄将军。在吉姆家的院门口他们勒住马，奥提斯下马进院。他看我们，像人看腐肉；不同我们打招呼——正中我们的下怀。他径直走向胡安娜；她坐在一张小凳上，我倚着树干立在她旁边。我们谁也没动。他在姑娘面前站定。

===Original===

“I have come to tell you,” he said to her, “that I have done you the honor to choose you as my woman, to bear my children and keep my house in order.”
He stood then looking at her and I could feel the hair upon my head rise, and the corners of my upper lip twitched﻿—I know not why. I only know that I wanted to fly at his throat and kill him, to tear his flesh with my teeth﻿—to see him die! And then he looked at me and stepped back, after which he beckoned to some of his men to enter. When they had come, he again addressed Juana, who had risen and stood swaying to and fro, as might one who has been dealt a heavy blow upon the head and half-stunned.
“You may come with me now,” he said to her, and then I stepped between them and faced him, and again he stepped back a pace.
“She will not come with you now, or ever,” I said, and my voice was very low﻿—not above a whisper. “She is my woman﻿—I have taken her!”
It was a lie﻿—the last part; but what is a lie to a man who would commit murder in the same cause. He was among his men now﻿—they were close around him and I suppose they gave him courage, for he addressed me threateningly.
“I do not care whose she is,” he cried, “I want her and I shall have her. I speak for her now, and I speak for her when she is a widow. After you are dead I have first choice of her and traitors do not live long.”
“I am not dead yet,” I reminded him. He turned to Juana.
“You shall have thirty days as the law requires; but you can save your friends trouble if you come now﻿—they will not be molested then and I will see that their taxes are lowered.”
Juana gave a little gasp and looked around at us and then she straightened her shoulders and came close to me.
“No!” she said to Or-tis. “I will never go. This is my man﻿—he has taken me. Ask him if he will give me up to you. You will never have me﻿—alive.”
“Don’t be too sure of that,” he growled. “I believe that you are both lying to me, for I have had you watched and I know that you do not live under the same roof. And you!” he glared at me. “Tread carefully, for the eyes of the law find traitors where others do not see them.” Then he turned and strode from the yard. A minute later they were gone in a cloud of dust.
Now our happiness and peace had fled﻿—it was always thus﻿—and there was no hope. I dared not look at Juana after what I had said; but then, had she not said the same thing?

===Chinese===

“我来告诉你，”他对她说，“我已降恩于你，选你做我的女人，为我生儿育女，替我管家。”
他站着打量她；我觉得头顶的头发根根竖起，上唇的角抽动——我不知道为什么。我只知道我想扑上他的喉咙杀死他，想用牙齿撕他的肉——想看他死！随后他看了我一眼，退后一步，招呼他的几个兵进来。他们进来后，他重新对胡安娜说话；她已站起，摇摇曳曳地立着，像头上挨了重重一击、被打得半昏的人。
“你现在就可以跟我走。”他对她说。随即我插到两人中间，面对着他；他又退了一步。
“她现在不会跟你走，永远也不会，”我说，我的嗓音低得很——低过耳语，“她是我的女人——我已经占了她！”
这是谎话——后半句；可对一个为同样缘故连谋杀都干得出来的人，谎话算什么。此刻他在他的人中间——他们紧围着他，想必是他们给了他胆子，因为他出言威吓我了。
“我不管她是谁的，”他喊，“我要她，我就要得到她。现在是我讨她，她做了寡妇还是我讨。你死了，我第一个挑她——而叛徒活不长。”
“我还没死呢。”我提醒他。他转向胡安娜。
“依法你有三十天；可你若现在就来，可以省你朋友们的麻烦——他们不会再受骚扰，我还会叫他们减税。”
胡安娜小小抽了口气，环顾我们，随后挺直肩膀，紧紧挨近我。
“不！”她对奥提斯说，“我永远不去。这是我的男人——他占了我。问他肯不肯把我让给你。你永远得不到我——活的得不到。”
“这个可别太拿得稳，”他咆哮，“你们两个都在同我撒谎，我派了人盯着，知道你们不同住一个屋顶。还有你！”他瞪着我，“放规矩些——法律的眼睛，能在别人看不见的地方发现叛徒。”说罢他转身大步出了院子。一分钟后，他们在一溜尘土里没了影。
我们的幸福与安宁就这样飞了——事情永远是这样——而且没有指望。说过那样的话之后，我不敢看胡安娜；可是，她自己不也说了同样的话么？

===Original===

We all talked lamely for a few minutes and then Father and Mother rose to go, and a moment later Jim and Mollie went indoors. I turned to Juana. She stood with her eyes upon the ground and a pretty flush upon her cheek. Something surged up in me﻿—a mighty force, that I had never known, possessed me, and before I realized what it impelled me to do I had seized Juana in my arms and was covering her face and lips with kisses.
She fought to free herself; but I would not let her go.
“You are mine!” I cried. “You are my woman. I have said it﻿—you have said. You are my woman. God, how I love you!”
She lay quiet then, and let me kiss her, and presently her arms stole about my neck and her lips sought mine in an interval that I had drawn them away, and they moved upon my lips in a gentle caress, that was yet palpitant with passion. This was a new Juana﻿—a new and very wonderful Juana.
“You really love me?” she asked at last﻿—“I heard you say it!”
“I have loved you from the moment I saw you looking up at me from beneath the hellhound,” I replied.
“You have kept it very much of a secret to yourself then,” she teased me. “If you loved me so, why did you not tell me? Were you going to keep it from me all my life, or﻿—were you afraid? Brother Or-tis was not afraid to say that he wanted me﻿—is my man, my Julian, less brave than he?”
I knew that she was only teasing me, and so I stopped her mouth with kisses and then: “Had you been a hellhound, or Soor, or even Or-tis,” I said, “I could have told you what I thought of you; but being Juana and a little girl the words would not come. I am a great coward.”
We talked until it was time to go home to supper and I took her hand to lead her to my house. “But first,” I said, “you must tell Mollie and Jim what has happened, and that you will not be back. For a while we can live under my father’s roof; but as soon as may be I will get permission from the teivos to take the adjoining land and work it and then I shall build a house.”
She drew back and flushed. “I cannot go with you yet,” she said.
“What do you mean?” I asked. “You are mine!”
“We have not been married,” she whispered.
“But no one is married,” I reminded her. “Marriage is against the law.”
“My mother was married,” she told me. “You and I can be married. We have a church and a preacher. Why cannot he marry us?

===Chinese===

大家干巴巴地说了几分钟话，父亲母亲起身要走，随后吉姆和莫莉进了屋。我转向胡安娜。她立着，眼睛看地，颊上一抹好看的红。有什么东西在我胸中涌起——一股我从未识得的大力攫住了我，不等我明白它驱我做什么，我已把胡安娜搂进怀里，把吻印满她的脸和唇。
她挣扎着要脱身；我不放。
“你是我的！”我喊，“你是我的女人。我说了——你也说了。你是我的女人。上帝，我多爱你！”
她于是安静了，由我吻她；不多时她的手臂悄悄挽上我的脖子，在我把唇移开的间隙里，她的唇反过来寻找我的，在我的唇上轻轻厮磨，温存里颤着情热。这是一个新的胡安娜——一个崭新的、美妙无比的胡安娜。
“你真的爱我？”她末了问——“我听见你说了！”
“从你从地狱犬底下仰望我那一刻起，我就爱你。”我答。
“那你可藏得真够严实，”她逗我，“你既然这样爱我，怎么不告诉我？打算瞒我一辈子么——还是你怕？奥提斯弟兄要我的时候可不怕说——难道我的男人、我的朱利安，胆子比他还小？”
我知道她不过是逗我，便用吻堵住她的嘴；随后说：“你若是一头地狱犬，或是苏尔，甚至是奥提斯，我都能把我对你的看法说出口；可你是胡安娜，是个姑娘，那些话就出不来。我是个大懦夫。”
我们一直谈到该回家吃晚饭的时候，我牵她的手领她去我家。“不过先得，”我说，“去把这一切告诉莫莉和吉姆，告诉他们你不回去了。暂且我们可以住在父亲屋顶下；一俟办妥，我就向特沃斯讨准，领下相邻的那块地来种，然后盖一座房子。”
她后退一步，红了脸。“我还不能跟你去。”她说。
“什么意思？”我问，“你是我的！”
“我们还没有成婚，”她低声说。
“可谁都没有成婚，”我提醒她，“结婚是违法的。”
“我母亲成过婚，”她告诉我，“你同我可以成婚。我们有教会，有传道人。他为什么不能给我们主持婚礼？

===Original===

He is not ordained because there is none to ordain him; but being the head of the only church that he knows of or that we know of, it is evident that he can be ordained only by God and who knows but that he already has been ordained!”
I tried to argue her out of it, as now that heaven was so near I had no mind to wait three weeks to attain it; but she would not argue﻿—she just shook her head and at last I saw that she was right and gave in﻿—as I would have had to do in any event.
I went to Pthav, who was one of our representatives in the teivos, and asked him to procure for me permission to work the vacant land adjoining my father’s. The land all belonged to the community; but each man was allowed what he could work as long as there was plenty, and there was more than plenty for us all.
Pthav was very ugly﻿—he seemed to have forgotten that I had saved his child’s life﻿—and said that he did not know what he could do for me﻿—that I had acted very badly to General Or-tis and was in disfavor, beside being under suspicion in another matter.
“What has General Or-tis to do with the distribution of land by the teivos?” I asked. “Because he wants my woman will the teivos deny me my rights?”
Pthav’s woman came in while I was talking and recognized me; but she said nothing to me other than to mention that the child had asked for me. Pthav scowled at this and ordered her from the room just as a man might order a beast around. It was nothing to me, though, as the woman was a renegade anyway.
Finally I demanded of Pthav that he obtain the concession for me unless he could give me some valid reason for refusing.
“I will ask it,” he said, finally; “but you will not get it﻿—be sure of that.”
As I was leaving the house Pthav’s woman stopped me. “I will do what I can for you,” she whispered. She must have seen me draw away instinctively as from an unclean thing, for she flushed and then said: “Please don’t! I have suffered enough. I have paid the price of my treachery; but know, Yank,” and she put her lips close to my ear, “that at heart I am more Yank than I was when I did this thing. And,” she continued, “I have never spoken a word that could harm one of you. Tell them that﻿—please tell them! I do not want them to hate me so, and God of our Fathers, how I have suffered﻿—the degradation, the humiliation﻿—it has been worse than what you are made to suffer.

===Chinese===

他没有受过任圣职，因为没有可给他授任的人；可他既是这唯一的教会——他所知的、也是我们所知的唯一的教会——的头，那么显然，能授任他的只有上帝，谁又知道他不是早已受了任呢！
我想劝她回心转意——天堂既然这样近，我实在不想再等三个礼拜才进；可她不讲理——只是摇头；末了我看出她是对的，让了步——横竖我也只得让步。
我去找普塔夫——他是我们在特沃斯里的代表之一——请他替我讨一份许可，耕种我家相邻的那块空地。土地全归公社；只要地足，每人都许领他耕得动的一份，而地对我们全体绰绰有余。
普塔夫一脸丑相——似乎忘了我救过他孩子的命——说他不知道能替我做什么，说我得罪了奥提斯将军，失了宠，另外还涉着一桩嫌疑。
“土地由特沃斯分配，同奥提斯将军什么相干？”我问，“因为他要我的女人，特沃斯就要剥夺我的权利么？”
我说话时普塔夫的女人进来了，认出了我；除了提一句孩子念叨过我，她一句话也没对我说。普塔夫为此沉下脸，喝令她出屋，像人喝令一头牲口。这于我倒无所谓——那女人反正是叛徒。
末了我向普塔夫下了硬话：除非他拿出一条像样的拒绝理由，就得替我把许可讨来。
“我去问问，”他末了说，“可你拿不到——这点你放心。”
我出屋时，普塔夫的女人拦住了我。“我能替你出力的，一定出。”她低声说。她必定看见我本能地退开，像避开一件不洁的东西，因为她红了脸，随后说：“请别这样！我受够了。我为我的背信付清了代价；可你要知道，美国佬，”她把嘴唇凑到我耳边，“我心里如今比我干那件事的时候更像美国佬。还有，”她接着说，“我从没说过一个字害过你们哪一个。替我把这话捎给他们——求你捎到！我不愿他们那样恨我。我们列祖的上帝啊，我受了多少罪——那种糟蹋，那种作践——比你们受的还狠。

===Original===

The creatures are lower than the beasts of the forest. When his friends come he serves them food and drink and﻿—me! Ugh! I could kill him, if I were not such a coward. I have seen and I know how they can make one suffer before death.”
I could not but feel sorry for her, and I told her so. The poor creature appeared very grateful and assured me that she would aid me.
“I know a few things about Pthav that he would not want Or-tis to know,” she said, “and even though he beats me for it I will make him get the land for you.”
Again I thanked her and departed, realizing that there were others worse off than we﻿—that the closer one came to the Kalkars the more hideous life became.
At last the day came and we set out for the church. As before I took Juana, though she tried to order it differently; but I would not trust her to the protection of another. We arrived without mishap﻿—sixteen of us﻿—and after the religious services were over Juana and I stood before the altar and were married﻿—much after the fashion of the ancients, I imagine.
Juana was the only one of us who was at all sure about the ceremony and it had been she who trained Orrin Colby﻿—making him memorize so much that he said his head ached for a week. All I can recall of it is that he asked me if I would take her to be my lawfully wedded wife﻿—I lost my voice and only squeaked a weak “yes”﻿—and that he pronounced us man and wife, and then something about not letting anyone put asunder what God had joined together. I felt very much married and very happy, and then just as it was all nicely over and everybody was shaking hands with us there came a loud knocking at the door and the command: “Open, in the name of the law!”
We looked at one another and gasped. Orrin Colby put a finger to his lips for silence and led the way toward the back of the church where a rough niche was built in, containing a few shelves upon which stood several rude candle sticks. We knew our parts and followed him in silence, except one who went quickly about putting out the lights. All the time the pounding on the door became more insistent, and then we could hear the strokes of what must have been an ax beating at the panels. Finally, a shot was fired through the heavy wood and we knew that it was the Kash Guard.

===Chinese===

这些畜生比林子里的野兽还低。他的朋友一来，他就叫人上酒上菜——还有我！呸！我恨不得杀了他，若不是我这样没胆子。我见过、也知道他们能叫人死前受多少罪。”
我不由得可怜她，也对她这样说了。这可怜人显得很感激，向我保证她一定帮我。
“我知道普塔夫几件事，是他不愿叫奥提斯知道的，”她说，“哪怕他为这个打我，我也要叫他把你那块地弄来。”
我再三谢了她，走了，心里明白世上还有比我们更苦的——离卡尔卡人越近，生活就越丑恶。
这一天终于来到，我们动身去教堂。同先前一样我带着胡安娜，虽然她想把次序另排一排；可我不肯把她托给旁人保护。我们一路无事——十六个人——宗教仪式完毕后，胡安娜同我站到祭坛前，成了婚——同古人比，我想也差不多。
我们中间对这套仪节有把握的只有胡安娜一个，奥林·科尔比就是她调教的——叫他背这背那，他说脑袋疼了一个礼拜。我如今记得起的只有：他问我愿不愿娶她为我的合法妻子——我的嗓子哑了，只挤出一声细弱的“愿意”；随后他宣布我们结为夫妻，又说了一句什么，叫任何人不得拆散上帝所结合的。我感到十足成了婚，十足的快活；正在一切圆满收场、人人同我们握手的当口，门上响起重重的擂声和一声喝令：“以法律的名义，开门！”
我们面面相觑，倒抽冷气。奥林·科尔比把一根指头压在唇上示意肃静，领路走向教堂后部；那里砌着一个粗陋的壁龛，几层搁板上立着几支粗制的烛台。我们各知各的位置，默然跟上，只有一人快步去灭灯。门上的擂声始终不停，随后听得见斧头砍门板的声响。末了，一颗子弹射穿厚木门板——我们知道了：是卡什卫队。

===Original===

Taking hold of the lower shelf Orrin pulled upward with all his strength with the result that all the shelving and woodwork to which it was attached slid upward revealing an opening beyond. Through this we filed, one by one, down a flight of stone steps into a dark tunnel. When the last man had passed I lowered the shelving to its former place, being careful to see that it fitted tightly.
Then I turned and followed the others, Juana’s hand in mine. We groped our way for some little distance in the stygian darkness of the tunnel until Orrin halted and whispered to me to come to him. I went and stood at his side while he told me what I was to do. He had called upon me because I was the tallest and the strongest of the men. Above us was a wooden trap. I was to lift this and push it aside.
It had not been moved for generations and was very heavy with earth and growing things above; but I put my shoulders to it and it had to give﻿—either it or the ground beneath my feet and that could not give. At last I had it off and in a few minutes I had helped them all out into the midst of a dense wood. Again we knew our parts, for many times had we been coached for just such an emergency, and one by one the men scattered in different directions, each taking his woman with him.
Suiting our movements to a prearranged plan, we reached our homes from different directions and at different times, some arriving after sundown, to the end that were we watched, none might be sure that we had been upon the same errand or to the same place.

===Chinese===

奥林抓住最下面一层搁板，用尽全力向上拉；整架搁板连同连着的木活一齐滑了上去，露出后面的洞口。我们一个跟一个钻过去，顺一道石阶下进一条黑暗的地道。最后一人过去后，我把搁板放回原位，仔细看它合得严丝合缝。
随后我转过身，牵着胡安娜的手跟上众人。在地道漆黑的暗里，我们摸索着走了好一段，奥林停住，唤我到他身边去。我过去立在他旁边，听他吩咐我的差事。他挑中我，因为我是这些人里最高最壮的。头顶上有一扇木活板。我要把它顶起来，推到一边。
它几代人没动过了，压着土，生着草木，重得很；可我把肩膀顶上去，它就得让——不是它让，就是我脚下的地让，而地是不会让的。末了我把它掀掉了；几分钟内，我把他们全都拉了上去，出到一片密林中央。我们又是各知各的位置，这样的急难我们操演过许多回；汉子们一个个朝不同方向散去，各自带着自己的女人。
按预先约定的方案，我们从不同方向、在不同时刻回到各自家中，有的日落之后才到——为的是即便有人盯着，也没有谁能断定我们是在办同一件事、去了同一个地方。

## VII The Arrest of Julian 8th / 七 朱利安八世被捕
===Original===

A week later, Pthav sent for me and very gruffly told me that the teivos had issued the permit for me to use the land adjoining that allotted to my father. As before, his woman stopped me as I was leaving.
“It was easier than I thought,” she told me, “for Or-tis has angered the teivos by attempting to usurp all its powers and knowing that he hates you they were glad to grant your petition over his objection.”
During the next two or three months I was busy building our home and getting my place in order. I had decided to raise horses and obtained permission from the teivos to do so﻿—again over Or-tis’ objections. Of course the government controlled the entire horse traffic; but there were a few skilled horsemen permitted to raise them, though at any time their herds could be commandeered by the authorities. I knew that it might not be a very profitable business; but I loved horses and wanted to have just a few﻿—a stallion and two or three mares. These I could use in tilling my fields and in the heavier work of hauling, and at the same time I would keep a few goats, pigs and chickens to insure us a living.
Father gave me half his goats and a few chickens, and from Jim I bought two young sows and a boar. Later I traded a few goats to the teivos for two old mares that they thought were no longer worth keeping and that same day I was told of a stallion﻿—a young outlaw﻿—that Hoffmeyer had. The beast was five years old and so vicious that none dared approach him and they were on the point of destroying him.
I went to Hoffmeyer and asked if I could buy the animal﻿—I offered him a goat for it, which he was glad to accept and then I took a strong rope and went to get my property. I found a beautiful bay with the temper of a hellhound. When I attempted to enter the pen he rushed at me with ears back and jaws distended; but I knew that I must conquer him now or never and so I met him with only a rope in my hand, nor did I wait for him. Instead, I ran to meet him and when he was in reach I struck him once across the face with the rope, at which he wheeled and let both hind feet fly out at me. Then I cast the noose that was at one end of the rope and caught him about the neck and for half an hour we had a battle of it.

===Chinese===

一个礼拜后，普塔夫把我叫了去，粗声粗气地告诉我：特沃斯已发下许可，准我使用父亲名下那份地之外相邻的那块。同先前一样，他女人在我离开时拦住了我。
“比我想的容易，”她告诉我，“奥提斯想篡夺特沃斯的全权，把它惹恼了；他们知道他恨你，乐得逆着他批准你的呈请。”
随后两三个月里，我忙着盖我们的家、整治我的地方。我打定主意养马，也向特沃斯讨到了准——又是顶着奥提斯的反对。当然，全部马的买卖归政府管；可有数的几个好马手获准养马，尽管马群随时可以被当局征走。我知道这未必是赚钱的营生；可我爱马，只想养几匹——一匹儿马、两三匹骒马。耕地、拉重活用得着它们；同时我再养几只羊、几口猪、几只鸡，保我们一份生计。
父亲把羊分了我一半，还有几只鸡；我从吉姆那里买了两只小母猪、一口公猪。后来我拿几只羊同特沃斯换了两匹他们嫌不值得再留的老骒马；同一天，有人告诉我霍夫迈耶手里有一匹儿马——一匹年轻的劣马。那牲口五岁，烈得没人敢近前，他们正要着手弄死它。
我去找霍夫迈耶，问能不能买——我出一只羊，他欣然收下；我拿一根结实的绳子去取我的财产。那是一匹漂亮的栗色马，一身地狱犬的脾气。我试着进栏，它耳朵一伏、牙关大张地冲我扑来；可我知道，此刻不制服它就再没有机会了，于是手里只攥着一根绳子迎上去，也不等它。我反而跑去迎它，够得着了，一绳子抽在它脸上；它一旋身，双双后蹄朝我踢来。我把绳头的活套攒出去，套住它的脖子；随后我们大战了半个钟头。

===Original===

I never struck him unless he tried to bite or strike me and finally I must have convinced him that I was master, for he let me come close enough to stroke his glossy neck, though he snorted loudly all the while that I did so. When I had quieted him a bit I managed to get a half hitch around his lower jaw and after that I had no difficulty in leading him from the pen. Once in the open I took the coils of my rope in my left hand and before the creature knew what I was about, had vaulted to his back.
He fought fair, I’ll say that for him, for he stood on his feet; but for fifteen minutes he brought into play every artifice known to horse-kind for unseating a rider. Only my skill and my great strength kept me on his back and at that even the Kalkars who were looking on had to applaud my horsemanship.
After that it was easy. I treated him with kindness, something he had never known before, and as he was an unusually intelligent animal, he soon learned that I was not only his master but his friend, and from being an outlaw he became one of the kindest and most tractable animals I have ever seen, so much so in fact, that Juana used to ride him bareback.
I love all horses and always have; but I think I never loved any animal as I did Red Lightning, as we named him.
The authorities left us pretty well alone for some time because they were quarreling among themselves. Jim said there was an ancient saying about honest men getting a little peace when thieves fell out and it certainly fitted our case perfectly; but the peace didn’t last forever and when it broke the bolt that fell was the worst calamity that had ever come to us.
One evening Father was arrested for trading at night and taken away by the Kash Guard. They got him as he was returning to the house from the goat pens and would not even permit him to bid goodbye to Mother. Juana and I were eating supper in our own house about three hundred yards away and never knew anything about it until Mother came running over to tell us. She said that it was all done so quickly that they had Father and were gone before she could run from the house to where they arrested him. They had a spare horse and hustled him onto it﻿—then they galloped away toward the lake front. It seems strange that neither Juana nor I heard the hoof beats of the horses; but we did not.

===Chinese===

它不咬不踢，我就不打它；末了它想必信了我是主人，竟容我凑近去抚它油亮的脖子——尽管我抚的全程它都在大声喷鼻。把它安抚得差不多，我设法把绳子在下颚上绕了个半扣；此后牵它出栏就不费事了。一到空地，我把绳圈收进左手，趁那牲口还没明白我要干什么，已经翻上了它的背。
它打得规矩，我得替它说这句——它始终四脚着地；可足足十五分钟，马类所知的每一种下鞍的伎俩它都使尽了。全凭我的技术和这一身大力，我才稳在它背上；连旁观的卡尔卡人也不得不为我的骑术喝彩。
打那以后就容易了。我善待它——它从没受过这个——它又是头出奇有灵性的牲口，很快就明白我不但是它的主人，还是它的朋友；从一匹劣马，它变成我平生所见最和善、最驯顺的牲口之一，驯到胡安娜常骑着它裸鞍走。
我素来爱一切马；可我想我从没有爱过哪只牲口像爱红闪电——我们这样叫它——这样深。
有一阵子当局相当放过我们，因为他们自己吵起了架。吉姆说，古话讲，贼咬贼的时候老实人得几天清净；这话对我们再合适不过。可清净没有维持到永远；它一破，落下的霹雳是我们遭过的最大灾殃。
一天傍晚，父亲因夜里交易被捕，被卡什卫队带走了。他们在他从羊圈回屋的路上抓的他，连同母亲道别的工夫都不给。胡安娜同我正在自己家里吃晚饭，隔着三百码，直到母亲跑来报信才知道。她说一切快得很：她还没从屋里跑到抓人的地方，他们已经押着父亲走了。他们备着一匹空马，把他推上去，随后朝湖滨疾驰而去。说来奇怪，胡安娜同我竟都没听见马蹄声；可我们就是没听见。

===Original===

I went immediately to Pthav and demanded to know why Father had been arrested; but he professed ignorance of the whole affair. I had ridden to his place on Red Lightning and from there I started to the Kash Guard barracks where the military prison is. It is contrary to law to approach the barracks after sunset without permission, so I left Red Lightning in the shadow of some ruins a hundred yards away and started on foot toward that part of the post where I knew the prison to be located. The latter consists of a high stockade around the inside of which are rude shelters upon the roofs of which armed guards patrol. The center of the rectangle is an open court where the prisoners exercise, cook their food, and wash their clothing﻿—if they care to. There are seldom more than fifty confined here at a time as it is only a detention camp where they hold those who are awaiting trial and those who have been sentenced to the mines. The latter are usually taken away when there are from twenty-five to forty of them.
After I reached the stockade I was at a loss to communicate with my father, since any noise I might make would doubtless attract the attention of the guard; but finally, through a crack between two boards, I attracted the attention of a prisoner. The man came close to the stockade and I whispered to him that I wished to speak with Julian 8th. By luck I had happened upon a decent fellow, and it was not long before he had brought Father and I was talking with him, in low whispers.
He told me that he had been arrested for trading by night and that he was to be tried on the morrow. I asked him if he would like to escape﻿—that I would find the means if he wished me to, but he said that he was innocent of the charge as he had not been off our farm at night for months and that doubtless it was a case of mistaken identity and that he would be freed in the morning.
I had my doubts; but he would not listen to escape as he argued that it would prove his guilt and then they would have him for sure.
“Where may I go,” he asked, “if I escape? I might hide in the woods; but what a life! I could never return to your mother, and so sure am I that they can prove nothing against me that I would rather stand trial than face the future as an outlaw.”

===Chinese===

我立刻去找普塔夫，要他说出父亲被捕的缘故；可他推说对整件事一无所知。我是骑红闪电去的；离开他家，我又朝卡什卫队兵营去——军事监狱就在那里。日落后无照近营是违法的，我便把红闪电留在百码外一片废墟的影子里，徒步朝营区监狱所在的那一角走去。监狱是一圈高高的栅栏，里面围着些粗陋的棚屋，屋顶上有持枪的卫兵巡逻。长方形的中央是露天院子，囚犯在那里放风、做饭、洗衣——倘若他们还有这份心思的话。这里一次难得关上五十人以上，因为它只是个拘押营，关的是候审的、和判了矿山的人。判矿山的凑够二十五到四十个，就成批押走。
到了栅栏跟前，我却没法同父亲通消息，弄出声响必得招来卫兵；末了，我隔着两块木板的一条缝，引起一个囚犯的注意。那人凑到栅栏边，我低声说要找朱利安八世。运气好，碰上的是个正派人；不多时他就把父亲领来了，我们低声切切地谈起话来。
他说他因夜里交易被捕，明天受审。我问他想不想逃——他若愿意，我去设法；可他说他是冤枉的，几个月来他夜里根本没离开过我们的农场，这必定是认错了人，明早就会放他。
我心里存疑；可他不听逃的话，说逃了倒坐实了罪名，那他们就真抓定他了。
“我逃了，”他问，“又能去哪里？躲进林子么——那叫什么日子！我再回不到你母亲身边。我拿得稳他们拿不出我的罪证，与其去做亡命之徒，我宁可受审。”

===Original===

I think now that he refused my offer of assistance not because he expected to be released but rather that he feared that evil might befall me were I to connive at his escape. At any rate I did nothing, since he would not let me, and went home again with a heavy heart and dismal forebodings.
Trials before the teivos were public, or at least were supposed to be, though they made it so uncomfortable for spectators that few, if any, had the temerity to attend; but under Jarth’s new rule the proceedings of the military courts were secret and Father was tried before such a court.

===Chinese===

如今我想，他拒绝我的援手，倒不是指望获释，而是怕我助他脱逃会招来祸事。总之他既然不许，我什么也没做成，怀着一颗沉重的心和不祥的预感回了家。
特沃斯的审判是公开的——至少名义上公开，尽管他们叫旁听的人那样难受，鲜有谁敢到场；可在雅思的新规之下，军事法庭的诉讼是秘密的，父亲受审的正是这样一个法庭。

## VIII I Horsewhip an Officer / 八 我马鞭抽一个军官
===Original===

We passed days of mental anguish﻿—hearing nothing, knowing nothing﻿—and then one evening a single Kash Guard rode up to Father’s house. Juana and I were there with Mother. The fellow dismounted and knocked at the door﻿—a most unusual courtesy from one of these. He entered at my bidding and stood there a moment looking at Mother. He was only a lad﻿—a big, overgrown boy, and there was neither cruelty in his eyes nor the mark of the beast in any of his features. His mother’s blood evidently predominated, and he was unquestionably not all Kalkar. Presently he spoke.
“Which is Julian 8th’s woman?” he asked; but he looked at Mother as though he already guessed.
“I am,” said Mother.
The lad shuffled his feet and caught his breath﻿—it was like a stifled sob.
“I am sorry,” he said, “that I bring you such sad news,” and then we guessed that the worst had happened.
“The mines?” Mother asked him, and he nodded affirmatively.
“Ten years!” he exclaimed, as one might announce a sentence of death, for such it was. “He never had a chance,” he volunteered. “It was a terrible thing. They are beasts!”
I could not but show my surprise that a Kash Guard should speak so of his own kind, and he must have seen it in my face.
“We are not all beasts,” he hastened to exclaim.
I commenced to question him then and I found that he had been a sentry at the door during the trial and had heard it all. There had been but one witness﻿—the man who had informed on Father, and Father had been given no chance to make any defense.
I asked him who the informer was.
“I am not sure of the name,” he replied; “he was a tall, stoop-shouldered man. I think I heard him called Peter.”
But I had known even before I asked. I looked at Mother and saw that she was dry-eyed and that her mouth had suddenly hardened into a firmness of expression such as I had never dreamed it could assume.
“Is that all?” she asked.
“No,” replied the youth, “it is not. I am instructed to notify you that you have thirty days to take another man, or vacate these premises,” and then he took a step toward Mother. “I am sorry, Madam,” he said. “It is very cruel; but what are we to do? It becomes worse each day.

===Chinese===

我们在精神熬煎里过了好些天——听不见音信，什么都不知道——随后一天傍晚，一名卡什卫兵独自骑马到父亲家门前。胡安娜同我正陪母亲在那里。那家伙下马敲门——这一号人对人客气，稀罕得很。他应我声进了屋，站住，望了母亲一会。他不过是个小伙子——个头蹿得很高的大孩子——眼里没有凶残，五官里也寻不出畜生的印记。显然他母亲的血占了上风，他无疑不是纯种卡尔卡人。片刻他开了口。
“哪位是朱利安八世的女人？”他问；眼睛却望着母亲，像早已猜到。
“我是。”母亲说。
小伙子蹭了蹭脚，抽了口气——像一声憋住的呜咽。
“我很抱歉，”他说，“给您带来这样的噩耗。”我们便猜到最坏的发生了。
“矿山？”母亲问，他点头。
“十年！”他喊，像宣判死刑一样——这也就是死刑。“他根本没有机会，”他自己又添了一句，“太可怕了。他们是畜生！”
卡什卫兵竟这样说自己人，我不能不露出惊讶，他想必也在我脸上看见了。
“我们并不都是畜生。”他忙喊。
我随即盘问他，才知道审判时他在门口站岗，全听见了。证人只有一个——告发父亲的那个人；而父亲连一句申辩的机会都没有得到。
我问他告密的是谁。
“名字拿不准，”他答，“是个高个子、佝肩膀的男人。我记得听见人叫他彼得。”
可不等他答我早已知道了。我看母亲：她眼睛干着，嘴却骤然绷出一种我做梦也想不到的坚毅。
“就这些？”她问。
“不，”小伙子答，“还有。我奉命通知您：三十天内另择一主，或迁出此屋。”说着他朝母亲迈了一步。“对不起，夫人，”他说，“太狠了；可我们有什么办法？一天比一天坏。

===Original===

Now they are grinding down even the Kash Guard, so that there are many of us who﻿—;” but he stopped suddenly as though realizing that he was on the point of speaking treason to strangers, and turning on his heel he quit the house and a moment later was galloping away.
I expected Mother to break down then; but she did not. She was very brave; but there was a new and terrible expression in her eyes﻿—those eyes that had shone forth always with love. Now they were bitter, hate-filled eyes. She did not weep﻿—I wish to God she had. Instead she did that which I had never known her to do before﻿—she laughed aloud. Upon the slightest pretext, or upon no pretext at all, she laughed. We were afraid for her.
The suggestion dropped by the Kash Guard started in my mind a train of thought of which I spoke to Mother and Juana, and after that Mother seemed more normal for a while, as though I had aroused hope, however feeble, where there had been no hope before. I pointed out that if the Kash Guard was dissatisfied, the time was ripe for revolution, for if we could get only a part of them to join us there would surely be enough of us to overthrow those who remained loyal to The Flag. Then we would liberate all prisoners and set up a republic of our own such as the ancients had had.
It took time to develop my plan. I talked with everyone I could trust and found them all willing to join me when we had enough. In the meantime, I cared for my own place and Father’s as well﻿—I was very busy and time flew rapidly.
About a month after Father was taken away, I came home one day with Juana who had accompanied me upriver in search of a goat that had strayed. We had found its carcass, or rather its bones, where the hellhounds had left them. Mother was not at our house, where she now spent most of her time, so I went over to Father’s to get her. As I approached the door I heard sounds of an altercation and scuffling that made me cover the few remaining yards at a rapid run.
Without waiting to knock, as Mother had taught me always to do, I burst into the living room to discover Mother in the clutches of Peter Johansen. She was trying to fight him off; but he was forcing her slowly toward her bedroom, for he was a large and powerful man. He heard me just as I leaped for him and turning, grappled with me.

===Chinese===

如今他们连卡什卫队也往下碾，我们中间有很多人——”他猛然收住，像是醒悟到自己正要对陌生人出叛逆之言；随即转身出屋，片刻后纵马疾驰而去。
我原以为母亲会当场垮掉；她没有。她非常勇敢；可她眼里换了一副新的、可怕的神情——那双一向放出慈爱的眼睛，如今是苦恨的眼睛。她没有哭——我求上帝叫她哭出来才好。她反而做了一件我从没见她做过的事——放声笑。为一丁点由头，或全然没有由头，她就笑。我们都为她怕。
卡什卫兵漏下的那半句话，引出我一连串念头，我把它说给母亲和胡安娜听；此后母亲似乎正常了一阵，仿佛我在原先无望的地方唤起了一点希望，尽管微弱。我指出：卡什卫队既然心怀不满，造反的时机就熟了；只要争取到他们一部分倒向我们，我们的人数便足以推倒其余那些忠于他们那套的，随后放出全部囚犯，建立我们自己的共和国，同古人的一样。
我的计划需要时间展开。凡信得过的我都谈了，发现只等人手够了，人人愿意入伙。其间我照管自己的地方，也照管父亲的——忙得很，日子飞快。
父亲被带走约莫一个月后，有一天我同胡安娜回家——她陪我沿河上游找一只走失的羊去了。我们找到的是它的残骸，或者说骨头，地狱犬吃剩的。母亲不在我们家——她如今大半时光在我们那边过——我便去父亲家接她。走近门口，听见一阵争执扭打的声音，剩下那几码我是飞跑过去的。
顾不得敲门——母亲素来教我先敲——我撞进外间，正见母亲落在彼得·约翰森的爪子里。她正想挣脱；他把她慢慢往卧室里逼——他是个高大有力的人。我跃向他的那一刻他听见了，转过身同我扭作一团。

===Original===

He tried to hold me off with one hand then while he drew his knife; but I struck him in the face with one fist and knocked him from me, way across the room. He was up again in an instant, bleeding from nose and mouth, and back at me with his knife in his hand, slashing furiously. Again I struck him and knocked him down and when he arose and came again I seized his knife-hand and tore the weapon from him. He had no slightest chance against me, and he saw it soon, for he commenced to back away and beg for mercy.
“Kill him, Julian,” said Mother; “kill the murderer of your father.”
I did not need her appeal to influence me, for the moment that I had seen Peter there I knew my long awaited time had come to kill him. He commenced to cry then﻿—great tears ran down his cheeks and he bolted for the door and tried to escape. It was my pleasure to play with him as a cat plays with a mouse.
I kept him from the door, seizing him and hurling him bodily across the room, and then I let him reach the window, through which he tried to crawl﻿—and I permitted him to get so far that he thought he was about to escape and then I seized him again and dragged him back to the floor and lifting him to his feet I made him fight.
I struck him lightly in the face many times and then I laid him on his back across the table and kneeling on his chest I spoke to him, softly.
“You had my friend, old Samuels, murdered and my father, too, and now you come to befoul my mother. What did you expect, swine, but this? Have you no intelligence? You must have known that I would kill you﻿—speak!”
“They said that they would get you today,” he whimpered. “They lied to me. They went back on me. They told me that you would be in the pen at the barracks before noon. Damn them, they lied to me!”
So! that was how it was, eh? And the lucky circumstance of the strayed goat had saved me to avenge my father and succor my mother; but they would come yet. I must hurry or they might come before I was through, and so I took his head between my hands and bent his neck far back over the edge of the table until I heard his spine part﻿—and that was the end of the vilest traitor who ever lived﻿—one who professed friendship openly and secretly conspired to ruin us. In broad daylight I carried his body to the river and threw it in. I was past caring what they knew.

===Chinese===

他起初想用一只手抵住我，另一只手去抽刀；可我一拳打在他脸上，把他打飞出去，摔到屋子那一头。他霎时爬起来，鼻口淌着血，提着刀又扑上来，乱砍乱劈。我又一拳把他打倒；他再爬起再扑，我便攫住他持刀的手，把刀夺了下来。他同我对阵没有半分胜算，他很快也看出来了，因为他开始后退讨饶。
“杀了他，朱利安，”母亲说，“杀了这个害你父亲的凶手。”
其实用不着她这句话来推动我；一看见彼得在那里，我就知道我久候的杀他的时辰到了。他这时哭了起来——大颗大颗的泪滚下面颊——夺门想逃。猫耍老鼠，我便这样耍他。
我拦在门前，抓起他整个人，横掼过屋子；随后放他到窗前——他试图从窗里爬出去——我容他爬到自以为就要脱身的地步，再一把揪回来掼在地上，拎他起来，逼他打。
我在他脸上轻轻打了许多拳；随后把他仰面横按在桌上，跪上他的胸口，柔声对他说话。
“你害死了我的朋友老萨缪尔斯，又害死我的父亲，如今还来糟蹋我的母亲。你指望什么，猪猡？你长了脑子没有？你该知道我会杀你——说！”
“他们说今天会来抓你，”他呜咽，“他们骗了我。他们背了我。他们说晌午前你就进兵营的栅栏了。该死的，他们骗了我！”
原来如此，是么？走失的那只羊这桩侥幸，救下来好叫我为父报仇、救母于难；可他们还会来。我得赶紧，免得他们赶在我办完之前来。于是我双手捧住他的头，把他的颈子朝桌沿外远远折过去，直到听见脊柱断开——这就是那个天下最卑劣的叛徒的下场——当面向你称友，背地里布局毁你的人。光天化日之下，我把他的尸首背到河边，丢了进去。他们知道不知道，我已经不在乎了。

===Original===

They were coming for me and they would have their way with me whether they had any pretext or not; but they would have to pay a price for me, that I determined, and I got my knife and strapped it in its scabbard about my waist beneath my shirt; but they did not come﻿—they had lied to Peter just as they lie to everyone.
The next day was market day and tax day, so I went to market with the necessary goats and produce to make my trades and pay my taxes. As Soor passed around the market place making his collections, or rather his levies, for we had to deliver the stuff to his place ourselves, I saw from the excited conversation of those in his wake that he was spreading alarm and consternation among the people of the commune.
I wondered what it might all be about, nor had I long to wait to discover, for he soon reached me. He could neither read nor write; but he had a form furnished by the government upon which were numbers that the agents were taught how to read and which stood for various classes of produce, livestock and manufactures. In columns beneath these numbers he made marks during the month for the amounts of my trades in each item﻿—it was all crude, of course, and inaccurate; but as they always overcharged us and then added something to make up for any errors they might have made to our credit, the government was satisfied, even if we were not.
Being able to read and write, as well as to figure, I always knew to a dot just what was due from me in tax and I always had an argument with Soor, from which Government emerged victorious every time.
This month I should have owed him one goat, but he demanded three.
“How is that?” I asked.
“Under the old rate you owed me the equivalent of a goat and a half; but since the tax had been doubled under the new law you owe me three goats.” Then it was I knew the cause of the excitement in other parts of the market place.
“How do you expect us to live if you take everything from us?” I asked.
“The government does not care whether you live or not,” he replied, “as long as you pay taxes while you do live.”
“I will pay the three goats,” I said, “because I have to; but next market day I will bring you a present of the hardest cheese I can find.”
He did not say anything, for he was afraid of me unless he was surrounded by Kash Guards; but he looked ugly.

===Chinese===

他们要来拿我，有没有由头都会下手；可他们得为我付一个价钱，这个我拿定了主意。我取来刀，连鞘缚在腰上，掩在衬衫底下；可他们没有来——他们骗了彼得，正如他们骗每一个人。
第二天是集市日，也是缴税日，我便带着该带的羊和货去赶集、缴税。苏尔在集市里挨个收缴——说征收不如说勒索，因为货还得我们自己送到他那里——我看他身后那串人激动的议论，便知他正往公社的人心里撒惊惶。
我不知道出了什么事，也没等多久就知道了，因为他很快到了我跟前。他不识字也不会写；可他有一张政府发的表格，上面印着数码，税吏都学过怎样认，各个数码代表各类农产品、牲畜和制造品。一个月里我各项交易了多少，他就当月在数码底下一项项划记号——当然又粗又糙，又不准；可他们回回多收，再添上点什么，补上可能错在我们头上的账，政府便满意了，哪怕我们不满意。
我既识字又会算，我该缴的税总是一清二楚，于是每回总要同苏尔理论一场，而每回都是政府大获全胜。
这个月我本该缴他一只羊，他开口要三只。
“这怎么讲？”我问。
“按旧税率你欠我一只羊带一半；新法把税翻了一番，你就欠我三只。”这时我才明白集市别处那阵骚动的缘故。
“你们把什么都拿走，指望我们怎么活？”我问。
“你活不活，政府不管，”他答，“只要你活着，就得缴税。”
“这三只羊我缴，”我说，“因为不得不缴；可下个集市日，我送你一件礼——我能寻见的最硬的一块奶酪。”
他没言语，因为没有卡什卫兵围着，他就怕我；可他的脸色难看得很。

===Original===

The commander of the Kash Guard company must have noticed the crowd around us, for he rode straight toward me, alone. I would not give him the satisfaction of thinking that I feared him and so I stood there waiting.
The officer reined in before me.
“What are you doing here?” he barked.
“Minding my own business, as you had better do,” I replied.
“You swine are becoming insufferable,” he cried. “Get to your pen, where you belong﻿—I will stand for no mobs and no insolence.”
I just stood there looking at him; but there was murder in my heart. He loosened the bull-hide whip that hung at the pommel of his saddle.
“You have to be driven, do you?” He was livid with sudden anger and his voice almost a scream. Then he struck at me﻿—a vicious blow﻿—with the heavy whip﻿—struck at my face. I dodged the lash and seized it, wrenching it from his puny grasp and then I caught his bridle and though his horse plunged and fought, I lashed the rider with all my strength a dozen times, before he tumbled from the saddle to the trampled earth of the market place.
Then his men were upon me and I went down from a blow on the head. They bound my hands while I was unconscious and then hustled me roughly into a saddle. I was half dazed during the awful ride that ensued﻿—we rode to the military prison at the barracks and all the way that fiend of a captain rode beside me and lashed me with his bull-hide whip.

===Chinese===

卡什卫队那名连指挥官想必注意到了围着我们的人群，因为他单人独骑径直朝我而来。我不肯叫他称心当我怕他，便站在原地等着。
那军官在我面前勒住马。
“你在这里干什么？”他咄咄地喝。
“管我自己的事，你也最好管你自己的。”我答。
“你们这帮猪猡越来越无法无天了，”他喊，“滚回你的栏里去——聚众放肆，我不容。”
我只是站在那里看他；心里装着杀机。他解开挂在鞍头上的公牛皮鞭。
“非得赶着才走，是么？”他气得发青，嗓音几乎是尖叫。随后他抽我——狠狠一鞭，那条重鞭，抽向我的脸。我闪过鞭梢，一把攥住，从他那只没用的手里夺了过来；随即抓住他的笼头，任他的马怎样腾挪扑跳，我抡圆了朝马上那人抽了十几鞭，把他抽下马鞍，掼在集市被踏烂的泥地上。
随后他的兵扑到我身上，我头上挨了一记，倒了。我昏过去时他们绑了我的手，又把我粗暴地推上马鞍。其后那趟骇人的骑行我半昏半醒——我们驰向兵营的军事监狱，一路上那个魔鬼船长始终傍着我，用他的公牛皮鞭一路抽我。

## IX Revolution / 九 造反
===Original===

Then they threw me into the pen where the prisoners were kept, and after they had left I was surrounded by the other unfortunates incarcerated there. When they learned what I had done they shook their heads and sighed. It would be all over with me in the morning, they said﻿—nothing less than The Butcher for such an offense as mine.
I lay upon the hard ground, bruised and sore, thinking not of my future but of what was to befall Juana and Mother if I too, were taken from them, and the thought gave me new strength and made me forget my hurts, for my mind was busy with plans, mostly impossible plans, for escape﻿—and vengeance. Vengeance was often uppermost in my mind.
Above my head at intervals, I heard the pacing of the sentry upon the roof. I could tell, of course, each time that he passed and the direction in which he was going. It required about five minutes for him to pass above me, reach the end of his post and return﻿—that was when he went west. Going east he took but a trifle over two minutes. Therefore, when he passed me going west his back was toward me for about two and a half minutes; but when he went east it was only for about a minute that his face was turned from the spot where I lay.
Of course he could not see me while I lay beneath the shed; but my plan﻿—the one I finally decided upon﻿—did not include remaining in the shed. I had evolved several subtle schemes for escape; but finally cast them all aside and chose, instead, the boldest that occurred to me. I knew that at best the chances were small that I could succeed in any plan and therefore the boldest seemed as likely as any other and it at least had the advantage of speedy results﻿—I would be free or I would be dead in a few brief moments after I essayed it.
I waited therefore, until the other prisoners had quieted down and comparative silence in the direction of the barracks and the parade assured me that there were few abroad. The sentry came and went and came again upon his monotonous round. Now he was coming toward me from the east and I was ready, standing just outside the shed beneath the low eaves which I could reach by jumping. I heard him pass and gave him a full minute to gain the distance I thought necessary to drown the sounds of my attempt from his ears and then I leaped for the eaves, caught with my fingers and drew myself quickly to the roof.

===Chinese===

随后他们把我攒进关押囚犯的栅栏里；他们一走，同被关在那里的其他不幸者便围了上来。听说我干的事，他们摇头叹气。明天我就完了，他们说——我这样的罪，少说也是屠夫伺候。
我躺在硬地上，一身青肿疼痛，想的不是自己的将来，而是倘若我也被夺走，落到胡安娜和母亲头上的是什么；这个念头给了我新的力气，叫我把伤痛都忘了，因为我的脑子正忙着盘算计划——多半是不可能实行的计划——为的是逃脱，和报仇。报仇二字常常压在我心上最上面。
头顶上，我隔一阵便听见哨兵在屋顶上来回的脚步。当然，他每次走过、往哪个方向走，我分得清清楚楚。他从我头顶走到岗位一头再折回，要五分钟光景——那是往西走。往东走，只消两分钟多一点。因此他往西经过我时，背对heits约两分半钟；往东时，脸离开我躺的地方，只有约莫一分钟。

===Original===

I thought that I did it very quietly, but the fellow must have had the ears of a hellhound, for no more had I drawn my feet beneath me for the quick run across the roof than a challenge rang out from the direction of the sentry and almost simultaneously the report of a rifle.
Instantly all was pandemonium. Guards ran, shouting, from all directions, lights flashed in the barracks, rifles spoke from either side of me and from behind me, while from below rose the dismal howlings of the prisoners. It seemed then that a hundred men had known of my plan and been lying in wait for me; but I was launched upon it and even though I had regretted it, there was nothing to do but carry it through to whatever was its allotted end.
It seemed a miracle that none of the bullets struck me; but of course, it was dark and I was moving rapidly. It takes seconds to tell about it, but it required less than a second for me to dash across the roof and leap to the open ground beyond the prison pen. I saw lights moving west of me, and so I ran east toward the lake and presently the firing ceased as they lost sight of me, though I could hear sounds of pursuit. Nevertheless, I felt that I had succeeded and was congratulating myself upon the ease with which I had accomplished the seemingly impossible when there suddenly rose before me out of the black night the figure of a huge soldier pointing a rifle point blank at me. He issued no challenge nor asked any question﻿—just pulled the trigger. I could hear the hammer strike the firing pin, but there was no explosion. I did not know what the reason was, nor did I ever know. All that was apparent was that the rifle missed fire and then he brought his bayonet into play while I was springing toward him.
Foolish man! But then he did not know that it was Julian 9th he faced. Pitifully, futilely he thrust at me and with one hand I seized the rifle and tore it from his grasp. In the same movement I swung it behind me and above my head, bringing it down with all the strength of one arm upon his thick skull. Like a felled ox he tumbled to his knees and then sprawled forward upon his face﻿—his head crushed to a pulp. He never knew how he died.
Behind me I heard them coming closer and they must have seen me, for they opened fire again and I heard the beat of horses’ hoofs upon my right and left.

===Chinese===

我自以为做得悄无声息，可那家伙的耳朵想必寒得过地狱犬：我刚把脚收拢到身下、准备飞跑着冲过屋顶，哨位那边便响起一声喝问，几乎同时是步枪的枪响。
霎时满营大乱。卫兵们从四面八方喊着跑来，兵营里灯火乱闪，步枪在我左右、在我身后纷纷开口，栅栏底下则腾起囚犯们凄惨的嚎叫。那光景倒像一百个人早知道我的计划，正埋伏着等我；可我既已出手，纵然后悔，也只有把它做到分派给它的那个尽头，不论是什么。
一颗子弹都没碰着我，算得一个奇迹；当然，天黑，我又快。这一切讲起来要几秒钟，当时我冲过屋顶、跃过监栏外的空地，用的却不到一秒。我望见西边有灯火移动，便朝东、朝湖跑；不多时他们看不见我了，枪声停了，虽听得见追兵的动静。我自觉已经得手，正暗自庆幸这桩看似不可能的事办得如此顺手，黑夜里忽然在我面前立起一条大兵的身影，一支步枪平指着我的胸口。他不喝问，也不发话——只扣扳机。我听得击锤砸上撞针，却没有炸响。缘故是什么，我不知道，往后也没有知道。只看得见枪哑了火；而正当我朝他扑去时，他挺起了刺刀。
蠢人！他不知道对面站的是朱利安九世罢了。他可怜地、徒劳地朝我乱搠，我一只手攥住枪，从他手里夺了下来；同一动作里把枪抡到脑后、举过头顶，单臂全力劈在他那厚脑壳上。他像放倒的牛，跪了下去，随即脸朝前扑倒——脑袋砸成了浆。他到死也不知道自己是怎么死的。
背后追兵近了，他们必定望见了我，因为又开了火；我听见左右两边都响起马蹄。

===Original===

They were surrounding me upon three sides and upon the fourth was the great lake. A moment later I was standing upon the edge of the ancient breakwater while behind me rose the triumphant cries of my pursuers. They had seen me and they knew that I was theirs.
At least, they thought they knew so. I did not wait for them to come closer; but raising my hands above me I dove head foremost into the cool waters of the lake, and swimming rapidly beneath the surface I kept close in the shadows and headed north. I had spent much of my summer life in the water of the river so that I was as much at home in that liquid element as in air; but this of course, the Kash Guard did not know, for even had they known that Julian 9th could swim they could not at that time have known which prisoner it was who had escaped and so I think they must have thought what I wanted them to think﻿—that I had chosen self-drowning to recapture.
However I was sure they would search the shore in both directions and so I kept to the water after I came to the surface and when I was sure that no one was directly above me I swam farther out until I felt there was little danger of being seen from shore, for it was a dark night. And thus I swam on until I thought I was opposite the mouth of the river, when I turned toward the west, searching for it. Luck was with me. I swam directly into it and a short distance up the sluggish stream before I knew that I was out of the lake; but even then I did not take to the shore, preferring to pass the heart of the ancient city before trusting myself to land.
At last I came out upon the north bank of the river, which is farthest from the Kash Guard barracks and made my way as swiftly as possible up stream in the direction of my home. Here, hours later, I found an anxious Juana awaiting me, for already she had heard what had transpired in the market place. I had made my plans and had soon explained them to Juana and Mother. There was nothing for them but to acquiesce, as only death could be our lot if we remained in our homes another day. I was astonished even, that they had not already fallen upon Juana and Mother. As it was, they might come any minute﻿—there was no time to lose.
Hastily wrapping up a few belongings I took The Flag from its hiding place above the mantel and tucked it in my shirt﻿—then we were ready.

===Chinese===

他们三面围我，第四面是大湖。片刻后我已立在古防波堤的边上，身后腾起追兵得意的喊叫。他们看见我了，知道我跑不脱了。
至少，他们自以为知道。我不等他们逼近，双手高举，一头扎进湖里清凉的水中，在水底疾潜，贴着暗影，一路朝北。我夏天的光阴多耗在河里，在这流动的原素里同在空气里一样自在；可这一点卡什卫队不知道——即便他们晓得朱利安九世会水，那时也无从知道逃走的是哪个囚犯。于是我想，他们想到的必定是我要他们想的：我宁可自沉，不肯再被拿。
不过我确信他们会沿岸两头搜，所以浮上来之后仍不离水；确准没有人正在头顶时，又往外游，直到我觉得离岸已远、被望见的危险很小——那是个黑夜。就这样一路游，游到我估量正对着河口，才转向西，摸索河口。运气照应我：我一路游进了它，在迟缓的水流里向上游了一段，才知道已经出了湖；即便那时也不上岸，宁可先越过古城的心脏再托身于陆地。
末了我在河北岸上岸——离卡什卫队兵营最远的一岸——尽快朝上游、朝家的方向赶。几个钟头后到家，焦急的胡安娜正候着我，集市上的事她已听说了。我的计划早已成竹，很快讲给胡安娜和母亲。她们别无选择，只能依我：再在家里待一天，等着我们的就只有死。我甚至诧异他们竟还没有扑到胡安娜和母亲身上。说到底，他们随时会来——一刻也耽搁不得。
匆匆包起几件随身之物，我从炉台上方的藏处取出旗帜，塞进衬衫里——这就齐了。

===Original===

Going to the pens we caught up Red Lightning and the two mares and three of my best milk goats. These latter we tied, and after Juana and Mother had mounted the mares I laid one goat in front of each across a mare’s withers and the third before myself upon Red Lightning, who did not relish the strange burden and gave me considerable trouble at first.
We rode out upriver, leaving the pens open that the goats might scatter and possibly cover our trail until we could turn off the dusty path beyond Jim’s house. We dared not stop to bid Jim and Mollie goodbye, lest we be apprehended there by our enemies and bring trouble to our good friends. It was a sad occasion for poor Mother, leaving thus her home and those dear neighbors who had been as close to her as her own people; but she was as brave as Juana, nor once did either of them attempt to dissuade me from the wild scheme I had outlined to them. Instead they encouraged me and Juana laid her hand upon my arm as I rode beside her, saying: “I would rather that you died thus than that we lived on as downtrodden serfs, without happiness and without hope.”
“I shall not die,” I said, “until my work is done at least, and then if die I must, I shall be content to know that I leave a happier country for my fellow men to live in.”
“Amen!” whispered Juana.
That night I hid them in the ruins of the old church which we found had been partially burned by the Kalkars. For a moment I held them in my arms﻿—my mother and my wife﻿—and then I left them to ride toward the southwest and the coal mines. The mines lie about fifty miles away﻿—those to which our people are sent﻿—and west of south, according to what I had heard. I had never been to them; but I knew that I must find the bed of an ancient canal and follow it through the district of Joliet and between fifteen and twenty miles beyond, where I must turn south, and after passing a large lake, I would presently come to the mines. I rode the balance of the night and into the morning until I commenced to see people astir in the thinly populated country through which I passed. Then I hid in a wood through which a stream wound and here found pasture for Red Lightning and rest for myself. I had brought no food, leaving what little bread and cheese we had brought from the house for Mother and Juana.

===Chinese===

到栏里牵出红闪电、两匹骒马和我三只最好的奶羊。羊捆好，胡安娜同母亲骑上骒马，我把一只羊横放在她们每人面前的马鞍前桥上，第三只搁在我自己身前的红闪电背上——它可不领这份陌生驮带的情，起初着实给我添了些麻烦。
我们沿河上行出发，栏门敞着，好教羊群四散，或许能遮一遮我们的行迹，直到我们过了吉姆家，拐下那条尘土小路。我们不敢停下同吉姆莫莉道别，惟恐在那里被敌人撞见，连累我们的好朋友。对可怜的母亲，这是个伤心的时刻：就这样撇下她的家，撇下那些亲如骨肉的邻人；可她同胡安娜一样勇敢，两人谁也没有试图劝阻我讲给她们的那个疯狂方案。她们反而给我打气；我骑在胡安娜旁边时，她把手搭上我的手臂，说：“我宁可你这样死，也不愿我们苟活下去做受践踏的农奴，没有幸福，没有指望。”
“我不会死，”我说，“至少活到大功告成；那时倘若非死不可，我也可以安然闭眼，知道自己留下了一个好些的国家给同胞们住。”
“阿门！”胡安娜低声道。
当夜我把她们藏进一座古教堂的废墟——我们发现它已被卡尔卡人烧过一部分。我把她们搂在怀里抱了片刻——我的母亲和我的妻——随后撇下她们，朝西南、朝煤矿驰去。矿离城约五十哩——我们的人被送往的那些——方位是南偏西，据我听来的说法。我从没去过；可我知道必须找到一条古运河的河床，沿它穿过乔利埃特区，再往前十五到二十哩，然后折向南，过了一座大湖，不久就到矿山。我骑完那夜余下的路，一直骑到清早，开始望见所过人烟稀少之地有人起身走动，便躲进一片有溪流婉蜒的林子，给红闪电找了草，也给自己找了休息。我没有带吃的——从家里带出的那点面包和奶酪，都留给母亲和胡安娜了。

===Original===

I did not expect to be gone over a week and I knew that with goat’s milk and what they had on hand in addition to what they could find growing wild, there would be no danger of starvation before I returned﻿—after which we expected to live in peace and plenty for the rest of our days.
My journey was less eventful than I had anticipated. I passed through a few ruined villages and towns of greater or less antiquity, the largest of which was ancient Joliet, which was abandoned during the plague of fifty years ago, the teivos headquarters and station being removed directly west a few miles to the banks of a little river. Much of the territory I traversed was covered with thick woods, though here and there were the remnants of clearings that must once have been farms which were not yet entirely reclaimed by nature. Now and again I passed those gaunt and lonely towers in which the ancients stored the winter feed for their stock. Those that have endured were of concrete, and some showed but little the ravages of time, other than the dense vines that often covered them from base to capital, while several were in the midst of thick forests with old trees almost entwining them, so quickly does nature reclaim her own when man has been displaced.
After I passed Joliet I had to make inquiries, and this I did boldly of the few men I saw laboring in the tiny fields scattered along my way. They were poor clods, these descendants of ancient America’s rich and powerful farming class﻿—those people of olden times whose selfishness had sought to throw the burden of taxation upon the city dwellers where the ignorant foreign classes were most numerous and had thus added their bit to fomenting the discontent that had worked the downfall of a glorious nation. They themselves suffered much before they died, but nothing by comparison with the humiliation and degradation of their descendants﻿—an illiterate, degraded, starving race.
Early in the second morning I came within sight of the stockade about the mines. Even at a distance I could see that it was a weak, dilapidated thing and that the sentries pacing along its top were all that held the prisoners within.

===Chinese===

我预计离家不过一个礼拜，也知道凭羊奶加上手头现有的、再添些野生的出产，我回来之前饿不着人——回来之后，我们指望下半辈子太平安足地过。
我这一路比预想的平淡。我穿过几座或古或新的残破村镇，最大的是古城乔利埃特——五十年前那场瘟疫里弃掉的，特沃斯总部和哨站西移了几哩，挪到一条小河的岸边。我经过的地界大半盖着密林，不过这里那里还留着些开垦地的残迹，想必从前的农场，如今尚未被大自然完全收回。时不时我路过那些瘦削孤零零的塔，古人在里面存放牲口的冬饲料。存留至今的都是混凝土的，有几座除了常春藤从脚缠到顶，几乎不见岁月的剥蚀；另有几座深陷密林中央，老树几乎把它们搂在怀里——人一退场，大自然收回自己的地盘，就是这样快。
过了乔利埃特我便得问路了；我大大方方地问那零星散在路上小田里做活的人。这些古代美洲富强农户的后裔，如今是可怜的土疙瘩——当年正是那号人的自私，要把捐税的重担推到城里人头上，因为城里的无知外来种最多；他们就这样给一个光荣民族的倾颓添了自己那一把柴。他们死前也受了些苦，但同他们子孙所受的作践和糟蹋一比，不算什么——一个不识字、被践踏、挨饿的种族。
第二个清晨将尽时，我望见了矿山周围的栅栏。隔着老远我也看得出那是个单薄、破烂的东西；沿着栅顶来回踱步的哨兵，就是拦住囚犯的全部力量。

===Original===

As a matter of fact, many escaped; but they were soon hunted down and killed as the farmers in the neighborhood always informed on them, since the commandant at the prison had conceived the fiendish plan of slaying one farmer for every prisoner who escaped and was not recaught.
I hid until night and then cautiously I approached the stockade, leaving Red Lightning securely tied in the woods. It was no trick to reach the stockade, so thoroughly was I hidden by the rank vegetation growing upon the outside. From a place of concealment I watched the sentry, a big fellow, but apparently a dull clod who walked with his chin upon his breast and with the appearance of being half asleep.
The stockade was not high and the whole construction was similar to that of the prison pen at Chicago, evidently having been designed by the same commandant in years gone by. I could hear the prisoners conversing in the shed beyond the wall and presently, when one came near to where I listened, I tried to attract his attention by making a hissing sound.
After what seemed a long time to me, he heard me; but even then it was some time before he appeared to grasp the idea that someone was trying to attract his attention. When he did he moved closer and tried to peer through one of the cracks; but as it was dark outside he could see nothing.
“Are you a Yank?” I asked. “If you are, I am a friend.”
“I am a Yank,” he replied. “Did you expect to find a Kalkar working in the mines?”
“Do you know a prisoner called Julian 8th?” I inquired.
He seemed to be thinking for a moment and then he said: “I seem to have heard the name. What do you want of him?”
“I want to speak to him﻿—I am his son.”
“Wait!” he whispered. “I think that I heard a man speak that name today. I will find out﻿—he is near by.”
I waited for perhaps ten minutes when I heard someone approaching from the inside and presently a voice asked if I was still there.
“Yes,” I said; “is that you, Father?” for I thought that the tones were his.
“Julian, my son!” came to me almost as a sob. “What are you doing here?”
Briefly I told him and then of my plan. “Have the convicts the courage to attempt it?” I asked in conclusion.
“I do not know,” he said, and I could not but note the tone of utter hopelessness in his voice. “They would wish to; but here our spirits and our bodies both are broken.

===Chinese===

其实逃出去的不少；可他们很快就被追猎杀死，因为邻近的农户回回告发他们——监狱指挥官想出了一条恶毒章程：逃一个抓不回，就杀一个农户抵数。
我藏到天黑，才小心摸向栅栏，红闪电牢牢拴在林子里。摸到栅栏底下毫不费事，外面的荒草长得那样密，把我遮得严实。我从藏身处观察哨兵：一个大块头，却显然是个迟钝的夯货，下巴抵着胸口走路，一副半睡的样子。
栅栏不高，整个构造同芝加哥的监栏一个路数， evidently 多年前出自同一位指挥官的手。我听得见墙那边棚屋里囚犯们说话；不多时有人凑近我窃听的地方，我便发出咝咝声引他注意。
在我像等了一个世纪之后，他听见了我；可又过了好一会他才明白有人在引他注意。明白之后他凑近了，想从板缝里张望；可外面黑，什么也看不见。
“你是美国佬么？”我问，“若是，我是朋友。”
“我是美国佬，”他答，“难道你指望在矿里见到卡尔卡人？”
“你认得一个叫朱利安八世的囚犯么？”我问。
他想了片刻，随后说：“这名字仿佛听见过。你找他何事？”
“我有话对他讲——我是他的儿子。”
“等着！”他耳语，“我记得今天有人说起这名字。我去问问——他就在近旁。”
约莫等了十分钟，听见里面有人过来；随即一个声音问我还在不在。
“在，”我说，“是您么，父亲？”那嗓音听着像他。
“朱利安，我的儿子！”传来的话几乎是一声呜咽。“你在这里做什么？”
我简单讲了经过，又讲了我的方案。“囚犯们可有胆子一试？”末了我问。
“不知道，”他说，话音里那种全然的绝望我不能不察觉，“他们愿意；可在这里，我们的精神和身子都断啦。

===Original===

I do not know how many would have the courage to attempt it. Wait and I will talk with some of them﻿—all are loyal; but just weak from overwork, starvation and abuse.”
I waited for the better part of an hour before he returned. “Some will help,” he said, “from the first, and others if we are successful. Do you think it worth the risk﻿—they will kill you if you fail﻿—they will kill us all.”
“And what is death to that which you are suffering?” I asked.
“I know,” he said; “but the worm impaled upon the hook still struggles and hopes for life. Turn back, my son, we can do nothing against them.”
“I shall not turn back,” I whispered. “I shall not turn back.”
“I will help you; but I cannot speak for the others.”
We had spoken only when the sentry had been at a distance, falling into silence each time he approached the point where we stood. In the intervals of silence I could hear the growing restlessness of the prisoners and I guessed that what I had said to the first man was being passed around from mouth to mouth within until already the whole adjacent shed was seething with something akin to excitement. I wondered if it would arouse their spirit sufficiently to carry them through the next ten minutes. If it did, success was assured.
Father had told me all that I wanted to know﻿—the location of the guard house and the barracks and the number of Kash Guard posted here﻿—only fifty men to guard five thousand! How much more eloquently than words did this fact bespeak the humiliation of the American people and the utter contempt in which our scurvy masters held us﻿—fifty men to guard five thousand!
And then I started putting my plan into execution﻿—a mad plan which had only its madness to recommend it. The sentry approached and came opposite where I stood, and I leaped for the eaves as I had leaped for the eaves of the prison pen at Chicago, only this time I leaped from the outside where the eaves are closer to the ground and so the task was easier. I leaped for them and caught them, and then I scrambled up behind the sentry and before his dull wits told him that there was someone behind him I was upon his back and the same fingers that threw a mad bull closed upon his wind pipe. The struggle was brief﻿—he died quickly and I lowered him to the roof.

===Chinese===

不知还有几个有胆一试。等等，我去同几个人说说——人心都是忠的；只是叫苦役、饥饿和毒打磨弱了。”
我等了大半个钟头他才回来。“有几个肯从一开头就帮忙，”他说，“事成之后，跟上的会更多。你认为值得冒这个险么——事败他们杀你——把我们全杀光。”
“你们受的这个，比死又如何？”我问。
“我知道，”他说，“可上了钩的虫，也还要挣一挣，也还指望活。回头吧，我的儿子，我们斗不过他们。”
“我不回头，”我低声说，“我不回头。”
“我帮你；可别人，我替他们应不了。”
我们只在哨兵走远时说话，他一走近我们立足的地方便住声。静默的间隙里，我听得见囚犯们愈来愈按捺不住的騷动，猜到我同第一个人说的话正在里面口口相传，此刻整座邻近的棚屋已经沸腾着近乎激昂的东西了。我不知道这能不能把他们的魂气提到足以撑过下一个十分钟。若能，事必成。
父亲把我想知道的都告诉了我——卫兵房和兵营的位置、此地卡什卫兵的数目——五十个人看五千！这一桩事实，比任何言语都更雄辩地说出美国人的耻辱，说出我们那些龌龊的主子对我们的全然轻蔑——五十个人看五千！
随后我开始付诸实行——一个疯狂的方案，可取之处只有它的疯狂。哨兵走近，走到我站的地方对面；我朝屋檐跃去，同在芝加哥监栏那里一样——只是这次从外头跳，外头的檐离地近些，也就容易些。我跃上去抓住了，随即爬到哨兵背后；不等他那迟钝的脑筋告诉他身后有人，我已经骑上他的背，那双攒翻过疯牛的手指，攥上了他的喉管。挣扎很短——他死得很快，我把他从屋顶上放了下来。

===Original===

Then I took his uniform from him and donned it, with his ammunition belt, and I took his bayonetted rifle and started out upon his post, walking with slow tread and with my chin upon my breast as he had walked.
At the end of my post I waited for the sentry I saw coming upon the next and when he was close to me I turned back and he turned back away from me and then I wheeled and struck him an awful blow upon the head with my rifle. He died more quickly than the other﻿—instantly, I should say.
I took his rifle and ammunition from him and lowered them inside the pen to waiting hands, and then I went on to the next sentry and the next, until I had slain five more and passed their rifles to the prisoners below and while I was doing this, five prisoners who had volunteered to Father climbed to the roof of the shed and stripped the dead men of their uniforms and donned them.
It was all done quietly and in the black night none might see what was going on fifty feet away. I had to stop when I came near to the guard house. There I turned back and presently slid into the pen with my accomplices who had been going among the other prisoners with Father, arousing them to mutiny. Now were most of them ready to follow me, for so far my plan had proven successful. With equal quietness we overcame the men at the guard house and then moved on in a silent body toward the barracks.
So sudden and so unexpected was our attack that we met with little resistance and we were almost five thousand to forty now. We swarmed in upon them like wild bees upon a foe and we shot them and bayonetted them until none remained alive. Not one escaped. And now we were flushed with success so that the most spiritless became a veritable lion for courage.
We who had taken the uniforms of the Kash Guard discarded them for our own garb as we had no mind to go abroad in the hated livery of our oppressors. That very night we saddled their horses with the fifty saddles that were there and fifty men rode the balance of the horses bareback﻿—that made one hundred mounted men and the others were to follow on foot﻿—on to Chicago. On to Chicago, was our first slogan.
We traveled cautiously, though I had difficulty in making them do so, so intoxicated were they with their first success.

===Chinese===

随后我剥下他的制服穿上，连子弹带；提着他上了刺刀的步枪，踏上他的哨位，学他的样子，脚步沉沉，下巴抵着胸口走。
走到哨位尽头，我等那下一个哨位上来的哨兵；他一挨近，我转身往回走，他也背对我转回去；我便旋身，用步枪朝他头上狠狠一击。他比前一个死得更快——应该说，即刻就死了。
我取下他的枪和弹药，吊进栅栏里等着的手中；随后奔下一个哨兵，再下一个，直到又杀了五个，把他们的枪都递给下面的囚犯。我办这些事的时候，五个自愿追随父亲的囚犯爬上棚顶，剥下死人的制服穿上。
一切都在悄然中进行，黑夜里五十步外便无人看得见发生了什么。临近卫兵房时我停了手，转身折回，随即滑进栅栏，同我的同伙会齐——他们正跟着父亲在囚犯中间奔走，煽动他们哗变。此刻大多数已愿意跟我，因为到目前为止，方案步步得手。我们又同样悄无声息地收拾了卫兵房里的人，随后一队人影默默压向兵营。
我们的袭击那样突然、那样出人意料，几乎没有遇到抵抗；此刻我们几乎是五千对四十。我们像野蜂扑向仇家一样涌进去，枪杀他们，捅他们，直到一个不剩。没有一个逃脱。此刻我们被成功烧得浑身发热，最没血性的也成了一头真狮子。
夺了卡什卫队制服的那些人把制服脱下，换回自己的衣裳——我们不愿穿着压迫者的号衣招摇。当夜我们就用那里的五十副鞍子给马备鞍，五十人骑余下的马裸鞍——凑成一百骑，其余的徒步跟上——向芝加哥。向芝加哥，是我们的第一句口号。
我们赶路很谨慎——虽说逼他们谨慎费了我不少劲，他们叫头一场胜利灌得醉醺醺的。

===Original===

I wanted to save the horses and also I wanted to get as many men into Chicago as possible, so we let the weakest ride, though I had a time of it getting Red Lightning to permit another on his sleek back.
Some fell out upon the way, from exhaustion or from fear, for the nearer Chicago we approached the more their courage ebbed. The very thought of the feared Kalkars and their Kash Guards took the marrow from the hearts of many. I do not know that one may blame them, for the spirit of man can endure only so much and when it is broken only a miracle can mend it in the same generation.
We reached the ruined church a week from the day I left Mother and Juana there and we reached it with less than two thousand men, so rapid had been the desertions in the last few miles before we entered the district.
Father and I could scarcely wait to see our loved ones and so we rode on ahead to greet them, and inside the church we found three dead goats and a dying woman﻿—my mother with a knife protruding from her breast. She was still conscious when we entered and I saw a great light of happiness in her eyes as they fell upon Father and upon me. I looked around for Juana and my heart stood still, fearing that I would not find her﻿—and fearing that I would.
Mother could still speak, and as we leaned over her as Father held her in his arms she breathed a faint story of what had befallen them. They had lived in peace until that very day when the Kash Guard had stumbled upon them﻿—a large detachment under Or-tis, himself. They had seized them to take them away; but Mother had had a knife hidden in her clothing and had utilized it, as we saw, rather than suffer the fate she knew awaited them. That was all, except that Juana had had no knife and Or-tis had carried her off.
I saw Mother die then, in Father’s arms, and I helped him bury her after our men came and we had shown them what the beasts had done, though they knew well enough and had suffered themselves enough to know what was to be expected of the swine.

===Chinese===

我要省马，也要尽可能多带人进芝加哥，便叫最弱的骑马——虽说劝红闪电容另一个人上它那油亮的背，也费了我一番劲。
一路上有人掉队，有累垮的，有吓掉的：离芝加哥越近，他们的胆气越往下泄。单是想起可怕的卡尔卡人和他们的卡什卫队，就抽走了许多人骨头缝里的髓。这也不便深怪：人的魂气受得了的只有那么多，一经折断，除非有奇迹，同代人里接不回来。
从我把母亲和胡安娜留在那里那天起，一个礼拜后，我们到了那座残破的教堂，到时不足两千人——进本区前最后几里，开小差的快极了。
父亲同我等不得要见亲人，抢先骑行去迎；教堂里面，我们找到的是三只死羊和一个奄奄一息的女人——我的母亲，胸口插着一把刀。我们进去时她还有知觉；目光落到父亲和我身上时，她眼里亮起一大片幸福的光。我四下找胡安娜，心停了跳：既怕找不见她——又怕找见。
母亲还能说话；父亲把她搂在怀里，我们俯在她身上，她用一丝微弱的气讲了落到她们头上的事。她们一直太平安稳，直到当天，卡什卫队撞见了她们——奥提斯亲领的一大队。他们下手要掳走她们；母亲衣裳里藏着一把刀，用了它，正如我们看见的，不肯去受她明知等着她们的命运。就这些——只除了：胡安娜没有刀，被奥提斯掳走了。
我看着母亲死在父亲怀里；我们的人到后，我帮着他葬了她，并把畜生的作为指给他们看——其实他们清楚得很，受过的罪也尽够他们知道，这帮猪猡能干出什么。

## X The Butcher / 十 屠夫
===Original===

We went on then, Father and I filled with grief and bitterness and hatred even greater than we had known before. We marched toward the market place of our district, and on the way we stopped at Jim’s and he joined us. Mollie wept when she heard what had befallen Mother and Juana, but presently she controlled herself and urged us on and Jim with us, though Jim needed no urging. She kissed him goodbye with tears and pride mingled in her eyes, and all he said was: “Goodbye, girl, keep your knife with you always.”
And so we rode away with Mollie’s “May the Saints be with you” in our ears. Once again we stopped at our abandoned goat pens, and there we dug up the rifle, belt and ammunition of the soldier Father had slain years before. These we gave to Jim.
Before we reached the market place our force commenced to dwindle again﻿—most of them could not brave the terrors of the Kash Guard upon which they had been fed in whispered story and in actual experience since infancy. I do not say that these men were cowards﻿—I do not believe that they were cowards, and yet they acted like cowards. It may be that a lifetime of training had taught them so thoroughly to flee the Kash Guard that now no amount of urging could make them face it﻿—the terror had become instinctive as is man’s natural revulsion for snakes. They could not face the Kash Guard any more than some men can touch a rattler, even though it may be dead.
It was market day and the place was crowded. I had divided my force so that we marched in from two directions in wide fronts, about five hundred men in each party, and surrounded the market place. As there were only a few men from our district among us I had given orders that there was to be no killing other than that of Kash Guards until we who knew the population could pick out the right men.
When the nearest people first saw us they did not know what to make of it, so complete was the surprise. Never in their lives had they seen men of their own class armed and there were a hundred of us mounted. Across the plaza a handful of Kash Guard were lolling in front of Hoffmeyer’s office. They saw my party first, as the other was coming up from behind them, and they mounted and came toward us. At the same moment I drew The Flag from my breast and waving it above my head, urged Red Lightning forward, shouting, as I rode: “Death to the Kash Guard!

===Chinese===

我们继续前进，父亲和我心中满是悲伤、苦涩，还有比以往更深的仇恨。我们朝本区的市场进发，路上在吉姆家停了一停，他便同我们一起走。莫莉听说母亲和胡安娜的遭遇后哭了，但很快便强忍住，催我们快走，也催吉姆同去——其实吉姆用不着催。她含着泪、带着骄傲同他吻别，他只说了一句：“再会，姑娘，那把刀随时带在身上。”
就这样我们骑马上路，莫莉那句“愿圣徒与你们同在”还留在耳边。我们在废弃的羊栏又停了一次，从那里挖出父亲多年前杀掉的那个士兵的步枪、腰带和弹药，都交给了吉姆。
还没到市场，我们的队伍又开始缩减——他们大多数人从吃奶的年纪起，就被耳语的传闻和亲身的经历喂饱了卡什卫队的恐怖，再没有胆气去直面的了。我不是说这些人是懦夫——我不信他们是懦夫，可他们的举动分明像懦夫。也许是一辈子的驯服把他们教得太彻底，一见卡什卫队就只有逃，如今任凭怎样劝，也劝不动他们回头——那恐惧已成本能，正如人天生怕蛇。有些人连死响尾蛇都不敢碰，他们也就同样无法直面卡什卫队。
这天正逢集市，广场上人山人海。我把队伍分成两股，从两个方向以宽正面开进来，每股约五百人，把市场围了个严实。因为队伍里认得本区人口的没有几个，我下了命令：除卡什卫队外不得杀人，等我们这些认得人的先把该杀的挑出来。
最近处的人们起初看见我们，竟不知是怎么回事——突袭太彻底了。他们生平从未见过自己这个阶层的人拿着武器，何况我们还有上百人骑着马。广场对面，一小队卡什卫队正懒散地靠在霍夫梅耶的官署门前。他们先看见了我们的这一股——另一股正从他们背后包抄过来——便翻身上马朝我们冲来。就在这时，我从怀里抽出那面旗帜，高举过顶挥舞着，催动红闪电向前，一边驰一边高喊：“卡什卫队死到临头了！

===Original===

Death to the Kalkars!”
And then, of a sudden, the Kash Guard seemed to realize that they were confronted by an actual force of armed men and their true color became apparent﻿—all yellow. They turned to flee, only to see another force behind them. The people had now caught the idea and the spirit of our purpose and they flocked around us, shouting, screaming, laughing, crying. “Death to the Kash Guard!” “Death to the Kalkars!” “The Flag!” I heard more than once, and “Old Glory!” from some who, like myself, had not been permitted to forget. A dozen men rushed to my side, and grasping the streaming banner pressed it to their lips while tears coursed down their cheeks. “The Flag! The Flag!” they cried. “The Flag of our fathers!”
It was then, before a shot had been fired, that one of the Kash Guard rode toward me with a white cloth above his head. I recognized him immediately as the youth who had brought the cruel order to Mother and who had shown sorrow for the acts of his superiors.
“Do not kill us,” he said, “and we will join with you. Many of the Kash Guard at the barracks will join, too.”
And so the dozen soldiers in the market place joined us, and a woman ran from her house carrying the head of a man stuck upon a short pole and she screamed forth her hatred against the Kalkars﻿—the hatred that was the common bond between us all. As she came closer I saw that it was Pthav’s woman and the head upon the short pole was the head of Pthav. That was the beginning﻿—that was the little spark that was needed. Like maniacs, laughing horribly, the people charged the houses of the Kalkars and dragged them forth to death.
Above the shrieking and the groans and the din could be heard shouts for The Flag and the names of loved ones who were being avenged. More than once I heard the name of Samuels the Jew﻿—never was a man more thoroughly avenged than he that day.
Dennis Corrigan was with us, freed from the mines, and Betty Worth, his woman, found him there, his arms red to the elbows with the blood of our oppressors. She had never thought to see him alive, and when she heard his story, and of how they had escaped she ran to me and nearly pulled me from Red Lightning’s back, trying to hug and kiss me.
It was she who started the people shouting for me until a mad, swirling mob of joy-crazed people surrounded me.

===Chinese===

卡尔卡人死到临头了！”
刹那间，卡什卫队似乎才醒悟过来，面对的是一支真正荷枪实弹的队伍，他们的本色顿时显露无遗——一黄到底。他们掉头要逃，却见身后又有一支队伍。此时百姓已明白了我们的来意，也染上了我们的士气，纷纷涌到我们周围，喊着、叫着、笑着、哭着。“卡什卫队死到临头了！”“卡尔卡人死到临头了！”“那面旗！”我听见有人喊了不止一次，还有人喊“老光荣！”——那是同我一样不曾获准忘掉的人。十几个人冲到我身边，抓住那面猎猎飘扬的旗帜贴上自己的嘴唇，眼泪顺着脸颊往下淌。“那面旗！那面旗！”他们喊道，“我们父辈的旗！”
就在那时，一枪未发，一个卡什卫队骑兵却朝我骑来，头上举着一块白布。我一眼认出，正是那个给母亲送来残酷命令、又为上头的作为露过悲容的年轻人。
“别杀我们，”他说，“我们同你们一起干。兵营里许多卡什卫队也会反正的。”
于是市场上的这十几个士兵投了我们这边。一个女人从屋里跑出来，手里举着插在短杆上的一颗人头，尖声喊出她对卡尔卡人的仇恨——这仇恨正是把我们所有人连在一起的东西。她走近了，我看清那是普塔夫的女人，短杆上的人头正是普塔夫的头。这就是开端——这就是需要的那点火星。人们像疯了一样，发出骇人的大笑，冲进卡尔卡人的宅子，把他们拖出来处死。
惨叫声、呻吟声、喧嚣声之上，响着为那面旗的呐喊，和一个个正在被复仇的亲人的名字。我不止一次听见犹太人塞缪尔斯的名字——从没有哪个人，在那一天被复仇得这样彻底。
丹尼斯·科里根同我们在一起，他是从矿上放出来的；他的女人贝蒂·沃思在这里找到了他，他的双臂直到肘弯都浸透了我们压迫者的血。她本以为再也见不到他活着了，听了他怎样逃出来的经过，她扑向我，险些把我从红闪电背上拽下来，又搂又亲。
就是她带头，让人们为我欢呼，直到一圈发疯似的、狂喜的人群把我围在当中。

===Original===

I tried to quiet them, for I knew that this was no way in which to forward our cause and finally I succeeded in winning a partial silence and then I told them that this madness must cease, that we had not yet succeeded, that we had won only a single small district and that we must go forward quietly and in accordance with a sensible plan if we were to be victorious.
“Remember,” I admonished them, “that there are still thousands of armed men in the city and that we must overthrow them all, and then there are other thousands that The Twentyfour will throw in upon us, for they will not surrender this territory until they are hopelessly defeated from here to Washington﻿—and that will require months and maybe years.”
They quieted down a little then, and we formed plans for marching immediately upon the barracks that we might take the Kash Guard by surprise. It was about this time that Father found Soor and killed him.
“I told you,” said Father, just before he ran a bayonet through the tax collector, “that some day I would have my little joke, and this is the day.”
Then a man dragged Hoffmeyer from some hiding place and the people literally tore him to pieces and that started the pandemonium all over again. There were cries of “On to the barracks!” and “Kill the Kash Guard!” followed by a concerted movement toward the lake front. On the way our numbers were increased by volunteers from every house﻿—either fighting men and women from the houses of our class or bloody heads from the houses of the Kalkars, for we carried them all with us, waving above us upon the ends of poles, and at the head of all I rode with Old Glory, now waving from a tall staff.
I tried to maintain some semblance of order; but it was impossible and so we streamed along, screaming and killing, laughing and crying, each as the mood claimed him. The women seemed the maddest, possibly because they had suffered most, and Pthav’s woman led them. I saw others there with one hand clutching a suckling baby to a bare breast while the other held aloft the dripping head of a Kalkar, an informer, or a spy. One could not blame them who knew the lives of terror and hopelessness they had led﻿—they and their mothers before them.
We had just crossed the new bridge over the river into the heart of the great, ruined city when the Kash Guard fell upon us from ambush with their full strength.

===Chinese===

我尽力让他们安静下来，因为我知道这样成不了事，终于争得了几分肃静，然后我告诉我们，这种疯狂必须停止，我们还没有成功，只赢得了一个小小的区域，要想胜利，就得安安静静地照着切实的计划往前推进。
“记住，”我告诫他们，“城里还有几千武装人员，我们必须把他们全部推翻；何况‘二十四人’还会再调几千人来增援——不把他们从这里一路打到华盛顿、打得一败涂地，他们是不会交出这片地盘的——那要花上几个月，也许几年。”
这时他们稍稍安静了些，我们立刻定下方略，径直奔袭兵营，好打卡什卫队一个措手不及。大约就在此时，父亲找到了索尔，杀了他。
“我说过，”父亲说——就在把刺刀捅进这个税吏身上之前——“总有一天我要开我那个小小的玩笑，就是今天。”
接着有人把霍夫梅耶从什么藏身处拖了出来，众人竟把他活活撕成了碎块，于是疯狂又重新爆发。“打到兵营去！”“杀光卡什卫队！”的喊声四起，队伍一齐朝湖边涌去。一路上，每座房子都给我们添了人手——不是我们这一阶层出来参战的男女，就是卡尔卡人宅子里出的血淋淋的人头，我们把它们统统带着，挑在杆子尖上在我们头顶挥舞，而我打头骑着，老光荣如今飘在一根高高的旗杆上。
我竭力维持一点秩序；可根本办不到，于是我们便一路涌过去，又杀又喊，又哭又笑，各随各的情绪。女人们像是疯得最厉害，多半因为她们受的罪最深，领头的正是普塔夫的女人。我还看见别的女人，一只手把吃奶的孩子搂在袒露的胸前，另一只手高举着滴血的卡尔卡人、告密者或奸细的头。知道她们——连同她们的母亲一辈——过的是怎样提心吊胆、无望的日子，就不能怪她们。
我们刚跨过河上的新桥，进入那座残破大城的腹地，卡什卫队便倾巢而出，从埋伏中向我们扑来。

===Original===

They were poorly disciplined; but they were armed, while we were not disciplined at all nor scarcely armed. We were nothing but an angry mob into which they poured volley after volley at close range. Men, women and babies went down and many turned and fled; but there were others who rushed forward and grappled hand to hand with the Kash Guard, tearing their rifles from them. We who were mounted rode among them. I could not carry The Flag and fight, so I took it from the staff and replaced it inside my shirt and then I clubbed my rifle and guiding Red Lightning with my knees I drove into them.
God of our Fathers, but it was a pretty fight. If I had known that I was to die the next minute I would have died gladly for the joy I had in those few minutes. Down they went before me, to right and to left, reeling from their saddles with crushed skulls and broken bodies, for wherever I hit them made no difference in the result﻿—they died if they came within reach of my rifle, which was soon only a bent and twisted tube of bloody metal.
And so I rode completely through them with a handful of men behind me. We turned then to ride back over the crumbling ruins that were in this spot only mounds of debris, and from the elevation of one of these hillocks of the dead past, I saw the battle down by the river and a great lump came into my throat. It was all over﻿—all but the bloody massacre. My poor mob had turned at last to flee, they were jammed and stuck upon the narrow bridge and the Kash Guard were firing volleys into that wedged mass of human flesh. Hundreds were leaping into the river only to be shot from the banks by the soldiers.
Twenty-five mounted men surrounded me﻿—all that was left of my fighting force﻿—and at least two thousand Kash Guards lay between us and the river. Even could we have fought our way back we could have done nothing to save the day or our own people. We were doomed to die; but we decided to inflict more punishment before we died.
I had in mind Juana in the clutches of Or-tis﻿—not once had the frightful thought left my consciousness﻿—and so I told them that I would ride to headquarters and search for her and they said that they would ride with me and that we would slay whom we could before the soldiers returned.
Our dream had vanished, our hopes were dead. In silence we rode through the streets toward the barracks.

===Chinese===

他们纪律松懈；可他们手里有枪，而我们全无纪律，也几乎没有武器。我们不过是一群愤怒的暴民，他们贴着近距离一排接一排地朝我们放枪。男人、女人、婴孩纷纷倒下，许多人转身逃了；但也有别人迎上前去，同卡什卫队白刃相搏，把枪从他们手里夺下来。我们这些骑马的在人丛中往来冲杀。我一手举不了旗又打不了仗，便把旗从杆上取下，重新塞进衬衫里，然后倒提步枪当棍使，用两膝引导着红闪电，直撞进敌阵。
列祖列宗的上帝啊，那可真是一场痛快的厮杀。哪怕下一分钟就死，为了那几分钟的快意，我也死得甘心。他们在我面前纷纷落马，左一个右一个，歪着身子栽下鞍来，头骨碎裂，筋断骨折——因为我打在哪儿都一样见效——只要够得着我的枪，他们就活不成，那杆枪不久就成了一根弯扭的血淋淋的铁管。
就这样，我带着身后一小队人马从他们中间贯穿而过。我们拨转马头，要从那些残垣断壁上冲回去——这里往日的雄城如今只剩一堆堆瓦砾——从其中一座死去的往昔堆成的小丘上，我望见了河边的战况，喉咙里顿时堵起一团东西。完了——除了血腥的屠杀，全完了。我那可怜的队伍终于掉头逃命，挤作一团堵在窄桥上，卡什卫队正朝那团挤死的人肉一排排地放枪。成百的人跳进河里，只落得被岸上的士兵一一射杀。
我身边剩下二十五个骑兵——我的战斗队伍的全部残余——而横在我们与河流之间的是至少两千卡什卫队。就算我们能杀回去，也救不了大局，救不了自己人。我们是必死的人了；但我们决定，死之前多拉几个垫背的。
我心里惦着落在奥提斯手里的胡安娜——这个可怕的念头一刻也没离开过我的意识——于是我告诉他们，我要骑马去总部找她，他们说愿同我一起去，趁士兵们没回来，能杀几个是几个。
我们的梦破灭了，我们的希望死了。我们默默无言，穿过街道，朝兵营走去。

===Original===

The Kash Guard had not come over to our side as we had hoped﻿—possibly they would have come had we had some measure of success in the city; but there could be no success against armed troops for an undisciplined mob of men, women and children.
I realized too late that we had not planned sufficiently, yet we might have won had not someone escaped and ridden ahead to notify the Kash Guard.
