Could we have taken them by surprise in the barracks the outcome might have been what it had been in the market places through which we had passed. I had realized our weakness and the fact that if we took time to plan and arrange, some spy or informer would have divulged all to the authorities long before we could have put our plans into execution. Really, there had been no other way than to trust to a surprise attack and the impetuosity of our first blow.
I looked about among my followers as we rode along. Jim was there, but not Father﻿—I never saw him again. He probably fell in the battle at the new bridge. Orrin Colby, blacksmith and preacher, rode at my side covered with blood﻿—his own and Kash Guard. Dennis Corrigan was there, too.
We rode right into the barrack yard, for with their lack of discipline and military efficiency they had sent their whole force against us with the exception of a few men who remained to guard the prisoners and a handful at headquarters building. The latter we overcame with scarce a struggle and from one whom I took prisoner I learned where the sleeping quarters of Or-tis were located.
Telling my men that our work was done I ordered them to scatter and escape as best they might; but they said that they would remain with me. I told them that the business I was on was such that I must handle it alone and asked them to go and free the prisoners while I searched for Juana. They said that they would wait for me outside and so we parted.
Or-tis’ quarters were on the second floor of the building in the east wing and I had no difficulty in finding them. As I approached the door I heard the sound of voices raised in anger within and of rapid movement as though someone was running hither and thither across the floor. I recognized Or-tis’ voice﻿—he was swearing foully, and then I heard a woman’s scream and I knew that it was Juana.
I tried the door and found it locked.

===Chinese===

卡什卫队并没有如我们盼望的那样倒向我们——倘若我们在城里得了一分成功，他们或许会倒过来；可一支毫无纪律的男女老幼的乌合之众，是打不过正规武装的。
我明白得太晚了：我们筹划得不够。可若不是有人漏网、抢先骑马去给卡什卫队报信，我们本是可以赢的。倘若能在兵营里打他们个措手不及，结局也许会同我们一路走过的那些市场一样。我早已明白我们的弱点，也明白只要费时间去筹划布置，早就会有奸细或告密者把一切捅给当局，根本轮不到我们动手。说真的，除了指望突袭和头一击的锐气，本来就没有别的路可走。
一路行进，我在随行的人里环顾。吉姆在，父亲却不在——我从此再没见过他。他多半是阵亡在新桥那一仗了。铁匠兼传道人奥林·科尔比骑在我身边，浑身是血——有他自己的，也有卡什卫队的。丹尼斯·科里根也在。
我们径直冲进兵营大院，因为他们既无纪律又无军事头脑，竟把全军都拉出去打我们了，只留下几个人看守俘虏，另有一小撮守着总部大楼。这一小撮我们几乎不费一枪就解决了，从俘获的一个人嘴里，我打听出奥提斯的卧室在哪里。
我告诉部下大事已了，命令他们各自分散、设法逃命；他们却说愿意跟着我。我说我要办的事只能一个人去办，请他们去解救俘虏，我则去找胡安娜。他们说就在外面等我，我们便分了手。
奥提斯的住处在东翼二楼，我不费事就找到了。走近门口，我听见屋里传出怒气冲冲的人声，还有急促的脚步声，仿佛有人在屋里跑来跑去。我听出是奥提斯的声音——他在恶毒地咒骂，接着我听见一声女人的尖叫，我知道那是胡安娜。
我推门，门锁着。

===Original===

It was a massive door, such as the ancients built in their great public buildings, such as this had originally been, and I doubted my ability to force it. I was mad with apprehension and lust for revenge, and if maniacs gain tenfold in strength when the madness is upon them, I must have been a maniac that moment, for when, after stepping back a few feet, I hurled myself against the door the shot bolt tore through the splintering frame and the barrier swung in upon its hinges with a loud bang.
Before me, in the center of the room, stood Or-tis with Juana in his clutches. He had her partially upon a table and with one hairy hand he was choking her. He looked up at the noise of my sudden entry, and when he saw me he went white and dropped Juana, at the same time whipping a pistol from its holster at his side. Juana saw me too, and springing for his arm dragged it down as he pulled the trigger, so that the bullet went harmlessly into the floor.
Before he could shake her off I was upon him and had wrenched the weapon from his grasp. I held him in one hand as one might a little child﻿—he was utterly helpless in my grip﻿—and I asked Juana if he had wronged her.
“Not yet,” she said, “he just came in after sending the Kash Guard away. Something has happened. There is going to be a battle; but he sneaked back to the safety of his quarters,” and then she seemed to notice for the first time that I was covered with blood. “There has been a battle!” she cried, “and you have been in it.”
I told her that I had and that I would tell her about it after I had finished Or-tis. He commenced to plead and then to whimper. He promised me freedom and immunity from punishment and persecution if I would let him live. He promised never to bother Juana again and to give us his protection and assistance. He would have promised me the Sun and the Moon and all the little stars had he thought I wished them; but I wished only one thing just then and I told him so﻿—to see him die.
“Had you wronged her,” I said, “you would have died a slow and terrible death; but I came in time to save her and so you are saved that suffering.”

===Chinese===

那是一扇厚重的大门，是古人在宏伟公共建筑里用的那种——这栋楼原本正是这样的建筑——我怀疑自己撞不撞得开。我急得发疯，恨得发疯，若说疯子发起狂来力气会大上十倍，那一刻我必是疯子无疑，因为我退后几步，猛地朝门撞去，那根闩死的门栓竟撕裂了崩碎的门框，整扇门“哐当”一声荡开了。
我面前，屋子中央，奥提斯正把胡安娜攥在手里。他把她半按在一张桌子上，一只毛茸茸的手掐着她的喉咙。听见我破门而入的响动，他抬起头，一看见我，脸色刷地白了，撒开胡安娜，同时从腰间枪套里拔手枪。胡安娜也看见了我，扑上去抓住他的手臂往下压，正赶在他扣扳机的一刻，子弹无害地打进了地板。
没等他甩脱她，我已经扑到他身上，把手枪从他手里夺了下来。我一只手提着他，像提一个小孩子——他在我手里全然无能为力——我问胡安娜，他可曾糟蹋她。
“还没有，”她说，“他刚打发走卡什卫队就进来了。出事了，要打仗了；他却溜回自己房里来图安全。”接着她像是头一回注意到我浑身是血。“打过仗了！”她喊道，“你也打过了。”
我说是的，等收拾完奥提斯再细讲给她听。他先是求饶，继而抽泣。他许我自由，许我不受惩罚、不受迫害，只要我饶他一命。他许下从此不再纠缠胡安娜，还要给我们保护和帮助。只要他以为我想要，太阳、月亮和满天的小星星他也都肯许给我；可我那时只想要一样东西，我也告诉了他——看着他死。
“你若糟蹋了她，”我说，“会死得又慢又惨；幸而我来得及时，救下了她，也免了你受那份罪。”

===Original===

When he realized that nothing could save him he began to weep and his knees shook so that he could not stand, and I had to hold him from the floor with one hand and with my other clenched I dealt him a single terrific blow between the eyes﻿—a blow that broke his neck and crushed his skull﻿—then I dropped him to the floor and took Juana in my arms.
Quickly, as we walked toward the entrance of the building, I told her of all that had transpired since we parted and that now she would be left alone in the world for awhile, until I could join her. I told her where to go and await me in a forgotten spot I had discovered upon the banks of the old canal on my journey to the mines. She cried and clung to me, begging to remain with me; but I knew it could not be, for already I could hear fighting in the yard below. We would be fortunate indeed if one of us escaped. At last she promised on condition that I would join her immediately, which, of course, I had intended doing as soon as I had the chance.
Red Lightning stood where I had left him before the door. A company of Kash Guard, evidently returning from the battle, were engaged with my little band that was slowly falling back toward the headquarters building. There was no time to be lost if Juana was to escape. I lifted her to Red Lightning’s back from where she stooped and threw her dear arms about my neck, covering my lips with kisses.
“Come back to me soon,” she begged, “I need you so﻿—and it will not be long before there will be another to need you too.”
I pressed her close to my breast. “And if I do not come back,” I said, “take this and give it to my son to guard as his fathers before him have,” and I placed The Flag in her hands.
The bullets were singing around us and I made her go, watching her as the noble horse raced swiftly across the parade and disappeared among the ruins to the west. Then I turned to the fighting to find but ten men left to me. Orrin Colby was dead and Dennis Corrigan. Jim was left and nine others. We fought as best we could; but we were cornered now, for other guards were streaming onto the parade from other directions and our ammunition was expended.
They rushed us then﻿—twenty to one﻿—and though we did the best we could, they overwhelmed us. Lucky Jim was killed instantly; but I was only stunned by a blow upon the head.

===Chinese===

他明白什么也救不了他，便哭了起来，双膝抖得站都站不住，我只得一只手把他提离地面，另一只手攥紧拳头，照他两眼之间狠狠一击——这一拳打断了他的脖颈，砸碎了他的头骨——然后我把尸身撂在地板上，把胡安娜搂进怀里。
朝楼门口走去的时候，我很快把分别以后发生的一切讲给她听，告诉她如今她要独自在世上待一阵子，等我去同她会合。我告诉她该到哪里等我——那是我去矿上的路上、在旧运河岸边发现的一处被人遗忘的地方。她哭着抱住我，求留下来陪我；可我知道不行，楼下院子里已经传来了厮杀声。我们两个能逃出一个，就算万幸了。最后她答应了，条件是我立刻去会她——这本来也是我一有机会就打算做的。
红闪电还拴在我离开时它站的门前。一队卡什卫队，看样子是从战场上撤回来的，正同我那小小的一伙人交火，那伙人在慢慢朝总部大楼退。要让胡安娜脱身，一刻也不能耽搁。我把她抱上红闪电的背，她在马上俯下身，伸出亲爱的手臂搂住我的脖子，把吻印满我的嘴唇。
“快些回到我身边，”她恳求道，“我这样需要你——而且要不了多久，还会有另一个也需要你呢。”
我把她紧紧搂在胸前。“倘若我回不来，”我说，“就收下这个，交给我的儿子，让他像他历代先人一样守护它。”说着，我把那面旗放到她手里。
子弹在我们四周嗖嗖地飞，我催她快走，望着那匹骏马载着她飞驰过操练场，消失在西边的废墟里。然后我转身投入战斗，身边只剩下十个人。奥林·科尔比死了，丹尼斯·科里根也死了。吉姆还在，另有九人。我们尽力苦战；可我们已被逼进死角，别的卫队正从四面八方涌向操场，我们的弹药也打光了。
他们朝我们扑来——二十对一——我们虽然拼尽全力，还是被压倒了。幸运的吉姆当场死了；我只是头上挨了一击，昏了过去。

===Original===

That night they tried me before a court-martial and tortured me in an effort to make me divulge the names of my accomplices; but there were none left alive that I knew of, even had I wished to betray them. As it was, I just refused to speak. I never spoke again after bidding Juana goodbye, other than the few words of encouragement that passed between those of us who remained fighting to the last.
Early the next morning I was led forth to The Butcher.
I recall every detail up to the moment the knife touched my throat﻿—there was a slight stinging sensation followed instantly by﻿—oblivion.
---
It was broad daylight when he finished﻿—so quickly had the night sped﻿—and I could see by the light from the east window of the room where we sat that his face looked drawn and pinched and that even then he was suffering the sorrows and disappointments of the bitter, hopeless life he had just described.
I rose to retire. “That is all?” I asked.
“Yes,” he replied, “that is all of that incarnation.”
“But you recall another?” I insisted. He only smiled as I was closing the door.

===Chinese===

当夜，他们对我进行了军事审判，还上了刑，逼我说出同伙的名字；可就我所知，活着的一个也没有了，即便我想出卖他们。我便只是拒绝开口。同胡安娜道别之后，我再没说过话，只在最后一同死战的几个人中间，说过几句互相打气的话。
第二天一早，我被押了出去，交给屠夫。
直到刀锋碰到喉咙的那一刻，每个细节我都记得——先是一下轻微的刺痛，随即便是——一片空白。
---
他讲完时已是天光大亮——黑夜竟过得这样快——从我们坐处的东窗透进光来，我看见他面容枯槁瘦削，即便此刻，他还受着他刚讲述的那种辛酸无望生涯的愁苦与失意的煎熬。
我起身告退。“就这些？”我问。
“是的，”他答道，“那一世就这些。”
“可你还记得另一世？”我追问。他只是笑了笑，在我掩门的时候。

# Part III The Red Hawk / 第三部 红鹰
===Original===



===Chinese===

第三部 红鹰

## I The Desert Clans / 一 荒漠部族
===Original===

The January sun beat hotly down upon me as I reined Red Lightning in upon the summit of a barren hill and looked down upon the rich land of plenty that stretched away below me as far as the eye could see toward the mighty sea that lay a day’s ride, perhaps, to the westward﻿—the sea that none of us had ever looked upon﻿—the sea that had become as fabulous as a legend of the ancients during the almost four hundred years since the Moon Men had swept down upon us and overwhelmed the Earth in their mad and bloody carnival of revolution.
In the near distance the green of the orange groves mocked us from below, and great patches that were groves of leafless nut trees, and there were sandy patches toward the south that were vineyards waiting for the hot suns of April and May before they, too, broke into riotous, tantalizing green. And from this garden spot of plenty a curling trail wound up the mountainside to the very level where we sat gazing fiercely down upon this last stronghold of our foes. When the ancients built that trail it must have been wide and beautiful indeed, but in the centuries that have elapsed man and the elements have sadly defaced it. The rains have washed it away in places and the Kalkars have made great gashes in it to deter us, their enemies, from invading their sole remaining lands and driving them into the sea; and upon their side of the gashes they have built forts where they keep warriors always. And well for them that they do. It is so upon every pass that leads down into their country.
Since fell my great ancestor, Julian 9th, in the year 2122, at the end of the first uprising against the Kalkars, we have been driving them slowly back across the world. That was over three hundred years ago. For a hundred years they have held us here, a day’s ride from the ocean. Just how far it is we do not know; but in 2408 my grandfather, Julian 18th, rode alone almost to the sea. He had won back almost to safety when he was discovered and pursued almost to the tents of his people. There was a battle, and the Kalkars who had dared invade our country were destroyed, but Julian 18th died of his wounds without being able to tell more than that a wondrous rich country lay between us and the sea, which was not more than a day’s ride distant. A day’s ride, for us, might be anything under a hundred miles.
We are desert people.

===Chinese===

一月的太阳灼热地照在我身上，我勒住红闪电（Red Lightning），登上一座荒山秃岭的顶巅，俯瞰脚下那片丰饶富足的大地——它一直延伸到目力尽头，再往西大约一天的路程，便是那片浩瀚的大海。我们当中谁也不曾见过那片海——自从月球人（Moon Men）在将近四百年前扑到我们头上、以他们疯狂血腥的革命的狂欢将地球吞没以来，那片海就已经变得像远古传说一样虚渺难考。
近处山脚下，橙林的翠绿在下面嘲弄着我们，还有大片大片光秃秃的坚果树林，南边还有一块块沙地，那是葡萄园，正等着四五月的热日一到，也爆发出喧闹撩人的新绿。就从这片丰饶的园地，一条蜿蜒的小径盘上山坡，直通到我们驻马俯瞰敌人这最后堡垒的地方。古人修筑这条小径时，想必宽阔而壮丽，可是经过悠悠几个世纪，人力与风雨已把它毁损得不成样子。雨水在有些地段把它冲毁，卡尔卡人（Kalkars）又在上面砍出一道道大豁口，想借此阻遏我们这些他们的敌人，不让我们侵入他们仅存的土地、把他们赶下海去；而在豁口靠他们的一侧，他们修筑了堡垒，常年驻着武士。他们这么做是明智的。凡是通向他们境内的山口，处处都是如此。
自从我的伟大祖先朱利安九世（Julian 9th）于2122年在第一次反卡尔卡人起义的末尾倒下以来，我们一直把他们缓慢地往世界的另一头驱赶。那是三百多年前的事了。一百年来，他们把我们挡在这里，离海洋一天的路程。究竟有多远，我们不得而知；但在2408年，我的祖父朱利安十八世（Julian 18th）单枪匹马几乎骑到了海边。他眼看就要安然返回，却被敌人发觉，一路被追杀到族人的营帐前。随后打了一仗，胆敢侵入我们国土的卡尔卡人被歼灭了，可朱利安十八世也因伤重不治，只来得及说：在我们和大海之间横亘着一片极其富庶的国度，而大海不过一天的路程。对我们来说，一天的路程，百里之内都算得上。
我们是荒漠部族。

===Original===

Our herds range a vast territory where feed is scarce that we may be always near the goal that our ancestors set for us three centuries ago﻿—the shore of the western sea into which it is our destiny to drive the remnants of our former oppressors. In the forests and mountains of Arizona there is rich pasture, but it is far from the land of the Kalkars where the last of the tribe of Or-tis make their last stand, and so we prefer to live in the desert near our foes, driving our herds great distances to pasture when the need arises, rather than to settle down in a comparative land of plenty, resigning the age-old struggle, the ancient feud between the house of Julian and the house of Or-tis.
A light breeze moves the black mane of the bright bay stallion beneath me. It moves my own black mane where it falls loose below the buckskin thong that encircles my head and keeps it from my eyes. It moves the dangling ends of the Great Chief’s blanket where it is strapped behind my saddle. On the twelfth day of the eighth month of the year just gone this Great Chief’s blanket covered the shoulders of my father, Julian 19th, from the burning rays of the summer’s desert sun. I was twenty on that day and on that day my father fell before the lance of an Or-tis in the Great Feud and I became The Chief of Chiefs.
Surrounding me today, as I sit looking down upon the land of my enemies, are fifty of the fierce chieftains of the hundred clans that swear allegiance to the house of Julian. They are bronzed and, for the most part, beardless men. The insignias of their clans are painted in various colors upon their foreheads, their cheeks, their breasts. Ochre, they use and blue and white and scarlet. Feathers rise from the head bands that confine their hair﻿—the feathers of the vulture, the hawk and the eagle. I, Julian 20th, wear a single feather. It is from a red-tailed hawk﻿—the clan-sign of my family.
We are all garbed similarly. Let me describe The Wolf, and in his portrait you will see a composite of us all. He is a sinewy, well built man of fifty, with piercing, gray-blue eyes beneath straight brows. His head is well-shaped, denoting great intelligence. His features are strong and powerful and of a certain fierce cast that might well strike terror to a foeman’s heart﻿—and does, if the Kalkar scalps that fringe his ceremonial blanket stand for aught.

===Chinese===

我们的畜群在一片广袤的牧草稀少的地域上放养，为的是始终逼近祖先三百年前为我们立下的目标——西海之滨，把昔日压迫者的残余赶进海里，正是我们的天命。亚利桑那的森林与群山之中有丰美的牧场，但它远离卡尔卡人的土地，远离奥提斯（Or-tis）部族作最后挣扎的地方，因此我们宁愿住在贴近仇敌的荒漠里，必要时赶着畜群远涉千里去放牧，也不肯安居在一片相对富足的土地上，放弃那场绵延已久的斗争——朱利安家族与奥提斯家族之间的世仇。
一阵微风拂动我胯下那匹枣红骏马的黑鬃，也拂动我自己的黑发——它松散地垂在束发皮绳之下，免得遮住眼睛。它又拂动大首领毯子垂下的边角，那毯子拴在我的马鞍后。就在去年第八个月的第十二天，这条大首领的毯子还披在我父亲朱利安十九世（Julian 19th）的肩头，替他遮挡盛夏荒漠的烈焰。那天我满二十岁，也就在那一天，我父亲倒在世仇大战中一支奥提斯家族的长矛之下，我成了众首领之首。
今天，当我坐在那里俯瞰敌人的土地时，环绕在我身边的是向朱利安家族宣誓效忠的百族之中五十名剽悍的首领。他们个个古铜肤色，多半不蓄须。各族的族徽用各种颜色涂画在额头上、脸颊上、胸膛上。他们用赭石，也用蓝、白、朱红。束缚头发的束带里插着羽毛——兀鹫的、鹰隼的、雕的羽毛。我，朱利安二十世（Julian 20th），只插一根羽毛，那是一支红尾鹰的羽毛——那是我家族的族徽。
我们装束都相仿。且让我描绘一下“狼”（The Wolf）吧，从他的肖像里你就能看到我们所有人的模样。他是个筋骨结实、体格匀称的五十岁汉子，平直的眉下一双灰蓝色的锐眼。他头形端正，透着过人的智识。他相貌刚毅威武，带着几分凶悍之气，足以令敌胆寒——倘若他仪毯边缘缀着的那一圈卡尔卡人头皮不是虚饰的话，那就确实如此。

===Original===

His breeches, wide below the hips and skin tight from above the knees down are of the skin of the buck deer. His soft boots, tied tight about the calf of each leg, are also of buck. Above the waist he wears a sleeveless vest of calfskin tanned with the hair on. The Wolf’s is of fawn and white. Sometimes these vests are ornamented with bits of colored stone or metal sewn to the hide in various manners of design. From The Wolf’s headband, just above the right ear, depends the tail of a timber-wolf﻿—the clan-sign of his family.
An oval shield, upon which is painted the head of a wolf, hangs about this chief’s neck, covering his back from nape to kidneys. It is a stout, light shield﻿—a hard wood frame covered with bull hide. Around its periphery have been fastened the tails of wolves. In such matters each man, with the assistance of his women folk, gives rein to his fancy in the matter of ornamentation. Clan-signs and chief-signs, however, are sacred. The use of one to which he is not entitled might spell death for any man. I say might, because we have no inflexible laws. We have few laws. The Kalkars were forever making laws, so we hate them. We judge each case upon its own merits, and we pay more attention to what a man intended doing than what he did.
The Wolf is armed, as are the rest of us, with a light lance about eight feet in length, a knife and a straight, two-edged sword. A short, stout bow is slung beneath his right stirrup leather and a quiver of arrows is at his saddle bow.
The blades of his sword and his knife and the metal of his lance tip come from a far place called Kolrado and are made by a tribe that is famous because of the hardness and the temper of the metal of its blades. The Utaws bring us metal, also, but theirs is inferior and we use it only for the shoes that protect our horses’ feet from the cutting sands and the rocks of our hard and barren country.
The Kolrados travel many days to reach us, coming once in two years. They pass, unmolested, through the lands of many tribes because they bring what none might otherwise have and what we need in our never-ending crusade against the Kalkars. That is the only thread that holds together the scattered clans and tribes that spread east and north and south beyond the ken of man. All are animated by the same purpose﻿—to drive the last of the Kalkars into the sea.

===Chinese===

他的裤子臀部以下宽大、膝上至脚踝紧裹，是用雄鹿皮做的。他脚上的软靴在小腿处紧紧绑住，也是鹿皮所制。腰以上，他穿一件无袖的坎肩，是带毛鞣制的小牛皮。狼的那件是小鹿毛色的，间杂白斑。这种坎肩有时缀着彩色石子或金属小片，以各种图案缝在皮面上。狼的束带上，恰在右耳上方，垂着一条灰狼的尾巴——那是他家族的族徽。
一面椭圆形的盾牌，上面画着狼头，挂在这位首领的颈间，从后颈一直遮到腰。那是一面又坚韧又轻巧的盾——硬木框架蒙上公牛皮。盾的边缘缀着一圈狼尾。在这一类装饰上，每个男人都可以在家眷妇人的协助下随意发挥。但族徽与首领徽记却是神圣不可僭越的。冒用不该用的徽记，任何男人都可能因此丧命。我说“可能”，是因为我们没有一成不变的法律。我们的法律寥寥无几。卡尔卡人动辄立法，所以我们厌恶法律。我们逐案酌情裁断，而且比起一个人做了什么，我们更看重他本来想做什么。
狼的兵器与我们大家一样：一杆八英尺左右的长矛，一把刀，一柄双刃直剑。一张短而结实的弓挂在右边的马镫皮带下，一壶箭斜插在鞍前。
他的剑刃、刀刃和矛头的金属来自一个遥远的地方，叫科罗拉多（Kolrado），出自一个以刀剑钢材坚硬而有韧性闻名的部族之手。尤塔人（Utaws）也给我们带来金属，但他们的货较差，我们只用来打造蹄铁，保护马蹄免受我们这片坚硬贫瘠的土地上割人的沙石之害。
科罗拉多人要走许多天的路才能到我们这里，每两年来一次。他们一路穿行许多部族的土地而无人侵犯，因为他们带来了别人无从获得、而我们在讨伐卡尔卡人的永无止境的圣战中所必需的东西。正是这唯一的纽带，把散布在东、北、南三方、远超人迹所知的各个部族联结在一起。所有人都被同一个信念激励着——把最后一批卡尔卡人赶下海去。

===Original===

From the Kolrados we get meager news of clans beyond them toward the rising sun. Far, far to the east, they say, so far that in a lifetime no man might reach it, lies another great sea, and that there, as here upon the world’s western edge, a few Kalkars are making their last stand. All the rest of the world has been won back by the people of our own blood﻿—by Americans.
We are always glad to see the Kolrados come, for they bring us news of other peoples, and we welcome the Utaws, too, though we are not a friendly people, killing all others who come among us, for fear, chiefly, that they may be spies sent by the Kalkars. It is handed down from father to son that this was not always so, and that once the people of the world went to and fro safely from place to place and that then all spoke the same language; but now it is different. The Kalkars brought hatred and suspicion among us, until now we trust only the members of our own clans and tribes.
The Kolrados, from coming often among us, we can understand, and they can understand us, by means of a few words and many signs, though when they speak their own language among themselves we cannot understand them, except for an occasional word that is like one of ours. They say that when the last of the Kalkars is driven from the world we must live at peace with one another, but I am afraid that that will never come to pass, for who would go through life without breaking a lance or dipping his sword point now and again into the blood of a stranger? Not The Wolf, I swear, nor no more The Red Hawk. By The Flag! I take more pleasure in meeting a stranger upon a lonely trail than in meeting a friend, for I cannot set my lance against a friend and feel the swish of the wind as Red Lightning bears me swiftly down upon the prey as I crouch in the saddle, nor thrill to the shock as we strike.
I am The Red Hawk. I am but twenty, yet the fierce chiefs of a hundred fierce clans bow to my will. I am a Julian﻿—the twentieth Julian﻿—and from this year, 2434, I can trace my line back five hundred and thirty-four years to Julian 1st, who was born in 1896. From father to son, by word of mouth, has been handed down to me the story of every Julian, and there is no blot upon the shield of one in all that long line, nor shall there be any blot upon the shield of Julian 20th.

===Chinese===

从科罗拉多人那里，我们偶尔听到些有关他们以东、日出之方向各部族的消息。他们说，在遥远的东方，远得一辈子也走不到的地方，还有一片大海，而在那里，正如在这世界的西部边缘一样，残存着少数仍在作最后挣扎的卡尔卡人。世界上其余的地方，都已被与我们同血统的人民夺了回来——被美国人夺了回来。
科罗拉多人到来时我们总是高兴的，因为他们带来别处人民的消息；我们也欢迎尤塔人，尽管我们并不是一个友善的民族——凡是来到我们中间的外人一律格杀，主要是唯恐他们是卡尔卡人派来的奸细。父子口耳相传，说从前并非如此，说曾经世上的人可以安全地四处往来，而且那时人人说着同一种语言；如今却不同了。卡尔卡人把仇恨与猜疑散布到我们中间，直到现在，我们只信本族本部的人。
科罗拉多人因为常来常往，我们能听懂他们的话，他们也能听懂我们的——靠寥寥几个词加上许多手势；不过当他们彼此说自己的语言时，我们就听不懂了，只能偶尔捕捉到一个与我们的话相似的词。他们说，等最后一批卡尔卡人被从世界上赶尽，我们彼此就该和平相处。可我恐怕那一天永远不会有，因为谁能一辈子不折断一杆长矛、不把剑尖偶尔蘸一蘸外乡人的血呢？绝不是狼，我敢起誓，也绝不再是红鹰。以旗帜起誓！在孤寂的山径上遇见一个陌生人，比遇见一个朋友更让我快活，因为对朋友我不能挺矛相向，不能体味红闪电驮着我伏在鞍上向猎物疾冲时风声的呼啸，也不能感受撞击那一刻的震颤。
我就是红鹰。我不过二十岁，可一百个剽悍部族的剽悍首领们都听命于我。我是朱利安家的——第二十位朱利安——从今年、2434年上溯，我可以把我这一支追溯到五百三十四年前的朱利安一世，他生于1896年。从父到子，口口相传，历代朱利安的功业都传到了我手里，在那绵长的世系中，任何一人的盾牌上都没有污点，朱利安二十世的盾牌上也不将有污点。

===Original===

From my fifth year to my tenth I learned, word for word, as had my father before me, the deeds of my forebears, and to hate the Kalkars and the tribe of Or-tis. This, with riding, was my schooling. From ten to fifteen I learned to use lance and sword and knife, and on my sixteenth birthday I rode forth with the other men﻿—a warrior.
As I sat there this day, looking down upon the land of the accursed Kalkars, my mind went back to the deeds of the 15th Julian, who had driven the Kalkars across the desert and over the edge of these mountains into the valley below just one hundred years before I was born, and I turned to The Wolf and pointed down toward the green groves and the distant hills and off beyond to where the mysterious ocean lay.
“For a hundred years they have held us here,” I said. “It is too long.”
“It is too long,” replied The Wolf.
“When the rains are over The Red Hawk leads his people into the land of plenty.”
The Rock raised his spear and shook it savagely toward the valley far below. The scalp-lock fastened just below its metal-shod tip trembled in the wind. “When the rains are over!” cried The Rock. His fierce eyes glowed with the fire of fanaticism.
“The green of the groves we will dye red with their blood,” cried The Rattlesnake.
“With our swords, not our mouths,” I said, and wheeled Red Lightning toward the east. The Coyote laughed and the others joined with him as we wound downward out of the hills toward the desert.
On the afternoon of the following day we came within sight of our tents where they were pitched beside the yellow flood of The River. Five miles before that we had seen a few puffs of smoke rise from the summit of a hill to the north of us. It told the camp that a body of horsemen was approaching from the west. It told us that our sentry was on duty and that doubtless all was well. At a signal my warriors formed themselves in two straight lines, crossing one another at their centers. A moment later another smoke signal arose informing the camp that we were friends and us that our signal had been rightly read.
Presently, in a wild charge, whooping and brandishing our spears, we charged down among the tents. Dogs, children and slaves scampered for safety, the dogs barking, the children and the slaves yelling and laughing.

===Chinese===

从五岁到十岁，我逐字逐句地学习——我父亲当年也是如此——列祖列宗的功业，学着憎恨卡尔卡人和奥提斯部族。这就是我的学业，外加骑术。十岁到十五岁，我学长矛、剑和刀；十六岁生日那天，我随其他男人一同出征——成为一名武士。
这一天，当我坐在那里俯瞰该诅咒的卡尔卡人的土地时，我的思绪回到了朱利安十五世的功业上——恰在我出生前一百年，他把卡尔卡人赶过了荒漠、赶过了这些山脉的边缘，赶进了下面的谷地。我转向狼，指向山下青翠的果园、远处的丘陵，以及更远处那片神秘的大海所在的方向。
“一百年来他们把我们堵在这儿，”我说，“太久了。”
“是太久了。”狼答道。
“雨季一过，红鹰就率领他的人民开进那片丰饶的土地。”
岩石（The Rock）举起长矛，朝脚下深谷狂挥。矛头下一束夺来的头皮在风中颤动。“雨季一过！”岩石大喊，凶猛的眼中燃着狂热之火。
“果园的翠绿，我们要用他们的血染成红色。”响尾蛇（The Rattlesnake）叫道。
“用我们的剑，不是用我们的嘴。”我说着拨转红闪电向东。郊狼（The Coyote）大笑，众人随他一起笑，我们盘山而下，朝荒漠驰去。
第二天下午，我们望见了我们的营帐，它们扎在大河那黄色的急流旁。在那之前五里，我们已看见北面一座山顶升起几缕烟。它告诉营地：一队骑手正从西面开来。它也告诉我们：哨兵在位，想必一切平安。一声令下，我的武士们排成两条笔直的队列，在中心处交叉。片刻后又一处烟信号升起，告知营地来者是朋友，也告知我们信号已被正确读出。
随即，我们狂呼着、挥舞着长矛，旋风般冲进营帐之间。狗、孩子和奴隶四散奔逃，狗吠着，孩子和奴隶又叫又笑。

===Original===

As we swung ourselves from our mounts before our tents, slaves rushed out to seize our bridle reins, the dogs leaped, growling, upon us in exuberant welcome, while the children fell upon their sires, their uncles or their brothers, demanding the news of the ride or a share in the spoils of conflict or chase. Then we went in to our women.
I had no wife, but there were my mother and my two sisters, and I found them awaiting me in the inner tent, seated upon a low couch that was covered, as was the floor, with the bright blankets that our slaves weave from the wool of sheep. I knelt and took my mother’s hand and kissed it and then I kissed her upon the lips and in the same fashion I saluted my sisters, the elder first. It is custom among us; but it is also our pleasure, for we both respect and love our women. Even if we did not, we should appear to, if only for the reason that the Kalkars do otherwise. They are brutes and swine. We do not permit our women a voice in the councils of the men, but none the less do they influence our councils from the seclusion of their inner tents. It is indeed an unusual mother among us who does not make her voice heard in the council ring, through her husband or her sons, and she does it through the love and respect in which they hold her and not by scolding and nagging. They are wonderful, our women. It is for them and The Flag that we have fought the foe across a world for three hundred years. It is for them that we shall go forth and drive him into the sea.
As the slaves prepared the evening meal I chatted with my mother and my sisters. My two brothers, The Vulture and Rain Cloud, lay also at my mother’s feet. The Vulture was eighteen, a splendid warrior, a true Julian. Rain Cloud was sixteen then, and I think the most beautiful creature I had ever seen. He had just become a warrior, but so sweet and lovable was his disposition that the taking of human life seemed a most incongruous calling for him, yet he was a Julian and there was no alternative. Everyone loved him and respected him too, even though he had never excelled in feats of arms for which he seemed to have no relish; but they respected him because they knew that he was brave and that he would fight as courageously as any of them, even though he might have no stomach for it.

===Chinese===

我们在自家帐前翻身下马，奴隶们奔出来牵住缰绳，狗儿们咆哮着扑上来，热烈地欢迎我们，孩子们则扑向各自的父亲、叔伯或兄长，讨要此行的消息，或是讨一份战利品、猎获的分成。然后我们进去见我们的女人。
我没有妻子，但有母亲和两个妹妹，我见她们正在内帐中等我，坐在一张矮榻上。榻上和地上一样，铺着我们奴隶用羊毛织的鲜亮毯子。我跪下握住母亲的手吻了吻，又吻她的唇，然后以同样的礼节向妹妹们致意，先长后幼。这是我们的习俗，也是我们的欢愉，因为我们既敬重又疼爱我们的女人。即便不是这样，我们也要装出如此，只因为卡尔卡人的做法相反。他们是畜生，是猪猡。我们不许女人在男人的议事会上开口，可她们依然能在内帐的深闺里左右我们的会议。在我们中间，做母亲的极少有不能通过丈夫或儿子在议事圈里传扬自己声音的，而她们靠的是丈夫儿子对她的敬爱，并非唠叨聒噪。我们的女人是了不起的。三百年来，正是为了她们和旗帜，我们才把仇敌赶过一个世界。也正是为了她们，我们即将出征，把他赶下海去。
奴隶们备晚膳的时候，我与母亲和妹妹们闲谈。我的两个弟弟，秃鹫（The Vulture）和雨云（Rain Cloud），也躺在我母亲脚边。秃鹫十八岁，已是出色的武士，真正的朱利安家风。雨云当时十六岁，我想那是我见过的最俊美的生灵。他刚成为武士，可他的性情那么温良可爱，杀人夺命这门营生在他实在是格格不入；然而他是朱利安家的人，别无选择。人人都爱他，也敬他，尽管他从不擅长武艺——他对武功似乎毫无兴趣；人们敬他，是因为知道他勇敢，知道一旦需要，他会打得同任何人一样勇猛，哪怕心里毫无兴致。

===Original===

Personally, I considered Rain Cloud braver than I, for I knew that he would do well the thing he hated while I would be only doing well the thing I loved.
The Vulture resembled me in looks and the love of blood, so we left Rain Cloud at home to help guard the women and the children, which was no disgrace since it is a most honorable and sacred trust, and we went forth to the fighting when there was likely to be any, and when there wasn’t we went forth and searched for it. How often have I ridden the trails leading in across our vast frontiers longing for sight of a strange horseman against whom I might bend my lance! We asked no questions then when we had come close enough to see the clan-sign of the stranger and to know that he was of another tribe and likely he was as keen for the fray as we, otherwise he would have tried to avoid us. We each drew rein at a little distance and set his lance, and each called aloud his name, and then with a mighty oath each bore down upon the other, and then one rode away with a fresh scalp-lock, and a new horse to add to his herd, while the other remained to sustain the vulture and the coyote.
Two or three of our great, shaggy hounds came in and sprawled among us as we lay talking with mother and the two girls, Nallah and Neeta. Behind my mother and sisters squatted three slave girls, ready to do their bidding, for our women do no labor. They ride and walk and swim and keep their bodies strong and fit that they may bear mighty warriors, but labor is beneath them as it is beneath us. We hunt and fight and tend our own herds, for that is not menial, but all other labor the slaves perform. We found them here when we came. They have been here always﻿—a stolid, dark skinned people, weavers of blankets and baskets, makers of pottery, tillers of the soil. We are kind to them and they are happy. The Kalkars, who preceded us, were not kind to them﻿—it has been handed down to them from father to son for over a hundred years that the Kalkars were cruel to them and they hate their memory, yet, were we to be driven away by the Kalkars, these simple people would remain and serve anew their cruel masters, for they will never leave their soil.

===Chinese===

在我自己看来，雨云比我更勇敢，因为我知道，他能把他憎恨的事做好，而我不过是把热爱的事做好罢了。
秃鹫在相貌和嗜血上都像我，于是我们把雨云留在家里护卫妇孺——这并非耻辱，那是最光荣、最神圣的职责——而我们，只要可能打仗就出征，没有仗打，我们就出去找仗打。多少次，我纵马驰过我们辽阔边界的条条小径，渴望望见一个外族骑手的身影，好让我把长矛向他挺去！等我们靠得够近，看清来人的族徽、知道他是异部族时，我们从不问一句话——而对方多半也和我们一样渴望交锋，否则他早设法避开我们了。两人相隔一小段距离勒马，各摆好矛，各高声报出自己的名号，然后各自发一声重誓，向对方猛冲过去。之后，一人带着新割的头皮、赶着归入自己马群的新坐骑离去，另一人则留下来喂养兀鹫和郊狼。
我们正陪母亲和娜拉（Nallah）、妮塔（Neeta）两个妹妹说话时，两三只高大蓬毛的猎犬钻进来，横躺在我们中间。母亲和妹妹们身后蹲着三个女奴，随时听候差遣，因为我们的女人是不做劳役的。她们骑马、步行、游泳，把身体练得强健结实，好多生勇武的战士；劳役对她们是卑贱的，正如对我们一样。我们打猎、打仗、亲自照管畜群，这不算贱役，其余的活计全由奴隶承担。我们来的时候他们就在这里。他们从来就在这里——一个沉静的深肤色民族，织毯编篮，制陶耕田。我们待他们和善，他们也快活。先我们而来的卡尔卡人待他们却不和善——百余年来父子相传，他们念念不忘卡尔卡人对他们的残虐，恨着那些人的名字；然而，倘若我们被卡尔卡人赶走，这些淳朴的人仍会留下，重新伺候他们残暴的主人，因为他们永远不肯离开自己的土地。

===Original===

They have strange legends of a far time when great horses of iron raced across the desert dragging iron tents filled with people behind them, and they point to holes in the mountain sides through which these iron monsters made their way to the green valleys by the sea, and they tell of men who flew like birds and as swiftly, but of course we know that such things were never true and are but the stories that the old men and the women among them told to the children for their amusement. However, we like to listen to them.
I told my mother of my plans to move down into the valley of the Kalkars after the rains. She was silent some time before replying.
“Yes, of course,” she said; “you would be no Julian were you not to attempt it. At least twenty times before in a hundred years have our warriors gone down in force into the valley of the Kalkars and been driven back. I wish that you might have taken a wife and left a son to be Julian 21st before you set out upon this expedition from which you may not return. Think well of it, my son, before you set forth. A year or two will make no great difference. But you are The Great Chief and if you decide to go we can but wait here for your return and pray that all is well with you.”
“But you do not understand, Mother,” I replied. “I said that we are going to move down into the valley of the Kalkars after the rains. I did not say that we are coming back again. I did not say that you would remain here and wait for our return. You will accompany us. The tribe of Julian moves down into the valley of the Kalkars when the rains are over, and they take with them their women and their children and their tents and all their flocks and herds and every other possession that is movable, and﻿—they do not return to live in the desert ever more.”
She did not reply, but only sat in thought. Presently a man-slave came to bid us warriors to the evening meal. The women and the children eat this meal within their tents, but the warriors gather around a great, circular table, called The Council Ring.
There were a hundred of us there that night. Flares in the hands of slaves gave us light and there was light from the cooking fire that burned within the circle formed by the table. The others remained standing until I had taken my seat which was the signal that the eating might begin.

===Chinese===

他们有种种奇怪的传说，说在遥远的古代，铁打的大马驰过荒漠，身后拖着坐满了人的铁帐篷；他们指着山腰上的孔洞，说那些铁怪物就是从那里穿山而过，去到海边的青翠谷地；他们还说有人能像鸟一样飞行，一样迅疾。这些当然都不是真事，不过是他们当中的老人和女人讲给孩子取乐的故事。不过我们倒也爱听。
我把雨季过后便打进卡尔卡人谷地的计划告诉了母亲。她沉默了好一阵才回答。
“是啊，自然要去，”她说，“不去尝试，你就不配是朱利安家的人。一百年里，我们的武士至少有二十次成群结队杀进卡尔卡人的谷地，都被赶了回来。我倒但愿你先娶妻生子，留下个朱利安二十一世再踏上这条可能回不来的征途。我儿，出发前好好想想。晚一两年没有什么大不了。可你是大首领，你决意要去，我们只能在这里等你回来，求一切平安。”
“可您不明白，母亲，”我答道，“我说的是雨季过后我们就要开进卡尔卡人的谷地。我没说我们还会回来。我没说您留在这里等我们回来。您要随我们同去。雨季一结束，朱利安部族就开进卡尔卡人的谷地，带上他们的女人、孩子、帐篷，带上全部羊群牛马，带上每一件搬得动的家当，而且——他们永远不再回荒漠来住了。”
她没有作答，只坐着出神。片刻后，一名男奴来请我们武士去用晚膳。女人和孩子们在自己帐中用这一餐，武士们则围坐于一张巨大的圆桌旁，那桌子叫议事圈。
那晚我们一百人齐聚。奴隶手中的火把为我们照明，桌圈中央燃着的炊火也亮。众人一直站着，直到我入座——那便是开膳的号令。

===Original===

Before each warrior was an earthenware vessel containing beer and another filled with wine, and there were slaves whose duty it was to keep these filled, which was no small task, for we are hearty men and great drinkers, though there is no drunkenness among us as there is among the Kalkars. Other slaves brought meat and vegetables﻿—beef and mutton, both boiled and broiled, potatoes, beans and corn, and there were bowls of figs and dried grapes and dried plums. There were also venison and bear meat and fish. There was a great deal of talk and a great deal of laughter, loud and boisterous, for the evening meal in the home camp is always a gala event. We ride hard and we ride often and we ride long. Often we are fighting and much of the time away from home. Then we have little to eat and nothing to drink but water, which is often warm and unclean and always scarce in our country.
We sit upon a long bench that encircles the outer periphery of the table and as I took my seat, the slaves, bearing platters of meat, passed along the inner rim of the table, and as they came opposite each warrior he rose, and leaning far across the board, seized a portion of meat with a thumb and finger and cut it deftly away with his sharp knife. The slaves moved in slow procession without pause and there was a constant gleam and flash of blades and movement and change of color as the painted warriors rose and leaned across the table, the firelight playing upon their beads and metal ornaments and the gay feathers of their headdresses. And the noise!
Pacing to and fro behind the warriors were two or three score shaggy hounds waiting for the scraps that would presently be tossed them﻿—large, savage beasts bred to protect our flocks from coyote and wolf, hellhound and lion, and capable of doing it, too.
As the warriors fell to eating the din subsided, and at a word from me a youth at my elbow struck a deep note from a drum. Instantly there was silence.
“For a hundred years we have dwelt beneath the heat of this barren waste land while our foes occupied a flowering garden, their cheeks fanned by the cooling breezes of the sea.

===Chinese===

每个武士面前摆着一只盛啤酒的陶壶、一只盛酒的陶壶，另有奴隶专司添满——这可不是轻松的差事，因为我们个个食量豪迈、酒量惊人，不过我们不像卡尔卡人那样酗酒滋事。别的奴隶端上肉食和菜蔬——牛肉羊肉，煮的烤的都有，土豆、豆子和玉米，还有一碗碗无花果、葡萄干和李子干。此外还有鹿肉、熊肉和鱼。席间谈笑风生，喧腾热烈，因为家营的晚膳从来都是一场盛会。我们骑行艰苦，骑行频繁，路途漫长。我们常常在打仗，大部分时间不着家。那时我们吃得寡淡，除水之外别无饮品，而在我们的国土上，水常常是温的、脏的，而且总是匮乏的。
我们坐在环绕桌子外缘的一条长凳上。我在自己的位子上落座后，端着大盘肉食的奴隶沿着桌子内缘依次走过，每到一个武士面前，那人便起身，远远探过桌面，用拇指和食指拈住一块肉，手起刀落干净利落地割下。奴隶们的行列缓缓不停，刀光频频闪动，涂彩的武士们起身探臂，火光在他们串珠、铜饰和头插的鲜亮羽毛上跳动，人影攒动，色彩变换。还有那喧声！
三四十条蓬毛猎犬在武士们身后来回逡巡，等着待会儿抛给它们的残肉——那是高大凶猛的畜生，养来保护羊群免遭郊狼、灰狼、魔犬和山狮的侵害，而且真顶得住。
武士们开始大嚼，喧声渐息。我一示意，我肘边一个青年在鼓上击出一记沉沉的鼓音。霎时一片肃静。
“一百年来，我们蛰居在这片荒瘠热土之下，任仇敌占据着繁花似锦的园囿，任海上的凉风拂着他们的面颊。

===Original===

They live in plenty; their women eat of luscious fruits, fresh from the trees, while ours must be satisfied with the dried and wrinkled semblance of the real; ten slaves they have to do their labor for every one that we possess; their flocks and herds find lush pasture and sparkling water beside their masters’ tents, while ours pick a scant existence across forty thousand square miles of sandy, rockbound desert; but these things gall the soul of The Red Hawk least of all. The wine turns bitter in my mouth when in my mind’s eye I look out across the rich valleys of the Kalkars and I recall that here alone in all the world that we know there flies not The Flag.”
A great growl rose from the fierce throats. “Since my youth I have held one thought sacred in my breast against the day that the blanket of The Great Chief should fall upon my shoulders. That day has come and I but await the time that the rains shall be safely over before making of that thought a deed. Twenty times in a hundred years have the Julian warriors ridden down into the Kalkar country in force, but their women and their children and their flocks remained behind in the desert﻿—an unescapable argument for their return.
“It shall not be so again. In April the tribe of Julian leaves the desert forever. With our tents and our women and all our flocks and herds we shall descend and live among the orange groves. This time there shall be no turning back. I, The Red Hawk, have spoken.”

===Chinese===

他们活在丰足里；他们的女人吃的是枝头摘下的鲜美果子，我们的女人却只能满足于干瘪皱缩的赝品；他们役使的奴隶，十倍于我们；他们的牛羊畜群在主人帐篷旁觅得丰草甘泉，我们的牲口却在四万平方英里多沙多石的荒漠上勉强糊口；然而这一切最刺不痛红鹰的心。当我在心目中眺望卡尔卡人的富饶谷地时，最让我口中酒变苦的是：在我们所知的整个世界上，唯独那里不飘扬着旗帜。”
凶悍的喉咙里爆出一片低沉的怒吼。“自少年时起，我胸中便供奉着一个念头，等着大首领的毯子落到我肩头的这一天。这一天到了，我只等雨季平安结束，便把那个念头变成行动。一百年里，朱利安的武士们二十次成群结队杀进卡尔卡人的国土，可他们的女人、孩子和畜群都留在了荒漠里——那是逼他们回头的铁证。
“这一次绝不再如此。四月，朱利安部族永远告别荒漠。我们带上帐篷、女人和全部畜群牛羊，下山去住进橙林之中。这一次决不回头。我，红鹰，话已说完。”

## II Exodus / 二 大迁徙
===Original===

April arrived and with it the clans, coming at my bidding. Soon there would be little danger of heavy rains in the coast valleys. To have been caught there in a week of rain with an army would have been fatal, for the mud is deep and sticky and our horses would have mired and the Kalkars fallen upon us and destroyed us. They greatly outnumber us, and so our only hope must lie in our mobility. We realize that we are reducing this by taking along our women and our flocks, but we believe that so desperate will be our straits that we must conquer, since the only alternative to victory must be death﻿—death for us and worse for our women and children.
The clans have been gathering for two days and all are here﻿—some fifty thousand souls, and of horses, cattle and sheep there must be a thousand thousand, for we are rich in livestock. In the past two months, at my orders, all our swine have been slaughtered and smoked, for we could not be hampered by them on the long desert march, even if they could have survived it.
There is water in the desert this time of year and some feed, but it will be a hard, a terrible march. We shall lose a great deal of our stock, one in ten, perhaps; The Wolf thinks it may be as high as five in ten. We shall start tomorrow an hour before sunset, making a short march of about ten miles to a place where there is a spring along the trail the ancients used. It is strange to see all across the desert evidence of the great work they accomplished. After five hundred years the location of their well graded trail, with its wide, sweeping curves, is plainly discernible. It is a narrow trail, but there are signs of another, much wider, that we discover occasionally. It follows the general line of the other, crossing it and recrossing it, without any apparent reason, time and time again. It is almost obliterated by drifting sand, or washed away by the rains of ages. Only where it is of a material like stone has it endured. The pains those ancients took with things! The time and men and effort they expended! And for what? They have disappeared and their works with them.
As we rode that first night Rain Cloud rode often at my side and, as usual, he was gazing at the stars.
“There is so much that we do not know,” sighed Rain Cloud; “yet all that we can spare the time for is thoughts of fighting.

===Chinese===

四月到了，各氏族也应我的召集而来。再过不久，海岸谷地就不大有暴雨的危险了。若带着大军在雨季里被围在那里，那是致命的：泥泞又深又粘，马匹会陷进去，卡尔卡人就会扑上来把我们消灭。他们人数远胜于我们，因此我们唯一的指望便是机动。我们也明白，带上女人和畜群会削弱这份机动；但我们相信，处境愈是绝地，愈非打胜不可——因为胜利之外唯有死路，我们死了，女人孩子还要死得更惨。
氏族已集结了两天，全数到齐——约五万人口，马、牛、羊加起来怕不下一百万头，我们的牲畜是极丰足的。过去两个月里，我下令把猪全部宰了熏制，因为在漫长的荒漠行军中它们只会拖累我们，何况它们也未必走得过去。
这个时节荒漠里有水，也有些草料，但这仍将是一场艰苦可怕的长征。牲畜我们要损失许多，十成里去一成或许；狼估计可能高达十成里去五成。明天日落前一小时出发，先走一段短程，约十英里，到古道沿途一处有泉水的地方。放眼荒漠，处处可见古人伟业的遗迹，实在令人称奇。五百年过去了，他们那条平整的大道——带着宽阔舒展的弯道——走向仍清晰可辨。那是一条窄路，但间或还能发现另一条宽得多的路的痕迹，它大致顺着前一条的走向，却一次又一次毫无来由地与之交叉又交叉，几乎已被流沙掩埋，或被经年的雨水冲毁，只有铺了石头般材料的地段才保存下来。古人对事物的这番用心！耗去的时间、人力和心血！为了什么呢？他们连人带功业都消失了。
第一夜行军时，雨云常常骑在我身旁，照旧凝望着星空。
“我们不知道的东西太多了，”雨云叹道，“可我们腾得出工夫去想的，只有打仗。

===Original===

I shall be glad when we have chased the last of the Kalkars into the sea, so that some of us may sit down in peace and think.”
“It is handed down to us that the ancients prided themselves upon their knowledge, but what did it profit them? I think we are happier. They must have had to work all their lives to do the things they did and to know all the things they knew, yet they could eat no more, or sleep no more, or drink no more in a lifetime than can we. And now they are gone forever from the Earth and all their works with them and all their knowledge is lost.”
“And presently we will be gone,” said Rain Cloud.
“And we will have left as much as they to benefit those who follow,” I replied.
“Perhaps you are right, Red Hawk,” said Rain Cloud; “yet I cannot help wanting to know more than I do know.”
The second march was also made at night and was a little longer than the first. We had a good moon and the desert night was bright. The third march was about twenty-five miles and the fourth a short one, only ten miles; and there we left the trail of the ancients and continued in a southwesterly direction to a trail that followed a series of springs that gave us short marches the balance of the way to a lake called Bear by our slaves. The way, of course, was all well known to us, and so we knew just what was ahead and dreaded the fifth march, which was a terrible one, by far the worst of them all. It lay across a rough and broken area of desert and crossed a range of barren mountains﻿—for forty-five miles it wound its parched way from water hole to water hole. For horsemen alone it would have been but a hard march, but with cattle and sheep to herd across that waterless waste, it became a terrific undertaking. Every beast that was strong enough carried hay, oats or barley in sacks, for we could not depend entirely upon the sparse feed of the desert for so huge a caravan; but water we could not carry in sufficient quantities for the stock, transporting enough, however, on the longer marches to insure a supply for the women and all children under sixteen, and on the short marches enough for nursing mothers and children under ten.
We rested all day before the fifth march began, setting forth about three hours before sundown. From fifty camps in fifty parallel lines we started. Every man, woman and child was mounted.

===Chinese===

等我们把最后一批卡尔卡人赶进大海，我才会高兴——那样我们中才有人能安坐下来想一想。”
“相传古人以他们的知识自傲，可那对他们有什么用处？我看我们比他们快活。他们准是一辈子苦干，才做成那些事、懂得那些学问，可一辈子吃的、睡的、喝的，也不比我们多一口。如今他们连同他们的功业从地球上永远消失了，他们的学问也全失传了。”
“不久我们也要消失的。”雨云说。
“而我们留给后人的，会同他们留下的一样多。”我答道。
“也许你说得对，红鹰，”雨云说，“可我总是忍不住想多知道一些。”
第二段行程也在夜间走，比第一段略长。月亮很好，荒漠之夜亮堂堂的。第三程约二十五英里，第四程是短程，只有十英里；在那里我们离开古道，折向西南，沿一条串起一路泉水的小道走，余下的路程都有短歇，直到我们的奴隶称作熊湖的湖边。这条路我们当然烂熟，前头有什么都清楚，最怕的是第五程——那是最苦的一程，远远超过所有各程。它横穿一片崎岖破碎的荒漠，还要翻一道童山濯濯的山脉——四十五英里的焦渴路途，从一个水坑蜿蜒到另一个水坑。单是骑兵走，也不过是一场苦行军；可要赶着牛羊穿过这片无水的荒地，就成了天大的难事。凡是有力气的牲口都驮着成袋的干草、燕麦或大麦，这样庞大的队伍，光靠荒漠里稀疏的草料是靠不住的；但牲口的饮水无法足量携带，只能在较长的行段里为女人和所有十六岁以下的儿童运足用水，短程行军时则只为哺乳的母亲和十岁以下的儿童备水。
第五程出发前，我们歇了一整天，约在日落前三小时动身。五十座营盘排成五十条平行的纵列同时开拔。男女老幼人人有马。

===Original===

The women carried all children under five, usually seated astride a blanket on the horse’s rump behind the mother. The rest rode alone. The bulk of the warriors and all the women and children set out ahead of the herds, which followed slowly behind, each bunch securely hemmed in by outriders and followed by a rearguard of warriors.
A hundred men on swift horses rode at the head of the column, and as the night wore on, gradually increased their lead until they were out of sight of the remainder of the caravan. Their duty was to reach the camp site ahead of the others and fill the water tanks that slaves had been preparing for the past two months. We took but few slaves with us, only a few personal attendants for the women and such others as did not wish to be separated from their masters and had chosen to accompany us. For the most part the slaves preferred to remain in their own country and we were willing to let them, since it made fewer mouths to feed upon the long journey and we knew that in the Kalkar country we should find plenty to take their places, as we would take those from the Kalkars we defeated.
The long, tiresome march was over at last. The years of thought that I had given it, the two months of preparation that had immediately preceded it, the splendid condition of all our stock, the training and the temper of my people bore profitable fruit and we came through without the loss of a man, woman or child, and with the loss of less than two in a hundred of our herds and flocks. The mountain crossing on that memorable fifth march took the heaviest toll, fully ten thousand head, mostly lambs and calves, falling by the trail side.
With two days out for rest, we came, at the end of the tenth march and the twelfth day, to the lake called Bear and into a rich mountain country, lush with feed and game. Here deer and wild goats and wild sheep abounded, with rabbit and quail and wild chicken and the beautiful wild cattle that the legends of our slaves say are descended from the domestic stock of the ancients.
It was not my plan to rest here longer than was necessary to fully restore the strength and spirits of the stock. Our horses were not jaded as we had had sufficient to change often. In fact we warriors had not ridden our warhorses once upon the journey. Red Lightning had trotted into the last camp fat and sleek.

===Chinese===

五岁以下的幼儿都由女人带着，通常坐在母亲身后、鞍后毯子上，分腿跨骑。其余的孩子独自骑马。大部分武士和全部妇孺先于畜群出发，畜群缓缓跟在后面，每一群都有侧卫牢牢圈拢，后面还有一支武士殿后。
一百名骑快马的汉子走在队伍最前面，随着夜深，渐渐拉开距离，直到大队人马望不见他们。他们的任务是抢先赶到营地，把奴隶们两个月来备下的水槽灌满。我们带的奴隶很少，只有伺候女人的几名贴身仆役，另有几个不愿与主人分离、自愿随行的。大多数奴隶宁可留在自己的家乡，我们也乐得由他们去——长途跋涉少了许多张嘴，而且我们知道，到了卡尔卡人的地界，要多少有多少，就像我们会从被打败的卡尔卡人手里夺来补上一样。
漫长而疲累的行军终于结束了。我多年的筹划，行前两个月的准备，全部牲畜的极佳状态，我族人的训练与士气，都结出了硕果：一路走来，男女老幼无一损失，畜群损失也不足百分之二。翻山那难忘的第五程代价最重，足足折了一万头，多半是羊羔和牛犊，倒毙在路旁。
休整两天后，走完第十程、第十二天，我们到了熊湖，进入一片丰饶的山地，水草茂盛，猎物众多。这里鹿、野山羊、野绵羊成群，还有兔子、鹌鹑、野鸡，以及美丽的野牛——按我们奴隶的传说，它们是古人家牛的后裔。
我不打算在此久留，只等牲畜的体力气焰完全恢复便走。我们的马并不疲困，因为带的马多，可以常常轮换。实际上，一路上我们武士连战马都没骑过一回。红闪电一路小跑进最后一处营地时，还是膘肥毛亮。

===Original===

To have remained here long would have been to have apprised the enemy of our plans, for the Kalkars and their slaves hunt in these mountains which adjoin their land, and should a single hunter see this vast concourse of Julians, our coming would have been known throughout the valleys in a single day and our purpose guessed by all.
So, after a day of rest, I sent The Wolf and a thousand warriors westward to the main pass of the ancients with orders to make it appear that we were attempting to enter the valley there in force. For three days he would persist in this false advance and in that time I felt that I should have drawn all the Kalkar fighting men from the valley lying southwest of the lake of the Bear. My lookouts were posted upon every eminence that gave view of the valleys and the trails between the main pass of the ancients and that through which we should pour down from the Bear out into the fields and groves of the Kalkars.
The third day was spent in preparation. The last of the arrows were finished and distributed. We looked to our saddle leathers and our bridles. We sharpened our swords and knives once more and put keener points upon our lances. Our women mixed the war paint and packed our belongings again for another march. The herds were gathered in and held in close, compact bunches. Riders reported to me at intervals from the various lookouts and from down the trail to the edge of the Kalkar farms. No enemy had seen us, but that they had seen The Wolf and his warriors we had the most reassuring evidence in the reports from our outposts that every trail from south and west was streaming with Kalkar warriors converging upon the pass of the ancients.1
During the third day we moved leisurely down the mountain trails and as night fell our vanguard of a thousand warriors debouched into the groves of the Kalkars. Leaving four thousand warriors, mostly youths, to guard the women, the children, the flocks and the herds I set out rapidly in a northwesterly direction toward the pass of the ancients at the head of full twenty thousand warriors.
Our warhorses we had led all day as we came slowly out of the mountains riding other animals, and not until we were ready to start upon the twenty-five mile march to the pass of the ancients did we saddle and mount the fleet beasts upon which the fate of the Julians might rest this night.

===Chinese===

在这里久留，就会让敌人察觉我们的计划，因为卡尔卡人和他们的奴隶常在这些毗邻他们土地的山里打猎，只要有一个猎人看见这支庞大的朱利安人马，不出一天，各处谷地就会尽知我们的到来，我们的意图也会被人猜个正着。
于是，歇了一天之后，我派狼带一千武士往西去古人的主山口，命他们做出我们要以全力从那里突入谷地的样子。他要在这场佯攻上坚持三天，三天之内，我料想熊湖西南谷地里的卡尔卡战斗人员就会被尽数吸引过来。我派了瞭望哨守住每一处能望见各谷地和道路的高地——从古人主山口到我们将从熊湖倾泻而下、冲进卡尔卡人田庄园林的那条通道，都在监视之中。
第三天用于备战。最后一批箭矢制成并分发下去。我们检查了鞍革和笼头，再度磨利刀剑，给长矛配上更锋利的矛尖。女人们调制战纹颜料，把行装重新捆好待发。畜群收拢进来，密集成一群群。各处瞭望哨和通往卡尔卡人农庄边缘的路上，骑手不断来向我回报。没有敌人看见我们；至于狼和他的人马已被敌人发现，我们从哨所的回报里得到了最令人宽心的证据：从南面和西面来的每一条路上，都涌满了向古人山口集结的卡尔卡武士。1
第三天白天，我们从容地沿山路而下，入夜时，我们一千人的前锋已涌进卡尔卡人的园林。留下四千武士——多为少年——守护妇孺和畜群之后，我亲率足足两万武士，迅速朝西北方向，向古人山口进发。
白天出山时，我们一路骑的是别的马，战马全由人牵着；直到要动身走那二十五英里奔袭古人山口的路时，我们才给这些神骏备鞍上马——今夜，朱利安一族的命运也许就系在它们身上。

===Original===

In consequence our horses were fresh from a two weeks’ rest. Three hours of comparatively easy riding should see us upon the flanks of the enemy.
The Rock, a brave and seasoned warrior, I had left behind to guard the women, the children and the stock. The Rattlesnake, with five thousand warriors, bore along a more westerly trail, after fifteen miles had been covered, that he might fall upon the rear of the enemy from one point while I fell upon them from another, and at the same time place himself between their main body, lying at the foot of the pass, and the source of their supplies and reinforcements.
With The Wolf, the mountains and the desert upon one side and The Rattlesnake and I blocking them upon the south and the southeast, the position of the Kalkars appeared to me to be hopeless.
Toward midnight I called a halt to await the report of scouts who had preceded us and it was not long before they commenced to come in. From them I learned that the camp fires of the Kalkars were visible from an eminence less than a mile ahead. I gave the signal to advance. Slowly the great mass of warriors moved forward. The trail dipped down into a little valley and then wound upward to the crest of a low ridge, where a few minutes later I brought Red Lightning to a halt. Before me spread a broad valley bathed in the soft light of moon and stars. Dark masses in the nearer foreground I recognized as orange groves even without the added evidence of the sweet aroma of their blossoms that was heavy on the still night air. Beyond, to the northwest, a great area was dotted with the glowing embers of a thousand dying camp fires. I filled my lungs with the cool, sweet air; I felt my nerves tingle; a wave of exaltation surged through me; Red Lightning trembled beneath me. After nearly four hundred years a Julian stood at last, upon the threshold of complete revenge!

===Chinese===

因此我们的马经两周休整，精力充沛。三个小时比较轻松的骑行，就该让我们压上敌人的侧翼。
我留下岩石守卫妇孺和牲畜，他是一名勇敢而老练的武士。响尾蛇带五千武士，在走出十五英里后沿一条更偏西的小路前进，以便从一处袭击敌人后方，我则从另一处压上，同时他还得卡在扎于山口脚下的敌军主力与其粮草援兵的来路之间。
一边是狼、大山和荒漠，一边是响尾蛇和我在南面、东南面拦住去路，卡尔卡人的处境在我看来已绝无生机。
将近午夜，我下令暂停，等候先遣斥候的回报，不久他们便陆续回来了。从中得知，前方不足一英里的一处高地上已能望见卡尔卡人的营火。我发出前进的信号。黑压压的武士大队缓缓向前。小路俯冲进一条小谷，又蜿蜒而上，登上一道低矮的山脊，几分钟后，我在脊顶勒住了红闪电。眼前铺展开一片宽阔的谷地，浸在月与星柔和的光里。近处前景中黑压压的一团团，不用那浓重地浮在静止夜气里的橙花甜香佐证，我也认得出是橙树林。远处西北方向，一大片地上点缀着上千堆行将熄灭的营火的余烬。我满吸了一口清凉甜润的空气；只觉浑身的神经都在震颤；一阵激昂涌遍全身；红闪电在我身下微微发抖。隔了将近四百年，一个朱利安终于站在了彻底复仇的门槛上！

## III Armageddon / 三 哈米吉多顿
===Original===

Very quietly we crept down among the orange groves, nearer, ever nearer to the sleeping foe. Somewhere to the west of us, beneath the silvery moon, The Rattlesnake was creeping stealthily forward to strike. Presently the stillness of the night would be broken by the booming of his war drums and the hoarse war-cries of his savage horde. It would be the signal that would send The Wolf down from the mountain heights above them and The Red Hawk from the orange groves below them to sink fang and talon into the flesh of the hated Kalkars, and ever The Rattlesnake would be striking at their heels.
Silently we awaited the signal from The Rattlesnake. A thousand bowmen unslung their bows and loosened arrows in their quivers; swords were readjusted, their hilts ready to the hand; men spat upon their right palms that their lance grip might be the surer. The night dragged on toward dawn. The success of my plan depended upon a surprise attack while the foe slept. I knew that The Rattlesnake would not fail me, but something must have delayed him. I gave the signal to advance silently. Like shadows we moved through the orange groves and deployed along a front two miles in length, a thousand bowmen in the lead and behind these, line after line of lancers and swordsmen. Slowly we moved forward toward the sleeping camp. How like the lazy, stupid Kalkars that no sentries were posted at their rear. Doubtless there were plenty of them on the front exposed to The Wolf. Where they could see an enemy they could prepare for him, but they have not imagination enough to foresee aught. Only the desert and their great numbers have saved them from extermination during the past hundred years.
Scarce a mile away now we could catch occasional glimpses of the dying embers of the nearest fires, and then from the east there rolled across the valley the muffled booming of distant war drums. A momentary silence followed and then faintly, there broke upon our ears the war-cries of our people. At my signal our own drums shattered the silence that had surrounded us. It was the signal for the charge.

===Chinese===

我们悄无声息地潜下橙林之间，越来越近，逼近沉睡的敌人。在我们西面某处，银月之下，响尾蛇正蹑足潜行，准备出击。不多时，夜的寂静就会被他的战鼓轰鸣和他那凶蛮部众嘶哑的战吼打破。那将是信号——狼从他们头顶的山巅压下，红鹰从他们脚下的橙林杀出，把獠牙利爪深深刺入可憎的卡尔卡人的血肉，而响尾蛇将穷追他们的脚踵，永不停歇。
我们静候响尾蛇的信号。一千名弓手摘下弓，松了箭壶里的箭；剑被重新调好位置，剑柄随手可及；男人们往右手掌心啐一口唾沫，好把矛握得更牢。夜拖沓着向天明爬去。我的计划全靠趁敌熟睡时突袭成功。我知道响尾蛇不会误我的事，但一定有什么耽搁了他。我发出无声前进的信号。我们像影子一样穿过橙林，沿着两英里宽的正面展开，一千弓手在前，其后是一列又一列的长矛手和剑手。我们缓缓向沉睡的营地推进。这些懒惰愚蠢的卡尔卡人，背后竟不设一个哨兵。无疑，面向狼的那一面哨兵多得很。看得见敌人的地方他们就会防备，可他们没有足够的想象力预见任何事。百年来，只靠荒漠和他们庞大的人口，他们才免于被斩尽杀绝。
此刻离敌营已不足一里，我们能偶尔瞥见最近的篝火将熄的余烬。就在这时，东面传来沉闷的战鼓声，滚过山谷。片刻的静默之后，我们族人的战吼隐隐传入耳中。我一示意，我们自己的战鼓骤然撕裂了笼罩四野的寂静。那是冲锋的号令。

===Original===

From twenty thousand savage throats rose the awful cries of battle, twenty thousand pairs of reins were loosed and eighty thousand iron-shod hoofs set the earth atremble as they thundered down upon the startled enemy, and from the heights above came the growl of the drums of The Wolf and the eerie howls of his painted horde.
It was dawn as we smote the camp. Our bowmen, guiding their mounts with their knees and the swing of their bodies, raced among the bewildered Kalkars, loosing their barbed shafts into the cursing, shrieking mob that fled before them only to be ridden down and trampled by their horses’ feet.
Behind the bowmen came the lancers and the swordsmen thrusting and cutting at those who survived. From our left came the tumult of The Rattlesnake’s assault and from far ahead and above us the sounds of battle proclaimed that The Wolf had fallen on the foe.
Ahead I could see the tents of the Kalkar leaders and toward these I spurred Red Lightning. Here would be the representatives of the house of Or-tis and here would the battle center. Ahead the Kalkars were forming in some semblance of order to check and repel us. They are huge men and ferocious fighters, but I could see that our surprise attack had unnerved them. They gave before us, before their chiefs could organize them for resistance, yet again and again they reformed and faced us. We were going more slowly now, the battle had become largely a matter of hand-to-hand combats; they were checking us, but they were not stopping us. So great were their numbers that even had they been unarmed it would have been difficult to force our horses through their massed ranks. Back of their front line they were saddling and mounting their horses, which those who had borne the brunt of our first onslaught had been unable to do. We had cut the lines to which their animals had been tethered and driven them, terrified, ahead of us to add to the confusion of the enemy. Riderless horses were everywhere, those of the Kalkars and many of our own, whose riders had fallen in battle. The tumult was appalling, for to the shrieks of the wounded and the groans of the dying were added the screams of stricken horses and the wild, hoarse war-cries of battle-maddened men, and underlying all, the dull booming of the war drums. Above us waved The Flag, and here were the drums and a massed guard of picked men.

===Chinese===

两万条凶蛮的喉咙迸出骇人的战吼，两万对缰绳被撒开，八万只钉铁的马蹄擂得大地震颤，向惊愕的敌人雷霆般碾去；头顶高地上传来狼的战鼓的轰响，和他那涂彩部众凄厉的嚎叫。
我们捣入敌营时天刚破晓。我们的弓手用膝盖和身躯的摆动控马，在茫然失措的卡尔卡人中穿梭驰突，把带倒钩的箭射向那咒骂着、尖啸着四散奔逃的人群——他们逃到哪儿，就被马蹄赶到哪儿、踏进泥里。
弓手之后是长矛手和剑手，对幸存者又是猛刺又是劈砍。左面传来响尾蛇攻杀的喧嚣，前方远处和我们上方，战斗的声浪宣告狼已扑向敌人。
前面我能望见卡尔卡首领们的营帐，我策动红闪电直奔那里。奥提斯家族的代表必在那里，战斗的中心也必在那里。前方，卡尔卡人正在勉强集结成某种阵形，想要挡住并击退我们。他们个个身材魁梧，是凶猛的斗士，但我看得出突袭已让他们胆寒。不等他们的首领把队伍组织起来抵抗，他们就在我们面前退却了，然而他们一次又一次重整旗鼓，回身再战。此刻我们推进得慢了，战斗大体变成了白刃格斗；他们在迟滞我们，却挡不住我们。他们人数太多，纵然手无寸铁，要让我们的战马从他们密集的队列中冲过去也难。在前线之后，他们正在备马、上马——那些承受了我们第一波猛击的人一直无暇上马。我们砍断了拴马的绳索，把他们的马吓得惊逃，驱赶到我们阵前，加剧敌人的混乱。无主的马四处奔窜，有卡尔卡人的，也有许多我们自己的——它们的主人已战死沙场。喧嚣骇人听闻：伤者的尖号、垂死者的呻吟，混着中箭战马的悲鸣和杀红了眼的男人们狂哑的战吼，而压在这一切底下的，是战鼓沉闷的轰鸣。我们的旗帜在头顶飘扬，旗下是战鼓和一支精锐卫队。
旗帜和战鼓随我们一同推进。我身旁是我家族的族旗，旗下画着红鹰，族旗的战鼓也随之敲响。这一天，战场上共有一百面团旗，每一团的战鼓都在不停地擂响着向敌人挑战。

===Original===

The Flag and the drums moved forward as we moved. And near me was the clan flag of my family with the red hawk upon it and with it were its drums. In all there were a hundred clan flags upon that field this day, and the drums of each rolled out, incessantly, defiance of the enemy.
Their horsemen now were rallied, and the dismounted men were falling back behind them and presently a Kalkar chief upon a large horse confronted me. Already was my blade red with their blood. I had thrown away my lance long since, for we were fighting in too close quarters for its effective use, but the Kalkar had his spear, and there was a little open space between us. In the instant he crouched and put spurs to his horse and bore down upon me.
He was a large man as most Kalkars are, for they have bred with that alone in mind for five hundred years, so that many of them are seven feet in height and over. He looked very fierce, did this fellow, with his great bulk and his little, bloodshot eyes. He wore a war bonnet of iron to protect his head from sword cuts and a vest of iron covered his chest against the thrusts of sword or lance, or the barbed tips of arrows. We Julians, or Americans, disdain such protection, choosing to depend upon our skill and agility, not hampering ourselves and our horses with the weight of all this metal.
My light shield was on my left forearm and in my right hand I grasped my two-edged sword. A pressure of my knees, an inclination of my body, a word in his pointed ear were all that was needed to make Red Lightning respond to my every wish, even though the reins hung loose upon his withers.
The fellow bore down upon me with a loud yell and Red Lightning leaped to meet him. The Kalkar’s point was set straight at my chest and I had only a sword on that side to deflect it, and at that I think I might have done so had I cared to try, even though the Kalkar carries a heavy lance and this one was backed by a heavy man and a heavy horse. With my left hand I grasped Red Lightning’s mane and at the instant that the Kalkar thought to see his point tear through my chest I swung from my saddle and lay flat against Red Lightning’s near side, while the Kalkar and his spear brushed harmlessly past an empty saddle. Empty for but an instant though.

===Chinese===

此刻他们的骑兵集结起来了，步战的散兵退到骑兵身后，不一会儿，一个骑高头大马的卡尔卡首领朝我逼来。我的剑刃早已染满他们的血。我的长矛早就扔了——眼下搏杀得过于贴身，长矛施展不开——可那卡尔卡人还端着他的矛，我们之间有一小片空地。刹那间他伏低身子，一夹马刺，向我猛冲过来。
他是个大块头，卡尔卡人多半如此——五百年来他们专以体格为选育之念，因此许多人身高七尺开外。这家伙块头大，一双布满血丝的小眼睛，模样凶极了。他戴铁盔护住脑袋防剑劈，胸口罩着铁甲背心，抵御剑刺、矛戳和带倒钩的箭镞。我们朱利安家的人，也就是美国人，鄙夷这等护具，宁可凭自己的武艺和身手，也不愿让这一身铁的重量拖累自己和战马。
我轻巧的盾牌套在左小臂上，右手握着双刃剑。膝盖一夹，身子一倾，在那尖耳朵边低喝一声——缰绳纵然松松垂在鬐甲上，红闪电也能随心而动，处处合我之意。
那家伙大吼一声朝我冲来，红闪电腾跃迎上。卡尔卡人的矛尖直指我的胸口，我这一侧只有一柄剑可以拨挡，而且我想，只要肯试，我原也拨得开——尽管卡尔卡人的矛又重，这一矛后面又压着沉重的人和沉重的马。我左手抓住红闪电的鬃毛，就在卡尔卡人以为矛尖将洞穿我胸口的一瞬，我已翻身荡离马鞍，紧贴在红闪电的内侧，那卡尔卡人和他的长矛擦着一个空荡荡的鞍子无害地掠了过去。不过只空了一瞬。

===Original===

Swinging back to my seat in the instant that I wheeled Red Lightning I was upon the Kalkar from the rear even as the fighting mass before him brought him to a halt. He was swinging to have at me again, but even as he faced me my sword swung down upon his iron bonnet, driving pieces of it through his skull and into his brain. A fellow on foot cut viciously at me at the instant I was recovering from the blow I had dealt the mounted Kalkar, so that I was able only to partially parry with my shield, with the result that his point opened up my right arm at the shoulder﻿—a flesh wound, but one that bled profusely, though it did not stay the force of my return, which drove through his collarbone and opened up his chest to his heart.
Once again I spurred in the direction of the tents of the Or-tis, above which floated the red banners of the Kalkars, around which were massed the flower of the Kalkar forces; too thickly massed, perhaps, for most effective defense, since we were driving them in from three sides and packing them there as tightly as eggs in the belly of a she-salmon. But now they surged forward and drove us back by weight of numbers, and now we threw ourselves upon them again until they, in their turn, were forced to give the ground that they had won. Sometimes the force of our attack drove them to one side while at another point their warriors were pushing out into the very body of the massed clans, so that here and there our turning movements would cut off a detachment of the enemy, or again a score or more of our own men would be swallowed by the milling Kalkar horde, until as the day wore on, the great field became a jumbled mass of broken detachments of Julian and Kalkar warriors surging back and forth over a bloody shambles, the iron shoes of their reeking mounts trampling the corpse of friend and foe alike into the gory mire.
Once, late in the afternoon, during a lull in the battle, I sat looking about the chaos of the field. Red with our own blood from a score of wounds and with the blood of friend and foe, Red Lightning and I stood panting in the midst of the welter. The tents of the Or-tis lay south of us﻿—we had fought halfway around them﻿—but they were scarce a hundred yards nearer for all those bitter hours of battle.

===Chinese===

我荡回鞍上的同时拨转红闪电，趁他前面厮杀的人团把他逼停的当口，从背后扑向那个卡尔卡人。他正回身要再战，可不等他转过来，我的剑已劈在他的铁盔上，铁片被砸得嵌进头骨、深入脑髓。我刚击毙这名骑兵，一个步战的卡尔卡人趁我旧力已尽，狠狠一剑刺来，我只来得及用盾偏挡了半下，他的剑尖在我右臂肩头豁开一道口子——皮肉伤，血流如注，却没能挡住我回刺的力道：这一剑穿透他的锁骨，把他的胸口直豁到心口。
我又一次朝奥提斯家族的营帐杀去。帐顶飘着卡尔卡人的红旗，帐周围挤满了卡尔卡大军的精锐；挤得未免太厚了，也许反而不利于防守，因为我们正从三面把他们往那里压，把他们塞得像雌鲑肚腹里的鱼子一样密不透风。可他们又汹涌反扑，仗着人多把我们压了回去，我们又扑上去，直到他们再把夺去的地盘吐出来。有时我们攻击的冲力把他们挤向一侧，另一处他们的武士却反冲进我方部族集结的纵深，于是我们的迂回时而截下敌人一支分队，时而我们自己的二三十个弟兄又被搅动的卡尔卡人海吞没。就这样，随着白昼推移，整个大战场变成了一团乱麻——朱利安与卡尔卡武士的残破分队，在血腥的屠场上来回冲荡，他们坐骑的铁蹄踏着尸骸，把敌友的尸首一并踩进血污的泥泞里。
傍晚时分，战斗一度稍歇，我坐在马上环顾这片混沌的战场。红闪电和我浑身是血——我带着二十来处创口，敌友之血涂满了我们两个——在尸山血海中喘息。奥提斯的营帐在我们南面——我们已围着它血战了半个圈——可经过那许多苦战时辰，离它也只近了不足百步。

===Original===

Some of the warriors of The Wolf were near me, showing how far that old, grey chief had fought his way since dawn, and presently behind a mask of blood I saw the flashing eyes of The Wolf himself, scarce twenty feet away.
“The Wolf!” I cried and he looked up and smiled in recognition.
“The Red Hawk is red indeed,” he bantered; “but his pinions are yet unclipped.”
“And the fangs of The Wolf are yet undrawn,” I replied.
A great Kalkar, blowing like a spent hound, was sitting his tired horse between us. At our words he raised his head. “You are The Red Hawk?” he asked.
“I am The Red Hawk,” I replied.
“I have been searching for you these two hours,” he said.
“I have not been far, Kalkar,” I told him; “what would you of The Red Hawk?”
“I bear word from Or-tis the Jemadar.”
“What word has an Or-tis for a Julian?” I demanded.
“The Jemadar would grant you peace,” he explained.
I laughed. “There is only one peace which we may share together,” I said, “and that is the peace of death﻿—that peace I will grant him, and he will come hither and meet me. There is nothing that an Or-tis has the power to *grant* a Julian.”
“He would stop the fighting while you and he discuss the terms of peace,” insisted the Kalkar. “He would stop this bloody strife that must eventually annihilate both Kalkar and Yank.” He used an ancient term which the Kalkars have applied to us for ages in a manner of contempt, but which we have been taught to consider as an appellation of honor, though its very meaning is unknown to us and its derivation lost in antiquity.
“Go back to your Jemadar,” I said, “and tell him that the world is not wide enough to support both Kalkar and Yank, Or-tis and Julian; that the Kalkars must slay us to the last man or be slain.”
He wheeled his horse toward the tent of the Or-tis and The Wolf bade his warriors let him pass. Soon he was swallowed by the close-packed ranks of his own people, and then a Kalkar struck at one of us from behind and the battle raged again.
How many men had fallen one might not even guess, but the corpses of warriors and horses lay so thick that the living mounts could but climb and stumble over them and sometimes barriers of them nearly man-high lay between me and the nearest foeman so that I was forced to jump Red Lightning over the gory obstacle to find new flesh for my blade.

===Chinese===

狼的一些武士就在我近旁——可见这位灰发老首领自破晓以来已从多远一路杀来。不一会儿，我在一层血污的遮掩后面，望见了不到二十英尺外狼本人那双闪亮的眼睛。
“狼！”我喊道。他抬头一望，认出是我，笑了。
“红鹰可真是红了，”他打趣道，“好在翅膀还没被剪。”
“狼的獠牙也还没被拔呢。”我回敬。
一个大块头卡尔卡人，喘得像条跑乏了的猎犬，骑着一匹疲惫的马停在我们两人之间。听到我们说话，他抬起头。“你是红鹰？”他问。
“我就是红鹰。”我答。
“我找你找了两个时辰了。”他说。
“我没走远，卡尔卡人，”我告诉他，“你找红鹰何事？”
“我带来杰玛达尔（Jemadar）奥提斯的话。”
“奥提斯家的人能对朱利安家的人说什么？”我质问。
“杰玛达尔愿意与你们讲和。”他解释道。
我大笑。“我们之间只有一种和平可享，”我说，“那就是死亡的和平——这个和平我可以赐给他，让他到这里来见我。奥提斯家的人没有能力赐给朱利安家任何东西。”
“他想让你和他商谈和约，先行停战。”那卡尔卡人坚持道，“他想止住这场血战——它迟早要把卡尔卡人和扬基佬同归于尽。”他用了一个古老的词，卡尔卡人世世代代轻蔑地这样称呼我们，我们却从小被教导把它当作尊荣的称号，尽管它的本义我们已无从知晓，词源也早已失传。
“回去告诉你的杰玛达尔，”我说，“这个世界容不下卡尔卡人和扬基佬、奥提斯家和朱利安家并存；卡尔卡人若不把我们杀到最后一人，就得被我们杀尽。”
他拨转马头朝奥提斯的营帐驰去，狼吩咐部下放他过去。很快他就被自己人拥挤的队列吞没了。随即一个卡尔卡人从背后向我们中的一人偷袭，战端又起。
死了多少人无从猜测，但武士和战马的尸首堆积得那样厚，活着的坐骑只能在尸堆上攀爬、绊跌，有时我离最近的敌人之间横着几具尸体垒成的近人高的壁垒，我不得不纵红闪电跃过那血淋淋的障碍，去为我的剑找新的血肉。

===Original===

And then, slowly, night descended until man could not tell foe from friend, but I called to my tribesmen about me to pass along the word that we would not move from our ground that night, staying on for the first streak of dawn that would permit us to tell a Kalkar from a Yank.
All through the night we heard a considerable movement of men and horses among the Kalkars, and we judged that they were reforming for the dawn’s attack, and then quite suddenly and without warning of any sort, we saw a black mass moving down upon us. It was the Kalkars﻿—the entire body of them﻿—and they rode straight for us, not swiftly, for the corpse-strewn, slippery ground prevented that; but steadily, overwhelmingly, like a great, slow-moving river of men and horses. They swept into us and over us, or they carried us along with them. Their first line broke upon us in a bloody wave and went down and those behind passed over the corpses of those that had fallen. We hacked until our tired arms could scarce raise a blade shoulder high. Kalkars went down screaming in agony, but they could not halt, they could not retreat, for the great, ever-moving mass behind them pushed them onward, nor could they turn to right or left because we hemmed them in on both flanks, nor could they flee ahead, for there, too, were we.
Borne on by this resistless tide I was carried with it. It surrounded me. It pinioned my arms at my sides. It crushed my legs. It even tore my sword from my hand. At times, when the force ahead stemmed it for a moment, and the force behind continued to push on, it rose in the center until horses were lifted from the ground, and then those behind sought to climb over the backs of those in front, until the latter were borne to earth and the others passed over their struggling forms, or the obstacle before gave way and the flood smoothed out and passed along again between the flashing banks of Julian blades, hewing, ever hewing, at the Kalkar stream. Never have I looked upon such a sight as the moon revealed that night﻿—never in the memory or the tradition of man has there been such a holocaust. Thousands upon thousands of Kalkars must have fallen upon the edge of that torrent as it swept its slow way between the blades of my painted warriors, who hacked at the living mass until their arms fell numb at their sides, and then gave way to the eager thousands pressing from behind.

===Chinese===

随后，夜色缓缓降临，人已分不清敌友。我传令身边的族人依次转告：今夜我们寸步不退，坚守到黎明的第一线光亮，好让我们能分清谁是卡尔卡人、谁是扬基佬。
整夜我们都听见卡尔卡人中大量人马在调动，我们判断他们是在重整队形，准备天明进攻。可就在毫无预兆之间，我们看见一大团黑色的物体向我们压来。是卡尔卡人——倾巢而出的全部——他们直冲我们而来，不快，因为遍地尸骸的滑腻地面容不得快，却沉稳、不可抗拒，像一条由人和马汇成的浩浩荡荡、缓缓推进的大河。他们冲进我们、漫过我们，或者裹挟着我们一同向前。他们的第一线像血红的浪头砸在我们身上，倒了下去，后面的人踏着倒下者的尸首继续前进。我们劈砍到疲惫的手臂几乎举不起刀剑。卡尔卡人惨嚎着倒下，可他们停不得，退不得——身后那永不停歇的人潮推着他们向前；也不能左右转——两翼都被我们兜死；更不能向前逃——前面也是我们。
我被这股不可抗拒的洪流裹挟着走。它围住我，把我的双臂死死挤在身侧，挤压着我的双腿，甚至从我手里夺走了剑。有时前方的人潮受阻片刻，后面的推力却不止，中段便隆起堆高，战马被掀离地面，后面的人争相从前面人的马背上爬过去，直到前者被压倒在地，后者踏着他们挣扎的躯体而过；或者前面的阻碍突然溃开，洪流重新摊平，在朱利安刀剑闪光的两岸之间奔涌而过——刀在砍，不停地砍，砍向卡尔卡人的浊流。我从未见过那晚月光下的景象——在人类的记忆和传说里，也从未有过这样的大屠宰。当成千上万的卡尔卡人被那股浊流裹着，从我涂彩的武士们刀锋之间缓缓碾过时，倒下的必定数以千计；我的武士们对着活的人海劈砍，直到双臂麻木垂落，再换上背后急不可耐涌上来的数千生力军。

===Original===

And ever onward I was borne, helpless to extricate myself from the sullen, irresistible flood that carried me southward down the broadening valley. The Kalkars about me did not seem to realize that I was an enemy, or notice me in any way, so intent were they upon escape. Presently we had passed the field of yesterday’s thickest fighting, the ground was no longer strewn with corpses and the speed of the rout increased, and as it did so the massed warriors spread to right and left sufficiently to permit more freedom of individual action, still not enough to permit me to worm my way from the current.
That I was attempting to do so, however, was what attracted attention to me at first and then the single red hawk feather and my other trappings, so different from those of the Kalkars.
“A Yank!” cried one near me and another drew his sword and struck at me, but I warded the blow with my shield as I drew my knife, a pitiful weapon wherewith to face a swordsman.
“Hold!” cried a voice of authority near by. “It is he whom they call The Red Hawk, their chief. Take him alive to the Jemadar.”
I tried to break through their lines, but they closed in upon me, and though I used my knife to good effect upon several of them they overbore me with their numbers, and then one of them must have struck me upon the head with the flat of his sword, for of a sudden everything went black, and of that moment I remember only reeling in my saddle.

===Chinese===

我就这样被一直裹挟着向前，无法脱出这股阴沉、不可抗拒的洪流，它载着我向南，滑向越来越宽的谷地。我周围的卡尔卡人似乎并没有意识到我是敌人，全然不注意我——他们一心只顾逃命。不多时，我们越过了昨日鏖战最烈的地段，地上不再遍地尸首，溃逃的速度加快了，于是那密集的人马向左右散开了一些，足以让人各自腾挪，却还不够我从人流中挤出去。
我正设法脱身，这首先引来了注意，接着是我那支独到的红鹰羽毛和我全然不同于卡尔卡人的装束。
“一个扬基佬！”我身旁一人喊道，另一人拔剑就砍，我举盾挡开，同时抽出了刀——对着剑客，这武器寒酸得可怜。
“住手！”近旁一个有身份的声音喝道，“这就是他们叫红鹰的，他们的首领。留活口，带给杰玛达尔。”
我试图冲破他们的包围，可他们收紧了圈子，我虽然用刀结果了好几个，终究敌不过他们的人多，随后不知哪一个必是用剑脊砸了一下我的头，霎时眼前一黑——那一刻我只记得自己在马鞍上晃了两晃。

## IV The Capital / 四 都城
===Original===

When I regained consciousness it was night again. I was lying upon the ground, out beneath the stars. For a moment I experienced a sense of utter comfort, but as my tired nerves awoke they spoke to me of pain and stiffness from many wounds, and my head throbbed with pain. I tried to raise a hand to it, and it was then that I discovered that my wrists were bound. I could feel the matted stiffness of my scalp, and I knew that it was caked with dried blood, doubtless from the blow that had stunned me.
In attempting to move, that I might ease my cramped muscles, I found that my ankles were fastened together as well as my wrists, but I managed to roll over, and raising my head a little from the ground I looked about and saw that I was surrounded by sleeping Kalkars and that we lay in a barren hollow ringed by hills. There were no fires, and from this fact and the barrenness and seclusion of the camp I guessed that we were snatching a brief rest in hiding from a pursuing foe.
I tried to sleep, but could do so only fitfully, and presently I heard men moving about and soon they approached and awakened the warriors sleeping near me. The thongs were removed from my ankles shortly thereafter, and Red Lightning was brought and I was helped into the saddle. Immediately after, we resumed the march. A glance at the stars showed me that we were moving west. Our way led through hills and was often rough, evidencing that we were following no beaten trail, but rather that the Kalkars were attempting to escape by a devious route.
I could only guess at the numbers of them, but it was evident that there was not the great horde that had set forth from the battlefield below the pass of the ancients. Whether they had separated into smaller bands, or the balance had been slain I could not even conjecture; but that their losses must have been tremendous I was sure. We travelled all that day, stopping only occasionally when there was water for the horses and the men. I was given neither food nor water, nor did I ask for either. I would die rather than ask a favor of an Or-tis. In fact, I did not speak all that day, nor did any of the Kalkars address me.
I had seen more Kalkars in the past two days than in all my life before and was now pretty familiar with the appearance of them. They range in height from six to eight feet, the majority of them being midway between these extremes.

===Chinese===

我恢复知觉时，又是夜里了。我躺在地上，头顶是星空。有那么一瞬，我竟觉得浑身舒坦，可疲乏的神经一醒过来，便向我说起多处伤口的疼痛和僵痛，头也一阵阵抽痛。我想抬手去摸，这才发现手腕被绑。我摸得到头皮上结成饼的僵硬，知道那是干了的血，多半是把我打晕的那一下留下的。
我想挪动一下身子，好松快松快僵麻的肌肉，又发现脚踝也和手腕一样捆着；但我还是翻了个身，把头稍稍抬离地面四下张望，只见我周围躺满了熟睡的卡尔卡人，我们宿在一圈小山环抱的荒芜洼地里。营中没有生火，从这一点，加上营地的荒僻隐蔽，我猜他们是在躲避追兵，抓紧喘口气。
我想睡，却只能断断续续地打盹，不久就听见有人走动，很快他们走过来，叫醒了我身边的武士。随后不久，我脚踝上的皮绳被解开了，红闪电被牵来，有人扶我上鞍。紧接着队伍继续赶路。看一眼星象便知我们在向西走。一路穿行丘岭，路况常常很坏，可见走的不是现成的大道，卡尔卡人是在抄小路逃命。
他们有多少人我只能猜，但看得出已不是从古人山口下战场开拔出来的那支大军。是分成了小股，还是余者都被杀了，我连猜也无从猜起；但损失必定极惨重，这一点我毫不怀疑。我们走了一整天，只在人和马有水喝的地方偶尔停一停。我没得到一口吃的、一口喝的，我也不曾开口讨。我宁可死，也不向一个奥提斯乞恩。事实上，那一整天我一言未发，也没有一个卡尔卡人同我说话。
过去两天里我见到的卡尔卡人，比此前一生见的还多，如今对他们的模样已相当熟悉。他们身高从六英尺到八英尺不等，多数居于两者之间。

===Original===

There is a great variety of physiognomy among them, for they are a half-caste race, being the result of hundreds of years of interbreeding between the original Moon Men and the women of the Earth whom they seized for slaves when they overran and conquered the world. Among them there is occasionally an individual who might pass anywhere for a Yank, in so far as external appearances are concerned; but the low, coarse, brutal features of the Kalkar preponderate.
They wear a white blouse and breeches of cotton woven by their slaves and long, woolen cloaks fabricated by the same busy hands. Their women help in this work as well as in the work of the fields, for the Kalkar women are no better than slaves, with the possible exception of those who belong to the families of the Jemadar and his nobles. Their cloaks are of red, with collars of various colors, or with borders or other designs to denote rank. Their weapons are similar to ours, but heavier. They are but indifferent horsemen. That, I think, is because they ride only from necessity and not, as we, from love of it.
That night, after dark, we came to a big Kalkar camp. It was one of the camps of the ancients, the first that I ever had seen. It must have covered a great area, and some of the huge stone tents were still standing. It was in these that the Kalkars lived or in dirt huts leaning against them. In some places I saw where the Kalkars had built smaller tents from the building materials salvaged from the ruins of the ancient camp, but as a rule they were satisfied with hovels of dirt, or the half-fallen and never-repaired structures of the ancients.
This camp lies about forty-five or fifty miles west of the battlefield, among beautiful hills and rich groves, upon the banks of what must once have been a mighty river, so deeply has it scoured its pathway into the earth in ages gone.2
I was hustled into a hut where a slave woman gave me food and water. There was a great deal of noise and excitement outside, and through the open doorway I could hear snatches of conversation as Kalkars passed to and fro. From what I heard, I gathered that the defeat of the Kalkars had been complete and that they were flying toward the coast and their principal camp, called The Capital, which the slave woman told me lay a few miles southwest.

===Chinese===

他们的相貌形形色色，因为他们是个混血的种族——是当初的月球人与地球上被他们掳为奴的女人几百代杂交的后裔，当年他们横扫并征服了世界。其中偶尔也能见到一个外表随处可以充作扬基人的；但卡尔卡人那种低贱、粗野、凶残的面相终究占了上风。
他们穿白色的上衣和裤子，是奴隶织的棉布；披长长的羊毛斗篷，也出自那些忙碌的手。他们的女人也做这些活计，还下地干活，因为卡尔卡女人比奴隶强不了多少——也许除了杰马达尔和他贵族人家的女眷。斗篷是红色的，领子颜色各异，或以镶边和别的花样标示等级。他们的武器同我们相仿，但更笨重。他们是平平的马背上的民族。我想那是因为他们骑马只出于需要，不像我们，是出于爱好。
那天入夜后，我们到了一处卡尔卡人大营。那是古人的一座营地，是我生平头一回见到。它占地必定极广，有些巨大的石头帐篷还立着。卡尔卡人就住在这些里面，或者住在倚着它们搭起的土棚里。有些地方，我看见卡尔卡人用古人营址废墟里拆出的材料盖了较小的帐篷，但一般说来，随便一个土窝，或者古人那些塌了一半、从不修缮的建筑，他们就满足了。
这座营地位于战场以西约四十五到五十英里处，在秀丽的丘岭和繁茂的园林之间，坐落一条往日必定十分浩荡的大河岸边——它的河床在远古便已深深地剖开大地。2
我被推进一间棚屋，一个女奴给了我吃的和喝的。外面人声嘈杂，一片慌乱，从敞开的门口，我能听见卡尔卡人来来往往时的话语片段。听那意思，我明白了卡尔卡人败得很彻底，正在朝海岸和他们的主营地逃——那地方叫都城，女奴告诉我就在西南几英里外。

===Original===

This, she said, was a wonderful camp, with tents reaching so high into the heavens that often the Moon brushed against their tops as she made her way through the sky.
They had released my hands, but my feet were still bound and two Kalkars squatted just outside the door of the hut to see that I did not escape. I asked the slave woman for some warm water to wash my wounds and she prepared it for me.
